Apps open with the row's `key_epoch`: `Account.openVaultKey(vaultId, wrapped, "account", row.key_epoch, pinsLegacyWrapAllowed(pins, vaultId, row.key_epoch))`; a v2 wrap opens only for its epoch, a v1 wrap only when `legacyAllowed`. **Tightening** (key pins): after a v2 wrap opened (`accountWrapIsBound(wrapped)`), apps observe the row with `bound_wrap: true` (`pinsObserve`/`pinsAccept`; `admit` says `pin` when the pin doesn't know that epoch yet), which records `bound_since` = the highest epoch this account opened from a v2 wrap in the vault's epoch pin (sealed with the pins, merged as the higher one; pins from before have none); `pinsLegacyWrapAllowed(pins, vaultId, keyEpoch)` then refuses a v1 wrap at that epoch or below, and allows it above (a vault re-keyed in v1 by an app while the hint was off still opens). An account wrap is only ever written at vault creation (epoch 0) and by the account's own key replacement (a new epoch), so a v1 wrap at or below `bound_since` can only be an old key relabelled. `bound_wrap` on a sealed row is malformed.
- A row without `key_epoch` is refused when signed sharing is on (`missing_epoch`).

**Vault admission** (reviews H2, L3; round 3): `pinsAdmit(pins, {vault_id, key_kind, key: epochPin|null, roster_version: <trusted roster version>|null, roster_hash: rosterHash(<trusted roster>)|null, chain_hashes?, signed_share, paused, invitation?})` → `{status: "ok", epoch: new|same|newer, trust: own|signed|unsigned, pin}` | `{status: "consent", rejoin, replaced, own, first_signed, held, declinable}` | `{status: "refused", reason: missing_epoch|paused|left|older|conflict|downgraded|roster_forked|own_vault|held}`, in this order: `roster_version` and `roster_hash` not both given or both null (round 5 H), or an `account` invitation → malformed; no key epoch → `missing_epoch`; a sealed row while paused → `paused`; a vault in `left` → `consent {rejoin: true}` only for a sealed row with a verified share whose trusted roster version is above the tombstone's (and above any version its pin knew) and whose key isn't older than the one at leaving (a new key, or the same key at the same epoch), else `left`; an invitation for a vault pinned `own` → `own_vault`; the epoch check (`older`, `conflict`, `downgraded` refuse; a roster below `roster_version_max` is `downgraded`); the same version as `roster_version_max` with another `roster_hash` → `roster_forked`; a sealed row with a verified share but no roster (stripped), or without a verified share where a roster is trusted or the pin says signed → `downgraded`; an `account` row → `ok/own`; an invitation for a pinned vault → `consent {held: true}` only for a signed `newer` key on a vault with signed history, else `held`; a sealed row → `ok` when pinned (`same`/`newer`), or `new` and in `grandfathered` with **the same key id** and **unsigned** (never for invitations), else **`consent`**; a pinned sealed row whose key is `newer` while the pin has **no signed history** (no `signed_since`, no `roster_version_max`) → `consent {replaced: true}`, **signed or not**: unsigned (review round 3 M2: never a silent server-chosen key) and also signed (round 4, "puppet signer": a server can make up an account with its own X25519 and Ed25519 keys, correctly cross-signed, serve the row at `key_epoch + 1` with a key it chose, sign the share as that account and serve a roster version 1 signed by it that lists it as owner or admin and the user with the row's role; on a vault never seen signed every signature, chain and role check passes, so only the user's confirmation stands between that and a silently used server key; the first signed key replacement of a vault from before therefore needs one confirmation, with the signer shown). `own: true` when the pin is marked `own`: the account held this vault with its own key wrap, and a personal vault must never silently turn into one whose key someone else sealed: "<vault> was your own vault. Your server now says someone else gave you a new key for it. Accept only if you shared this vault and expected a key change." A pinned sealed row whose key is the **same** while the pin has no signed history, now **signed** (a share and a roster) → `consent {first_signed: true}` (round 5 M; `own` as above): whoever holds the key — a member who left, an approved emergency contact — can sign a version-1 roster that commits to it, with a server-made signer, and before this its signer was pinned silently and every later key it signed was accepted; now the first signer of a vault from before is trusted only with the user's OK: "<vault> is signed for the first time, by <signer, pinned email first>. Compare their fingerprint before you accept." `first_signed` is also set on a signed `replaced`. (`pinsObserve` also marks pins from before: an `account` row whose pin lacks `own` gives `ok` with `pin: true`.) The `VaultObservation` fields `signed_share` and `paused` are required: an observation without them is malformed (round 4 I2); unknown fields are refused (round 5 Info: a misspelled `chain_hashes` must not be ignored; in WASM the object goes through a JSON value so every field is seen). **`invitation: true`** marks an invitation (`/sync` `invitations`, round 5, Android M): never grandfathered, never the account's own vault (`own_vault`: an owner's own row is never re-sealed to them), and an invitation whose vault id is in `epochs` is acceptable only as a signed `newer` key on a vault with signed history (`consent {held: true}`) — anything else there is `held`; a vault in `left` gives the re-join consent as for rows. Clients pass the same observation (with `invitation`) to `pinsAccept`, so the flags shown are the flags accepted. `declinable` (round 5 M2) = none of `rejoin`, `replaced`, `own`, `first_signed`, `held`, and the row is signed or an invitation: only then is Decline offered; otherwise "Not now" (keep it locked). **`chain_hashes`** (round 4, closes the puppet chain on vaults already seen signed): `{"<version>": rosterHash(<roster>)}` for the trusted roster the chain started from (if any) and every newer roster `verifyRosterChain` checked this time (at most 1001; versions as plain decimal numbers). Apps **send it whenever they fetched a chain** — at every sync where `rosterHead` said `fetch`, and for an invitation's chain from version 1 (also when re-joining). It is checked first, after `paused`, against the pin the account remembers (or the tombstone's pin): when the chain reaches that pin's `roster_version_max`, the roster at that version must have the pinned `roster_hash`, else `refused {roster_forked}` (`pinsAccept` throws `roster_forked`); a chain whose versions skip the pinned version, or that lists another hash for the observation's own `roster_version`, is malformed. So a device that remembers no roster for a vault (or an older one) can't be shown a chain re-signed from version 1 by an account the server made up once the account saw the vault signed at any version; a chain that stops before that version is `downgraded` as before. Without `chain_hashes` (no chain fetched: the device's own trusted roster) nothing more is checked.
- `ok.pin` (review round 3 H1) says the key pins learn something: a `new`/`newer` key, the first trusted roster for a vault pinned unsigned (re-signing keeps the key and epoch, so without this no device would ever record `signed_since`, and a device with no memory would later accept the roster stripped), a higher roster version than `roster_version_max`, or that roster's hash. **Whenever `pin` is true**: `pinsAccept(pins, observation)` and write the key pins (once per sync, batched). Simpler: `pinsObserve(pins, observation)` → `{admission, pins, changed}` does `pinsAdmit` and applies what `pin` says (with the roster hash); chain it over the sync's rows and write the pins once if any `changed`. `rosterHash(json)` = base64url SHA-256 of `["passauth/member-roster-hash/v1", signed bytes, signature (64)]`.
- *Consent*: a sealed vault the account never accepted (a planted one, or accepted on an app from before whose pins were lost) is never opened beyond its name; it is shown like an invitation ("Your server added <vault> to your account. Accept only if you expected it."), and accepting = `pinsAccept(pins, observation)` (+ remember the roster, pin the signer). **Unsigned consent rows never offer Decline** (cross-client rule, round 4): declining writes a tombstone, and a server could make a real vault look like this; they offer Accept (by the key id shown) only where it is allowed — while trust memory is missing (below) or for `replaced` — and otherwise stay held, locked, with "Ask the vault's owner to sign this vault." `replaced: true` (a vault already accepted, never seen signed, whose key changed): unsigned, "The key of <vault> was replaced by an app that doesn't sign yet. Accept only if someone in this vault just replaced its key."; signed, "The key of <vault> was replaced and signed by <signer, pinned email first> for the first time. Compare their fingerprint before you accept." (with `own` the warning above). Accept = `pinsAccept(pins, observation)`; "Not now" = keep it locked (no `pinsLeave`; the user may accept later). **Declines** (round 5 M2) are offered only where the verdict says `declinable` (never for `rejoin`, `replaced`, `own`, `first_signed` or `held`: a puppet-signed consent for a vault the account uses must not lead the user into giving it up) and write `pinsDecline(pins, vaultId, currentRow, now)` (UniFFI `pins_decline`), never `pinsLeave` with a version: it writes a tombstone at roster version 0 (raised to what a pin knew) **only for a vault the account doesn't hold** — unchanged when `epochs[vaultId]` exists, the vault is already in `left`, or `currentRow` (the vault id is one of this sync's vault rows, whatever its verdict). A server-chosen roster version never goes into a tombstone. The invitation is declined on the server after (or regardless of) the pins write, and only once the server answered (or 404) is the decline final in the UI.
- *Grandfathering* (round 3 M3): at the account's **first signed sync** (pins with `grandfathered` null) the app writes `pinsGrandfather(pins, [{vault_id, key_id: VaultKey.keyId(vault_id)}…] of the vault rows it has now, createdIdentity)` once (UniFFI `pins_grandfather(pins_json, vaults_json, created_identity)`); `grandfathered` is stored as `{vault id: key id}` and covers a row only with that key (round 5: a planted key under a grandfathered id needs consent). `createdIdentity` is true **only when this unlock created the account's signing identity** (`user.identity` was null and `PUT /account/identity` answered `created: true`); only then are the ids written. Grandfathering covers **unsigned** rows only (a signed row comes through an invitation or consent, `pinsAdmit` enforces it); those stay usable without consent as "Not yet signed". Every later vault needs an invitation or consent. Two client rules close the remaining gaps (extension re-review M1/M2, 2026-11-16): (1) the ids passed are only vault rows that were already in **this device's own offline copy from an earlier session** (a device with no earlier copy — a fresh install, a re-added account — passes none, so every unsigned shared vault there waits for consent, accepted by the key id shown, exactly as under *Trust memory missing*); a server can fake `user.identity: null` + `created: true` to a fresh device, but then it only gets a consent prompt, never a silently used vault. (2) `pinsGrandfather` is called only when the identity state is known (`created` or the listed identity opened); after a failed or unanswered `PUT /account/identity` (5xx, network, `identity_exists`, stale) the app skips it and tries again at the next sync, so a transient error never writes an empty list for the account. (3) An identity counts as created by this device also when the `PUT` answer was lost but the listed record later opens to the hash this device generated before the `PUT` (kept in session and device memory; a server can't forge a record that opens under the Account Key); on `identity_exists` grandfathering is skipped for that sync. (4) Sticky checks become sticky only after the server has shown it supports signing (config lists `signed_sharing`, an identity `PUT`/`GET` or `GET /account/key-pins` succeeded, or a share, roster or identity was seen): while the config is unknown the checks run but nothing that keeps them on for good (memory flags, local pins) is persisted, and a 404 on `/account/identity` means this server doesn't sign. One failed `/api/config` against an older server therefore never locks shared vaults for good.
- *Trust memory missing*: the account has a signing identity but `GET /account/key-pins` returns none (`key_pins: null`, `seq` 0) on a device with no memory of a higher `seq`: the server withheld them (to re-grandfather planted vaults and reset "seen signed"), or the device that created the identity never wrote them. Then `createdIdentity` is false, `pinsGrandfather` writes an empty list, and every shared (sealed) vault needs consent on this device; the app shows "PassAuth couldn't find your list of known keys on your server, so each shared vault needs your OK again. If you didn't expect this, tell your server's admin." and, in this state only, also lets the user accept unsigned vaults (labelled "Not yet signed"). A device that remembers a higher `seq` keeps using its own copy (rollback rule above).
- *Tombstones* (instead of forgetting an epoch pin): leaving, a removal the user saw and deleting a vault write `pinsLeave(pins, vaultId, <trusted roster version, 0 if none>, now)` (declining: `pinsDecline`, above); the epoch pin moves into `left`, and the tombstone's version is never below what the pin knew (`signed_since`, `roster_version_max`). A tombstoned vault served again is refused (`left`), unless re-shared with a newer roster (above), which goes through consent; accepting (`pinsAccept`) clears the tombstone and sets `rejoined_at`, which is what lets the re-joined pin win a later merge against that tombstone (above). **A removal is observed, not assumed** (cross-client rule, round 4): an app writes a tombstone for a vault that disappeared only after confirmation — the vault absent from **two consecutive verified syncs**, or its members/rosters requests answering 404 — never after one sync that omitted it (a server omitting a vault once must not make the account give it up).

**Share statements**: whoever seals a vault key to a member signs `["passauth/share/v1", vault id, recipient user id, recipient identity hash (32), role, key epoch (u64), SHA-256 of the sealed key's base64url text (32), signer user id]`. The epoch is the key's: the vault's current epoch when adding or re-signing, the new epoch (current + 1) in a key replacement. `SigningIdentity.sealAndSignShare(vaultKey, vaultId, recipient, role, keyEpoch)` → `{sealed_key, signature}` seals and signs in one call (the recipient's identity must verify); `signShare` signs an already sealed key. `verifyShare(vaultId, recipient, role, keyEpoch, sealedKey, signer, signature)` → the signer's identity info, else `bad_signature` (a legacy signer signs nothing). A signed share always comes with a roster: a row with `share` but no roster is refused.
- Requests: `POST /vaults/{id}/members {user_id, role, sealed_key, key_epoch, signature, roster}`; `/rotate` and `/members/{user}/remove` keys `{user_id, wrapped_key, key_kind, signature}` (every `sealed` key, with the body's `roster`; none on `account` keys), and with a `roster` every item must carry its `revision` (400 otherwise; a stale one is 409 `revision_conflict`, so a concurrent edit is never overwritten by the re-encrypted copy — apps from before omitted it). The server checks each signature with the caller's stored identity (`signature` without `roster` or the reverse: 400; caller without identity: 400 `signing_identity_required`; not verifying: 400 `bad_signature`) and stores it (`vault_members.share_signature`, `share_signer` = the caller, and `share_signer_identity` = a snapshot of the caller's identity then, `{user_id, email, name, public_key, signing_key, identity_signature, x25519_signature}`; NULL when unsigned).
- Returned as `share` = `{signature, signer: {user_id, email, name, public_key, signing_key, identity_signature, x25519_signature}, signer_deleted}` or `null` (unsigned). `signer` is the snapshot taken when the share was signed, so a share still verifies after its signer changed their name or email or deleted their account (`signer_deleted: true`); apps verify and pin-check the snapshot like any identity and label such a share "Signed by <pinned email> (account deleted)" on `/sync` vault rows (full and delta), `/sync` `invitations` and `GET /vaults/{id}/members` rows (which add `key_kind`; top level `key_epoch`, `member_roster`, `member_roster_seq`). `account` rows never have one. Invitations also carry `key_epoch`.

**Member rosters**: per vault, the signed list of who holds its key. JSON (stored and returned as the text the app produced; parse it with the core, never by hand): `{format: 1, vault_id, version, key_epoch, key_commitment, members: [{user_id, identity (hash), role}] sorted by user id (bytes), each once, exactly one owner, signer: {user_id, public_key, signing_key, identity_signature, x25519_signature}, signature}`; unknown fields are refused. Signed bytes: `["passauth/member-roster/v1", vault id, version (u64), key epoch (u64), key commitment (32), member count (u64), then per member: user id, identity hash (32), role; signer user id, signer identity hash (32)]`. Every member can check the key they opened is the roster's (`VaultKey.matchesRoster(vaultId, json)`: also false for another vault's roster), also owners whose key is account-wrapped. A roster on its own (`SignedRoster::parse`/`check`) must list its signer as owner or admin with exactly the signer's full identity.
- **Chain** (`verifyRosterChain(vaultId, trusted|null, newer[], latest)`): every roster is `vaultId`'s; at most 1000 versions per call (`too_large`); version 1 starts the chain (signed by an owner or admin it lists); each next version: version + 1 (no gaps), key epoch not lower (`rollback`), at the same epoch the same key commitment, the same owner user id, signed by an owner or admin **of the version before** with exactly their full identity listed there, and within the **hierarchy** (review M1): only the owner changes the owner entry, adds an admin or raises someone to admin; an admin's entry otherwise changes (identity, a lower role) only by the owner or that admin; any manager may remove an admin (one who left; removing only takes access away); a member's identity hash changes only by the owner or that member; roles between read and write any manager (all else `roster_unauthorized`). The chain must end **exactly** at `latest` (the row's `member_roster`): shorter is malformed, longer `rollback`, another roster of the same version `roster_forked`. `rosterHead(vaultId, remembered, latest)` checks both are `vaultId`'s.
- **Signing** (`SigningIdentity.signRoster(vaultKey, vaultId, previous|null, keyEpoch, members, reviewed)`): the version is `previous.version + 1` (1 without one), where `previous` is the roster this device trusts (verified), and it must equal the server's `member_roster_seq` + 1 (else sync and check again; never take the version from the server's number alone). `reviewed` is the digest from `rosterReview` of the list the user saw: the core recomputes the list from `members` and throws `review_mismatch` for any other (so the member rows are fetched again right before signing and must give the same list; on `review_mismatch` show the review again). A signer who is neither the owner nor the member keeps a member's legacy listing from `previous` (only the owner or the member replaces it with the full hash). The core refuses changes outside the hierarchy (`roster_unauthorized`) and owners/admins without a signing identity, and checks `previous` on its own again.
- **Reviews** (`rosterReview(vaultId, previous|null, myPublicIdentity, memberRows, pins)` → `{vault_id, version, first, blocked, members: [{user_id, role, identity, fingerprint, is_self, pin, verified, pinned_email, listed_email, change: same|added|identity_changed|role_changed}], missing: [{user_id, role, identity, pinned_email}], digest}`; member rows may carry the server's `email`, never signed). `blocked` (a member's pin `changed`/`email_conflict`, the server lists another identity for me, or a member's identity differs from the trusted roster's) → stop, nothing is sealed or signed; a blocked review's `digest` is empty, so `signRoster` throws `review_mismatch` for it. The digest (`["passauth/member-list/v2", vault id, version (u64), first (u64: 1 or 0), previous roster's `rosterHash` (32) or an empty field, member count, members…]`, round 4 I3) binds the version, `first` and the previous roster, so a review made after one roster can't approve a signing after another, or a version 1. **Version 1 needs an explicit review** (review H4): whenever there is no trusted roster (`first: true`) — sharing, a key replacement (remove, rotate, emergency access, `rotation_pending`), re-signing — the app shows every member (pinned email first, else the listed one, the identity fingerprint and its pin state: new, verified, not verified) with "First time this vault is signed: compare these fingerprints with the people", and continues only on the user's explicit confirmation. Automatic or background flows never create version 1 (they stop and point to the vault's sharing page); a key replacement never seals to members only the server vouches for. `missing` members (in the trusted roster, not listed now) are confirmed **per user id** (the confirmed set must contain every missing user id; a confirmation for one vault or one review doesn't carry over).
- Server: latest in `vaults.member_roster` + `member_roster_seq` (0 = none), every version in `vault_rosters (vault_id, version, roster, signer_id, created_at)`, deleted with the vault. A roster is sent only with a change (`roster` in `POST /members`, `/rotate`, `/members/{user}/remove`, `/resign`) and checked inside that change's transaction, after it: `version` = stored + 1 (else 409 `member_roster_changed`), signed by the caller with their stored identity (else 400 `bad_signature`), `vault_id` the path's, `key_epoch` the vault's after the change, members = exactly the vault's members after the change (invitees included; roles; identity hashes matching the stored identities, full or legacy) — else 409 `members_changed` with `{key_epoch, unknown, identity_changed, role_changed, missing}` — and a next link of the chain from the stored roster (`verify_roster_chain`; 403 `roster_unauthorized` outside the hierarchy). **Once a vault has a roster**, adding or re-keying members and key replacements without one are 400 `update_required` ("this vault's members are signed: update PassAuth to change them"); leaving, declining, cancelling an invitation and `DELETE /vaults/{id}/members/{user}` stay possible unsigned (the roster then lists someone gone until the next key replacement, which `rotation_pending` asks for).
- `GET /vaults/{id}/rosters?after=N` (members and invitees, who thereby see the member list before accepting: accepted, review I4; 600 per hour): `{rosters: [<json text>…], latest, more}`, versions after N oldest first, 100 per page. **Per sync and vault, apps fetch at most 1000 versions (10 pages) and 16 MiB of roster text**; beyond that the vault is refused ("too many changes to check here") instead of trusting a partial chain. *Checkpoints* (so a long history is checked over several syncs, round 3 L10): the verified prefix may be remembered with the existing API: `verifyRosterChain(vaultId, remembered|null, page, page[page.length − 1])` returns the page's last roster, verified from the remembered one; store it as the vault's remembered roster (the next `rosterHead` then fetches only after it). A checkpoint is never used to admit the vault (no `pinsAdmit` with it, no `matchesRoster`, no member or role check): the vault stays refused until the chain reaches the row's `member_roster` exactly.
- `/sync` vault rows and invitations: `member_roster` (latest text or null), `member_roster_seq`; answers of the writes add `member_roster_seq`.

**What the apps do** (checks are **sticky**: on once the device has any trust memory or local pins, or the server sent any identity, share, roster or `key_pins_seq` > 0, even if `/api/config` stops listing `signed_sharing`; a missing or failing `/api/config` means "unknown", never "off"):
0. *Unlock*: identity, own-identity check and "me" as under Signing identity; read the key pins (rollback rule); at the first signed sync write `grandfathered` with `pinsGrandfather(pins, [{vault_id, key_id}], createdIdentity)` (trust memory missing: see Grandfathering).
1. *Every sync, every vault row* (before anything is shown): (a) open the key (`sealed`: as before; `account`: with `row.key_epoch` and `pinsLegacyWrapAllowed`). (b) Roster: `rosterHead(row.id, remembered, row.member_roster)` → `same` / `fetch {after}` (within the caps, then `verifyRosterChain(row.id, remembered, newer, row.member_roster)`) / `rollback` or `roster_forked` → refuse. With a trusted roster: `row.key_epoch` = its `key_epoch`, `VaultKey.matchesRoster(row.id, roster)`, and `rosterMemberRole(roster, me)` = `row.role` (review L5). (c) `key_kind = sealed` with a `share`: `verifyIdentity` + `pinCheck` the signer (`changed`/`email_conflict` refuse), `verifyShare(row.id, me, row.role, row.key_epoch, row.wrapped_key, share.signer, share.signature)` (me = `SigningIdentity.publicIdentity()`, or `Account.legacyIdentity()` before I have one; a share to my legacy identity also verifies), and `rosterSignerRole(roster, signer)` must be owner or admin. (d) `pinsAdmit(pins, {vault_id: row.id, key_kind, key: VaultKey.epochPin(row.id, row.key_epoch) or null, roster_version, roster_hash: rosterHash(trusted roster) or null, chain_hashes (when a chain was fetched in (b)), signed_share, paused})`: `ok` → use it (**when `admission.pin` is true: `pinsAccept` of the same observation**, or use `pinsObserve` which does it; a `new` signer pin: `pinSet`; write the key pins once per sync, batched — only now, after every check of the row passed; remember the roster only now too); `consent` → show it as an invitation needing consent (accept with `pinsAccept`; Decline only when `declinable`, see Consent); `refused` → the vault shows locked with "PassAuth couldn't verify this shared vault's key, so it isn't used. Ask its owner, or your server's admin." (with the reason) and is never used for fill, save or display; never silently fall back.
2. *Labels*: `ok` with `trust: unsigned` = a grandfathered share from before: usable, labelled "Not yet signed" (vault header and Vaults & sharing), and owners/admins see "Sign this vault" (step 6).
3. *Invitations*: on a server with `signed_sharing`, an invitation with `share: null` is not accepted: show it with Decline only and "This invitation isn't signed. Ask <name> to update PassAuth and share again." A signed one: `verifyIdentity` + `pinCheck` the signer (`changed`/`email_conflict` stop), `verifyShare(vault_id, me, role, invitation.key_epoch, wrapped_key, signer, signature)`, `GET …/rosters?after=0` (or after the remembered roster) → `verifyRosterChain(vault_id, remembered|null, …, member_roster)`, `rosterSignerRole(roster, signer)` owner/admin, `rosterMemberRole(roster, me)` = the invitation's role, the opened key `matchesRoster(vault_id, …)`, and `pinsAdmit` with `key_kind: "sealed"` and `invitation: true` must not be `refused` (a tombstoned vault needs the newer roster; `own_vault`/`held` refuse), and its `consent` flags are shown (`rejoin`, `held`: "a new key for <vault>, which you have"); then show "Signed by <pinned email, else the signer's> " with the signer's identity fingerprint and pin state. Accepting pins the signer, `pinsAccept(pins, {vault_id, key_kind: "sealed", key, roster_version: roster.version, roster_hash: rosterHash(roster), chain_hashes: {<version>: rosterHash} of every roster verified, signed_share: true, paused, invitation: true})` (clears a tombstone, marks the re-join), remembers the roster. Declining (only when `declinable`) writes `pinsDecline(pins, vault_id, <vault id among this sync's rows>, now)`.
4. *Sharing a vault* (owner/admin): `/users/lookup` → `verifyIdentity` → `pinCheck(pins, identity, email)` (`new`: show the fingerprint and ask to compare; `changed`/`email_conflict`: stop) → `GET …/members` (its `key_epoch` must be the verified one) → `rosterReview(vault, trusted|null, me, rows + the new member with their role, pins)` (no trusted roster: the version-1 review; `blocked`: stop; any other `added`/`identity_changed` than the new member: stop as in step 5) → confirm → pin the new member → `sealAndSignShare(key, vault, recipient, role, current key_epoch)` → `GET …/members` again → `signRoster(key, vault, trusted|null, key_epoch, rows + new member, review.digest)` → `POST /vaults/{id}/members` with `signature`, `roster`, `key_epoch`. A role change: the same with the new role. On 409 `member_roster_changed` / `members_changed` / `vault_key_changed` or `review_mismatch`: sync, check and review again (never retry silently).
5. *Replacing a key* (`/rotate`, `/members/{user}/remove`; also the emergency-access and `rotation_pending` flows, on phones too): re-encrypt **only from the state verified in this same sync** (key, items, meta, epoch; review Android H2) and require the members answer's `key_epoch` to equal the verified one (else sync again). `rosterReview(vault, trusted|null, me, remaining rows, pins)`: `blocked` → stop ("The server lists members your vault's signed member list doesn't: <emails>. PassAuth didn't change the key."); `first` → the version-1 review, explicit confirmation, never automatic; `added`/`identity_changed`/`role_changed` → stop; `missing` (left, declined, deleted) → confirm per user id. Then: new key; own row `VaultKey.wrapFor(account, vault)` (`account`, v1 for now); every other member `sealAndSignShare(newKey, vault, row, row.role, key_epoch + 1)`; every item re-encrypted **with its `revision`**; `signRoster(newKey, vault, trusted|null, key_epoch + 1, remaining rows, review.digest)`; send with `key_epoch` (the old one). After the server accepted it: `pinsRecordRotation(pins, vault, "account", newKey.epochPin(vault, key_epoch + 1), roster.version, rosterHash(roster))` (persisted as unsent first, see key pins), then write the key pins. Before any key replacement the device must have its pins loaded and readable (not paused, not unreadable memory). The member set checked to decide anything (e.g. "personal" for emergency access) is the same set the replacement uses (no second request deciding).
6. *Sign a vault from before* (owners/admins, vaults with `member_roster_seq` 0 or a sealed member whose `share` is null): the version-1 review (every member with fingerprint and pin state; `blocked` stops) → for every `key_kind = sealed` member (the caller's own sealed row too) `sealAndSignShare(currentKey, vault, row, row.role, key_epoch)` → `signRoster(currentKey, vault, null, key_epoch, all rows, review.digest)` → `POST /vaults/{id}/resign {key_epoch, keys: [{user_id, wrapped_key, signature}], roster}` (owner/admin; `keys` = exactly the sealed members (400 otherwise); `key_epoch` must be current (409 `vault_key_changed`); 30 per hour; one transaction; invitees get a revision; event `vault.resigned`; answer `{revision, member_roster_seq}`). Re-signing vouches only for the key the re-signing admin holds and the identities they checked. After it: `pinsRecordRotation(pins, vault, <own row's key_kind>, currentKey.epochPin(vault, key_epoch), 1, rosterHash(roster))`, so the re-signing device doesn't then see its own vault as `first_signed`; every other member's device asks once (`first_signed`).
7. *Emergency access "personal" vaults* (with signed sharing): a vault counts as personal only if the user is its owner with an `account` key, the server's member list (the same answer the wraps then use) is exactly the user, no trusted roster lists anyone else, and its epoch pin has no `signed_since`.
8. *Never*: accept a roster, share or identity the core refused; trust the server's `fingerprint` fields or `user.id`; treat a server-listed role or member as authoritative where a trusted roster exists; write pins before a row's checks passed; seal to members nobody reviewed.

Core API — Rust (`passauth_core::sharing`): `UnlockedAccount::{new_signing_identity → NewSigningIdentity {identity, record}, open_signing_identity(&IdentityRecord), legacy_identity, seal_key_pins(&KeyPins, seq), open_key_pins(sealed, seq)}`, `keys`: `wrap_is_bound(wrapped)`, `UnlockedAccount::{new_vault_key(vault) (v1), new_vault_key_bound(vault, epoch) (v2), wrap_vault_key(key, vault) (v1), wrap_vault_key_bound(key, vault, epoch) (v2), open_vault_key(vault, epoch, wrapped, legacy_allowed)}`; `SigningIdentity::{user_id, public, identity_hash, fingerprint, sign_share, seal_and_sign_share → SignedShare {sealed_key, signature}, sign_roster(key, vault, previous, epoch, members, reviewed) → SignedRoster}` (`sign_roster_unchecked` only for tests); `PublicIdentity {user_id, public_key, signing_key, identity_signature, x25519_signature}::verify → VerifiedIdentity {user_id, is_signing, hash, hash_b64, legacy_hash, public_key, fingerprint, matches, is_exactly}`; `xeddsa_sign`, `xeddsa_verify`, `xeddsa_public_key`, `identity_statement`, `identity_hash`, `share_statement(&ShareStatement)`, `verify_share`; `SignedRoster::{parse, check, to_json, signed_bytes, member_role, signer_role, commits_to(vault, key)}`, `verify_roster_chain(vault, trusted, newer, latest)` (`MAX_ROSTER_CHAIN` 1000), `roster_head(vault, remembered, latest) → RosterHead {Unsigned, Same, Fetch{after}}`, `compare_members → MemberDiff {unknown, identity_changed, role_changed, missing, is_clean}`, `roster_review → RosterReview {vault_id, version, first, blocked, members: [ReviewMember], missing: [ReviewMissing], digest (empty when blocked)}` (`MemberChange`), `member_list_digest(vault, previous, members)`, `SignedRoster::hash`; `key_commitment`, `key_id`, `EpochPin {key_epoch, key_id, retired, signed_since, roster_version_max, roster_hash, rejoined_at, rejoined_over, own, bound_since}::of(key, vault, epoch)` (`MAX_RETIRED_KEY_IDS` 1024, `MAX_TOTAL_RETIRED_KEY_IDS` 4096, `MAX_TOMBSTONE_RETIRED_KEY_IDS` 64, `RETIRED_KEY_ID_BYTES` 12), `epoch_check → EpochStatus {New, Same, Newer, Older, Conflict, Downgraded}`; `normalize_email`; `KeyPins {version, pins, epochs, own_identity, grandfathered: {vault id: key id}, left}::{check, check_identity(identity, email) → PinStatus {New, Match, Changed, Upgraded, EmailConflict}, set, merge (unsent changes), merge_last_known (a device's last-known copy), check_epoch(vault, current, roster_version), record_rotation(vault, key_kind, key, roster_version, roster_hash), leave(vault, roster_version, now), decline(vault, current_row, now), grandfather(&[GrandfatherCandidate {vault_id, key_id}], created_identity), admit(&VaultObservation {…, roster_hash, chain_hashes, invitation, bound_wrap}) → Admission {Ok{epoch, trust: Trust {Own, Signed, Unsigned}, pin}, Consent{rejoin, replaced, own, first_signed, held, declinable}, Refused{reason: RefuseReason {MissingEpoch, Paused, Left, Older, Conflict, Downgraded, RosterForked, OwnVault, Held}}}, observe(&VaultObservation) → Observed {admission, pins, changed}, accept(&VaultObservation) → KeyPins, legacy_wrap_allowed(vault, key_epoch), check_own_identity → OwnIdentityStatus {New, Match, Mismatch}, set_own_identity}`; `keys::UnlockedAccount::matches_bundle(&AccountBundle)` (`Tombstone {pin, roster_version, left_at}`). Errors (`Error::code`): `bad_signature`, `identity_mismatch`, `rollback`, `roster_forked`, `roster_unauthorized`, `too_large`, `review_mismatch` (others carry their description). WASM: `Account.matchesBundle(bundle)`, `Account.newSigningIdentity()` → `NewSigningIdentity {record, takeIdentity()}`, `Account.openSigningIdentity(record)`, `legacyIdentity()`, `sealKeyPins(pins, seq)`, `openKeyPins(sealed, seq)`, `openVaultKey(vaultId, wrapped, kind, keyEpoch, legacyAllowed)`, `newVaultKeyBound(vaultId, keyEpoch)`; class `SigningIdentity {userId, publicIdentity, fingerprint, identityHash, signShare, sealAndSignShare, signRoster(vaultKey, vaultId, previous, keyEpoch, members, reviewed)}`; `VaultKey.commitment(vaultId, keyEpoch)`, `VaultKey.keyId(vaultId)`, `VaultKey.epochPin(vaultId, keyEpoch)`, `VaultKey.matchesRoster(vaultId, json)`, `VaultKey.wrapFor(account, vaultId)` (v1), `VaultKey.wrapForBound(account, vaultId, keyEpoch)` (v2), `accountWrapIsBound(wrapped)` (and the existing `shareTo`, unsigned); numbers are whole 0…2^53−1). UniFFI (JSON strings for objects): `Account.{new_vault_key_bound(vault_id, key_epoch), matches_bundle(bundle_json), new_signing_identity → NewSigningIdentity {identity, record_json}, open_signing_identity(record_json), legacy_identity_json, seal_key_pins(pins_json, seq), open_key_pins(sealed, seq), open_vault_key(vault_id, wrapped, kind, key_epoch, legacy_allowed)}`; object `SigningIdentity {user_id, public_identity_json, fingerprint, identity_hash, sign_share, seal_and_sign_share → SignedShare {sealed_key, signature}, sign_roster(vault_key, vault_id, previous, key_epoch, members_json, reviewed)}`; `VaultKey.{share_to(vault_id, recipient_public_key) (unsigned, for servers/vaults without signing), wrap_for(account, vault_id) (v1), wrap_for_bound(account, vault_id, key_epoch) (v2), commitment, key_id(vault_id), epoch_pin_json(vault_id, key_epoch), matches_roster(vault_id, roster_json)}`; functions `verify_identity`, `verify_share`, `verify_roster_chain(vault_id, trusted, newer, latest)`, `roster_head(vault_id, remembered, latest)`, `compare_members`, `roster_member_role`, `roster_signer_role`, `roster_review(vault_id, previous, signer_json, members_json, pins_json)`, `epoch_check`, `pin_check(pins_json, identity_json, email)`, `pin_set`, `merge_pins` (unsent changes), `pins_merge_last_known(server_json, last_known_json)`, `pins_epoch_check(…, roster_version)`, `pins_record_rotation(pins_json, vault_id, key_kind, current_json, roster_version, roster_hash)`, `pins_leave`, `pins_decline(pins_json, vault_id, current_row, now)`, `pins_grandfather(pins_json, vaults_json, created_identity)`, `pins_admit`, `pins_observe(pins_json, observation_json)`, `pins_accept(pins_json, observation_json)`, `roster_hash(roster_json)`, `normalize_email(email)`, `pins_legacy_wrap_allowed(pins_json, vault_id, key_epoch)`, `account_wrap_is_bound(wrapped)`, `pins_check_own_identity`, `pins_set_own_identity`; `CoreError::{BadSignature, IdentityMismatch, Rollback, RosterForked, RosterUnauthorized, TooLarge, ReviewMismatch}`.

Remaining limits (stated in the apps' help): with unreadable device memory and a server listing no key pins, own vaults are checked without pins (there is nothing to check against); the first unlock after upgrading may use the offline cache as the earlier copy, which can include rows a background refresh wrote while locked; on a fresh device (no trust memory, no earlier offline copy) a server can turn the checks off by omitting `signed_sharing` and sending nothing signed (apps that keep vault manifests — web 0.15.0, desktop 0.13.0, extension, iOS and Android 0.12.0 — close this: they refuse to use a server that has `api` ≥ 2 without `signed_sharing`, or lists no features at all, with "Update your PassAuth server to keep using this app." on the sign-in screen and as the sync error, using no sync data; the hook is where each app reads `/api/config` and decides whether the checks are on), or fake the account's first signed sync — the latter only produces consent prompts, never a silently used vault; in a vault that has no manifest yet a server can serve an older copy of an item to a device that never saw the newer one, or hide recent changes and deletions (it can't forge or move items; a device that saw a newer edit counter flags the older copy, see Item edit counter); with a manifest that is left only for a device that never saw a later manifest version, and then only as one whole earlier state of the vault (see Vault manifests); trust on first use: the first identity an app sees for someone is trusted until compared (the fingerprint check is what makes it a proof; `email_conflict` catches a second account posing with a known email, not a first one); a device with no memory of a vault trusts the roster chain from version 1 the server shows it, bounded by the account's `signed_since`, highest roster version **and its hash** (`chain_hashes`) and retired key ids (a chain of valid signatures that contains the roster the account last trusted, possibly not the newest); for a vault the account never saw signed that chain may be signed entirely by an account the server made up ("puppet signer"), which can only reach the user as a consent prompt (`consent {replaced}` or a first acceptance), never as a silently used vault — the fingerprint shown there is the protection; tombstones keep only the newest 64 retired key ids, so a re-join with a very old key relabelled at a higher epoch is stopped by the share signature and roster key commitment (which bind key and epoch), not by the tombstone; email local parts have no confusables check (Display); grandfathering trusts the unsigned shared vaults present at the first signed sync of the unlock that created the identity; an admin can remove a member and add a different identity under the same user id in a later version (the members' pin checks show it as `changed`, but only to members who pinned the old identity; the roster keeps no list of former identities: round 3 L6, kept as a limit); key pins hold each pinned identity's raw keys (compacting them to identity hashes is a follow-up); shares from before stay unsigned until re-signed; personal (account-wrapped) vaults without a roster have only the account's epoch pins and key ids (v1 wraps, written while some app of the account doesn't read v2, don't bind the epoch; v1 at or below an epoch seen in v2 is refused); the web vault's code is served by the server (full protection only in the desktop, extension and phone apps). Round 5: the two verified syncs that confirm a removal are in the server's hands (it can send them seconds apart over the live socket), so a server can still make an app tombstone a vault it hid — the tombstone only refuses that vault until a re-share with a newer roster, which comes back through consent; a leave and a re-join made on different devices are ordered by those devices' clocks (`left_at` against `rejoined_over`); key pins (person pins, tombstones) grow without a bound other than the server's 190 KiB limit on the sealed copy; the core's ICU data and the browsers' Unicode tables may disagree on characters newer than the older of the two.

- **Web vault and desktop app**: `web/src/lib/signed-sharing.ts` (protocol), pure helpers in `signing.ts`, UI in `components/SignedSharing.tsx`. Device memory per server and account: `pa.vault-trust.<server origin>|<account id>` = `{on, own, vaults: {<id>: {roster, signed}}}` and `pa.key-pins.<origin>|<account id>` = `{seq, sealed, dirty}`; writes merge (higher roster version, marks only turn on, own identity kept, local pins keep the higher seq), every pins change is saved at once marked unsent, and the desktop app's Change server keeps these keys. Checks run in `vault.ts` `doSync` (refused if `user.id` isn't the unlocked account) on every sync: a server whose fetched `/api/config` doesn't list `signed_sharing` is refused (`api.ts` `serverTooOld`: `boot` asks for no `/sync` and shows "Update your PassAuth server to keep using this app." in place of the vault, `doSync` throws it before the request, `login`/`register` before sending anything, the desktop app's Connect screen at once), and so is one that answers 404 for the identity while its config can't be fetched; with an unknown config the checks run "unproven" (nothing sticky written) unless `checksRemembered`. Refused vaults → `State.blockedVaults`; never-accepted sealed vaults → `State.consents`, shown with invitations (Accept only signed ones, replaced keys, or unsigned ones while trust memory is missing; Decline via `pinsDecline`, offered only when `declinable`, otherwise "Not now"). Verdicts are cached per row plus the remembered roster, the vault's pins and the pinned people. Sharing, key replacement and re-signing go through `planChange` and the `ReviewDialog` (`ReviewNeededError` → the UI calls again with `{confirmed: {digest, missing}}`). Emergency access counts a vault as personal only without `sharedEvidence` (a trusted roster listing others, a seen-signed mark, `signed_since`) and re-keys only with the member set it checked (`rotateVaultKeys(ids, members)`). Final round (2026-11-16): `decideChecks` returns proven / unproven / off (unknown config = unproven: checks run, nothing sticky is written until the server proves it signs; a 404 on `/account/identity` = doesn't sign; rows without `key_epoch` = old server, off); the "earlier offline copy" is the per-scope list `pa.vault-seen.<server>|<account>` of vaults this device opened, read once per unlock (no list = new device: nothing grandfathered, consent with "This device had no earlier copy of your shared vaults…"); the vaults waiting at the first signed sync stay acceptable unsigned on later unlocks (device memory `missing`, written once, only shrinks; Accept or later, no Decline); identity hashes generated here are remembered (`generated`) so a lost `PUT` answer still counts as created, `identity_exists` skips grandfathering; Accept is bound to the signer hash, key id, epoch and roster the dialog showed (`CHANGED_SINCE_SHOWN`; unsigned consent shows the key id); `email_conflict` has its own wording and a required "I compared it" tick; pins after a review or trust dialog use the emails shown; an accepted shared vault missing from the server is tombstoned only after confirmation (absent from two consecutive verified syncs, or `GET /vaults/{id}/members` answering 404), never while paused (explicit Leave or Delete still records one while paused); every signed key replacement sends item revisions. Round 4 (2026-11-16): accepts use `pinsAccept` with `chain_hashes` and pin the email that was shown; Decline is offered only for signed consents, unsigned ones are accepted by the key id shown or stay locked with "Ask the vault's owner to sign this vault."; before the account's first signed sync is finished on this device unsigned sealed rows are held as `STILL_CHECKING`; device memory also holds `generated`, `missing`/`missingWhy` and `earlier` (the grandfathering candidates recorded once when the identity is generated; cleared with `generated` once the pins hold the own identity and the grandfathered list); unreadable device memory refuses shared vaults and checks own vaults against the server's pins read-only, and writes nothing; newer server pins are always merged with the device's copy and written back when the merge adds something; a 429 on a key replacement says how long to wait. Round 5 (2026-11-16): own key replacements and re-signs are recorded with `pinsRecordRotation`; newer server pins are merged with `pinsMergeLastKnown` unless there are unsent changes (`mergePins`), and `identity_mismatch` pauses sharing and freezes pin writes for the session; signed invitation declines are tombstoned only after the server answers, unsigned or refused ones tombstone nothing; the desktop "Never" restore and every `/sync` bundle are checked with `matchesBundle`; the Export page names the vaults it leaves out. The "two consecutive verified syncs" removal confirmation is paced by the server (it can push two syncs within seconds), so it is not a time guarantee. Vault manifests (web 0.15.0 / desktop 0.13.0; rules in `web/src/lib/manifest.ts`, state in `vault.ts`; the desktop app has no code of its own for them): **no `/sync` answer is taken over unchecked.** `doSync` fetches, runs the signed sharing checks, then `checkManifests` (`verifyVault` for every opened vault with all its rows of that answer; also when the server no longer lists `vault_manifest` but the row carries a manifest or this device remembers one, which then reads as a rollback), and only then `adopt` makes the answer the copy and shows it. *Device memory* is `{version, link, accepts}` per vault in `pa.vault-manifests.<origin>|<account id>` (same store and scope as the edit counters, kept across Change server; `ManifestMemory`). In ordinary use a version is only raised and a link never replaced at its version; tabs merge by the higher `accepts`, then the higher version, and at equal versions the stored entry wins (the tab that held another link takes the stored one, so its next check says so). Only an acceptance replaces an entry whatever it held (`replace`, also downwards) or empties it (`forget`, keeping the count), as read-modify-write with `accepts + 1`, so a tab that still holds the earlier entry can't bring it back. The all-zero link and an entry in the first format are nothing remembered. *An unreadable record* is never checked against or written over: vaults that have a manifest are "unchecked" (no warning, writes wait; never an earlier "ok" over new rows, also when the record breaks mid-session), vaults without one are written as before, no first manifest is started. After three syncs in a row that couldn't read it (counted in `pa.vault-manifests.failed.<scope>`, so reloads count), with one whole fetch among them, the notice "PassAuth can't read this device's record of your vaults." offers "Reset on this device" (`resetDeviceRecord`: the record is moved to `pa.vault-manifests.unreadable.<scope>` and the device goes on as one that remembers nothing, from a full sync). *The log:* `need_log` (a manifest two or more versions above the remembered one; one version above with another predecessor is a `mismatch` at once) → `GET …/manifest-log?after=<remembered>` (up to 5 pages), entries handed to the core exactly as the server sent them, and a second check with it; while the log can't be fetched (no answer, 5xx, 429) the whole answer is not taken over (copy, shown items and verdicts stay, the cursor doesn't move, the vault is "unchecked"), a 404/400, a malformed or never-ending log is a `mismatch`. *Races never raise a warning:* an answer that was on its way or being checked while this app showed an own write without asking (`ownMoves`: an import's batches, a first manifest, old trash) may be older than that write and than what the device remembers of it, so it is neither judged nor taken over but asked for again, without counting toward any limit; after 8 answers the sync ends without one (an import ends with a sync of its own, anything else is asked once more). An answer older than an own write already shown (a vault's `seq` below it) with no such write in between is fetched again whole, twice at most, and an answer that stays behind is then checked like any other, so a real rollback is reported. A failing verdict from a delta gets one full fetch before a warning is shown; a whole answer whose `rollback` is against an entry another tab changed while it was on its way gets one more fetch (two tabs); if a fetch fails nothing is taken over. A vault that already carries its warning from a whole answer isn't fetched in full again at every sync. *Items that can't be opened:* a vault whose manifest checks out but which holds a row its key can't open (a broken `item_key`; live or trashed) is unresolved too, with its own text (`Vault.manifestUnopenable`: "An item in this vault can't be opened. Delete it to keep using the vault.", for readers "…Someone who can edit this vault can delete it."), and that manifest is still what the device remembers. Such rows are in `State.unopenable` and listed in their vault (or the trash) as "Item that can't be opened" with "Delete for good" (`purgeUnopenable`: `DELETE /items/{id}/permanent` with the vault's next manifest built from the row as synced; nothing is opened; exempt from the hold while the vault's own record checks out; a vault without a manifest gets the plain request). Other writes stay held until the row is gone; "Use the server's copy" isn't offered for this case, no first manifest is started over such a row, and a vault that still has one stays warned after an acceptance. The web vault never counts a row by a server-sent `data_sha256` (it asks for whole rows; a row without `data` = `incomplete` → one full fetch → warning). The rows handed to the core are built from `id`, `deleted_at`, `data` only; `/sync` numbers the app counts with (`seq`, `manifest_seq`, `key_epoch`, `deleted_at`) must be whole numbers 0…2^53−1 or the answer is refused whole (`delta.ts` `check`); "in the trash" is `deletedAt !== null` everywhere (`types.ts` `isTrashed`). *"Use the server's copy"* (`acceptServerCopy`; banner in the item list, `Vault.manifestWarning`): with each failing verdict the vault is also checked with nothing remembered (`acceptVault` = the core's `manifestAccept`) and `Vault.manifestAcceptable` says whether the served copy is consistent in itself. After the confirmation and a fresh whole fetch (an answer taken over after the click; otherwise nothing is accepted): if the vault checks out now, nothing to do; if the copy is consistent (`ok`, or the server shows no manifest: `none`) the device **accepts locally**: its memory becomes the served manifest's `{version, link}`, also when it remembered a later version, or is emptied for `none` (a first manifest then follows by the ordinary rule); no request, so everyone gets the action, members who can only read too. Otherwise a member who can write stores a **baseline**: `manifestStart` over exactly the served rows at the row's version + 1 (never anything computed from the memory; the server refuses everything else), naming `rowLink` of the row, `PUT …/manifest`; when the answer's `manifest_link` is the baseline's the memory is replaced by it. A 409 is tried again from a fresh fetch (three times in all); the rate limit (10 baselines per member and vault an hour, 429) shows the usual "Too many attempts…" message and leaves the warning. A reader in that case sees "Someone who can edit this vault can review it." After a baseline, devices on the history under it follow by themselves; the others see the vault as acceptable and clear their warning locally. Items the device had seen newer are accepted as served with the copy (`RevMemory.accept`: the only place an edit counter goes down). *Writes* go through `manifestFor` (refused locally while unresolved, waiting while unchecked): create, edit, move (one entry per vault that has a manifest), trash/restore/delete (query `manifest_seq` + `manifest_prev` + `manifest`, with `revision`), import batches, key replacement (`manifestStart` with the new key and epoch on `rowLink` of the old head); every entry carries `prev`. `manifest_conflict`/`manifest_required` sync and resend, three sends at most. An own write is shown without a sync, and its manifest remembered, only when the answer's `manifest_link` is the link of the manifest sent (`linkStored`, `delta.ts` `afterWrite`/`applyOwnWrite`, which also sets `manifest_prev`); an import batch chains on `manifest_links[vault]` the same way, a first manifest and a baseline on their answer's link. Anything else (a request sent again whose first try was stored under another manifest answers with the same version and that manifest's link) is a sync, and nothing of what was sent is remembered. After a checked answer `manifestUpkeep` starts a first manifest (always version 1; `manifest_ok`, write access, nothing remembered, every row opens, no item marked as served older than seen) and deletes due trash through `POST …/trash/purge`: one request of at most min(250, `max_import_items`) items per vault and answer, one attempt, and only items that are due by the server's rule (`deleted_at < server_time − trash_days·86400`) **and** that this device itself has seen in the trash for max(`trash_days`, 7) days by its own clock (`pa.trash-seen.<origin>|<account id>`, `TrashSeen`; first seen = when a copy holding the row trashed was taken over; forgotten when the row is live again or gone) with the two clocks within 24 h, so the server's word alone never empties a trash. Item edit counters (`item-revs.ts`) are forgotten only on proven absence: this device deleted the item for good, or it is missing from a vault whose manifest checked out for that copy; an item the server merely stops sending keeps its counter and the last copy shown (in memory), at most 20,000 counters. Part S can't be dodged by breaking `/api/config`: the feature list last fetched is kept per server (`pa.server-features.<origin>`), and an unfetchable config is "can't connect" (no sign-in, no `/sync` asked for or used) unless that list includes `signed_sharing` (`vault.ts` `usableConfig`). The emergency-access view checks each vault that has a manifest against its answer (`checkView`: no memory; rows from parsed fields; a row that can't be read or opened is a warning) and says "not checked" for a vault without one. Send: a password is never written v1 (`send-client.ts`: without `send_v2` creating a password Send or setting/changing a password is refused with "Update your PassAuth server to protect a Send with a password."), a v2 Send stays v2 while it keeps its password, a Send whose fields and content key disagree is shown as unreadable and never rewritten, and on a server with `send_v2` a v1 Send that the server lists with a password takes a new name or text only together with the password (which makes it v2) or with the password removed ("Enter this Send's password to change its text.": its v1 fields may be an earlier state served again). Known limits: one vault's manifest-log request failing holds the whole account's sync (and the unlock) until it passes; an edit counter accepted lower in one tab can be raised again by another tab that still holds the higher one (the item then shows its "older version" mark again until that tab reloads).
- **Web vault and desktop app, vault manifests** (`web/src/lib/manifest.ts` = rules, wording, device memory `pa.vault-manifests.<server origin>|<account id>`; `vault.ts` = requests; tests `manifest.test.ts`, `vault-manifest.test.ts`): every `/sync` answer is judged (signed sharing, then each vault's manifest) before it replaces the copy. **A server that went back:** an answer whose `revision` is below the one shown is dropped only when it can be a race (the copy moved since the request was sent: an import batch, a first manifest, old trash); otherwise it is a restored database, so the whole account is fetched once and judged like any answer, which gives the rollback warning and "Use the server's copy" in a tab or desktop app that stayed unlocked across the restore (the revision checks after a reconnect and on wake sync on any difference, not only a higher number). **Rows that don't open** never change a vault's verdict and never hold its writes: (a) when only the served `item_key` fails for a row whose id, vault and `data` this session already opened, the session keeps its copy and its key (shown, editable, not listed; an honest server never changes a key without the data, and the manifest covers the data); (b) a row that decrypts but holds an item this app can't read (the core's `json:` error, a type or format from a later release) is listed as "Item from a newer version of PassAuth" / "Update PassAuth to see this item.", is counted in manifests like any row, doesn't hold off a first manifest and can't be deleted by write or read members: owners and admins of the vault can delete it for good (own confirmation: "This item was saved by a newer version of PassAuth. Update PassAuth to see it. Deleting it removes it for everyone."; a key replacement refused over such rows says to update or have an owner delete them); (c) any other row that doesn't decrypt is listed as "Item that can't be opened" (with the name this session last knew, if any), the vault shows the notice "An item in this vault can't be opened." (a vault without a manifest: the line among the warnings, as before), members who can edit get "Delete for good" (confirmation: it may still open on another device or in a newer app, and deleting removes it for everyone) while the vault's own verdict is ok or it has no manifest, and no first manifest is started over such a row. A key replacement still waits for (b) and (c) rows (it re-encrypts every item). The web vault holds decrypted copies in memory only, so after a reload or lock case (a) is case (c). **Accepting "no record":** when the fresh answer shows no manifest for a vault this device remembers one for, `acceptServerCopy` forgets the memory only after its own confirmation ("Your server no longer shows a record of changes for this vault. If you continue, PassAuth stops checking this vault…"; also when the server showed a record until the first confirmation: the dialog asks again), marks the memory entry (`forgotten`) and the vault shows "This vault isn't being checked for missing or restored items." until a manifest is remembered again. An acceptance also drops the item marks (kept newer copies, edit counters) of the items the device held in that vault before the fetch, never of an id the fetched answer newly places there.
- **Web vault addresses** (`web/src/App.tsx`, `lib/routes.ts`, tests `App.test.tsx`): the sign-in page is the site root `/` (never `/login`, which only redirects to `/` for old links and keeps `state.from`); a signed-out visit to any app page, and Log out on any page, lands on `/` and sign-in returns to that page; signed in (locked or unlocked) `/` opens `/vault`, as an unknown address does. `/register`, `/verify-email`, `/admin…` and `/send/:id` are unchanged. The desktop app uses the same router inside its window. The server's SPA fallback serves `index.html` for `/` like any other non-API path.
- **Extension**: `background/signed-sharing.ts` (checks, texts, memory merge, caps) + `vault.ts` `checkSharing` at every sync and offline open, before anything is shown; "me" = the core's account id (`legacyIdentity().user_id`); a `/sync` or offline copy with another `user.id` is refused. Trust memory in IndexedDB `trust:<server>|<account id>` (pins seq + sealed copy + unsent flag, last trusted roster per vault, vaults seen signed, `missing` = vaults present when the trust memory was found missing), written by read-merge-write, kept across sign-out, removed with the account (`AccountData.userId`). Rows go through `pinsObserve`; verdicts in the offline copy carry `row` (opened only for that row) and `stamp` (row + pins digest + remembered roster + seen-signed + paused + own identity hash), and an offline open reuses only matching stamps. Refused and waiting vaults are never opened (only a waiting vault's name is decrypted). The popup notice lists refused vaults (with the reason), waiting vaults (signer with pinned email first, fingerprint; "Accept…" for signed ones from the signer shown, for unsigned key replacements and, while trust memory is missing, unsigned vaults by the key id shown; Decline in the web vault), the paused / older-pins / trust-memory-missing warnings, and invitations that can't be accepted (the extension doesn't accept invitations). "I compared it: trust the new key" pins only the fingerprint shown, as verified; `email_conflict` has no trust action. Roster fetching per sync and vault is capped at 1000 versions / 16 MiB / 10 pages. Names and emails are cleaned and emails shown in `normalizeEmail` form. The extension never shares or replaces keys. Final round (2026-11-16): the consent token is a SHA-256 of what was shown (vault id, role, key id, epoch, signer identity hash, roster hash, replaced/rejoin) and Accept refuses a changed row; verdict stamps include `CHECKS_VERSION`; grandfathering uses only vault ids from this browser's offline copy at unlock (kept per unlock in session storage `earlier:<account>`, survives worker restarts); other unsigned shared vaults wait for consent with "This browser had no earlier copy of your shared vaults, so each one shared before signing needs your OK once. Accept only the ones you expected."; trust memory also holds `missingWhy` and `pendingIdentity`; an accepted vault missing from the server is tombstoned only after two consecutive verified syncs without it or a 404 on its rosters (memory field `gone`); a failed key-pins GET on a fresh browser counts as offline; with the config unknown, checks run but nothing is kept until the server shows it signs (a server without any `key_epoch` keeps checks off). Invitations name the sharer only by the verified signer's pinned email; anything else is "listed by your server as from …". Round 4 (2026-11-16): accepts use `pinsAccept` with `chain_hashes`; the consent token (`consent/v2`) also binds the shown email, whether it was pinned, and `own`, and the pin uses exactly that email (upgraded pins keep their email and `verified`); Decline in the web vault is linked only for signed rows, unsigned rows that may be accepted show their key id, others are locked with "Ask the vault's owner to sign this vault."; before the account's first signed sync is finished here, unsigned sealed rows are held ("PassAuth is still checking this shared vault"); trust memory holds `pendingIdentities` (list), `candidates` (grandfathering candidates recorded once when the identity is generated, only rows with a usable verdict) and `gone`; keys restored from disk count as a new session for "earlier"; a 404 on the identity upload isn't retried before the next unlock; unreadable trust memory refuses shared vaults, checks own vaults against the server's pins read-only (else reuses their last ok verdict for the same row) and writes nothing (repair: a sealed copy that won't open and has no unsent changes is replaced by a strictly newer server copy); `CHECKS_VERSION` 5. Round 5 (2026-11-16): grandfathering candidates are `{vault_id, key_id}` (a vault whose key changed by the first signed sync waits locked); copies saved with checks off are marked `checks: "off"` and give no candidates; newer server pins merge with `pinsMergeLastKnown` (unsent changes: `mergePins`) and an `identity_mismatch` pauses sharing with no merge and no writes, and pins are never written while paused; unreadable memory uses a per-account floor `pinsFloor:<id>` in session storage; the consent token is `consent/v3` (also binds `firstSigned`), Decline only when `declinable`, `CHECKS_VERSION` 6; restores (session, disk, sign-in approval, PIN) require `Account.matchesBundle`, and a `/sync` answer or offline copy whose bundle doesn't match is refused. Security round 7/9: own (`account`) rows opened from a bound wrap add `bound_wrap` to the observation (`CHECKS_VERSION` 7) and every open passes the row's epoch to `pinsLegacyWrapAllowed` (the extension never creates or re-keys vaults and has no Sends, so it writes no v2 wraps or v2 Sends); item edit counters: `background/item-revs.ts`, checked in `vault.ts` `prepare` on every newly decrypted row (full, delta and live syncs); a rollback keeps the newer copy it can still open (with the server's `revision`, so it can be edited), else shows the server's, marked on the item page and in a dismissible vault-list notice until the row is rewritten. Device memory (`background/device-memory.ts`, storage `deviceMemory`: one IndexedDB record per server + core account id, `device:<server>|<id>`): item edit counters and marks, per vault the manifest `{version, link}`, and `trashSeen` (first seen in the trash, device clock). It is not part of the offline copy: a sign-out and the "log out" vault timeout remove the copy, the memory goes only with the account (`local.removeAccount`); written before the copy (read-merge-write, manifest entries by higher version, a link never replaced at its version); a record that can't be read is never "nothing": nothing is checked against it or written over it, no vault is "ok", no manifest is started. A counter is forgotten only when the item is absent from a vault whose manifest checked "ok" for that answer, or this browser purged it; otherwise it stays (cap 20,000, least recently seen first) and the row is kept aside in the copy (`stash`, cap 2,000), so hide-then-serve-older is still caught with the newer copy kept. Since 0.12.0: a server whose `/api/config` doesn't list `signed_sharing` is refused in `api.ts` before any request (`refuseOutdated`; "Update your PassAuth server to keep using this app."); the feature list of every config read is kept per server (`knownFeatures`), and a config that can't be fetched or read is "Can't reach your PassAuth server" unless a kept list has `signed_sharing` (offline use), so a server can't dodge the check by breaking `/api/config`; a sync answer is always checked, whatever the feature list or the rows say. Vault manifests: `background/manifests.ts` + `vault.ts`. An answer is a candidate until judged (`prepare` → `judge` → `commit`, else `discard`): nothing unverified is shown or stored. `judge` = the core's verdict per open vault with the row's `manifest_prev` and the remembered `{version, link}`; `need_log` (only for a manifest two or more versions ahead) → `GET /vaults/{id}/manifest-log?after=` (≤25 requests; only well-typed entries `{seq, prev, digest}` go to the core, each as it came: `isLogEntry`) and a second verdict; a log that can't be fetched fails the sync (previous copy and state stay, no warning, writes into that vault wait: `logPending`), 404/400 or a log that doesn't connect = mismatch. No warning from bookkeeping: a delta that would give rollback/mismatch/incomplete is not used and the whole vault is fetched once, which decides; the offline copy at unlock gives only "ok"/"none", else "unchecked" (no warning, one full sync, writes wait). A vault that shows its warning keeps it without a whole fetch at every sync (the delta is still judged, so it clears by itself when an answer checks out), and the warnings are stored with the offline copy (`StoredSync.warnings` = `{status, cause, stamp}` per vault, `stamp` = the vault row judged; only warnings are read back, never an "ok"), so a worker start shows them at once without fetching everything. An answer behind an own write is asked for again in full (2 times), then judged as it is. An answer whose `revision` is lower than the one shown is dropped only when it can be a race (`shownCount`: something else was shown since its request was sent; `sync-rules.ts` `olderAnswer`); otherwise the server went back (a restored database): a delta is not used, one whole answer is fetched and judged like any other, so the rollback warning and "Use the server's copy" work (`isStale` also counts a lower revision). Rows that don't open (`prepare`; final review T1) change no verdict and hold no write: (a) when this browser holds a row of the same id, vault and `data` that it opened before (the copy shown, a trashed row, `stash`) and only the served `item_key` fails, it goes on with its own row, key kept in the copy: nothing listed or warned; (b) a row that decrypts but isn't an item this version reads (the core's `json: …` error, `openFailure`) is listed as "Item from a newer version of PassAuth" / "Update PassAuth to see this item.", with no notice (owners and admins also read "It can be deleted for good in the web vault or the desktop app."), counted in manifests and no bar to a first manifest; (c) anything else (live: no data or a key/data that doesn't decrypt; trashed: only a row that came with its data, a trash summary can't be told) is listed as "Item that can't be opened" (with the last known name: the last opened copy stays in `stash`, encrypted) and its vault shows "An item in this vault can't be opened." (`unopenableVaults`; writers also "It can be deleted for good in the web vault or the desktop app." with the link; the extension has no such delete); no first manifest is started over a (c) row. The list's "Other items" section shows the (b) and (c) rows (`otherItems`, `ItemList.others`). Rows: numbers validated (`validNumbers`), device-private fields stripped (`stripPrivate`); the hash of what the server sent for a kept newer copy lives beside the rows (`StoredSync.served`), never on a row; in the trash = `deleted_at` not null. Writes: create/edit/trash carry `{seq, prev, sealed}` from `manifestNext` (query `manifest_prev` on trash), 3 tries, applied locally only when the answer's `manifest_link` is the link of the manifest sent (a create sent again is answered with the stored sealing's link: then a sync) and the copy still holds the manifest they were built on, then `{seq, link}` remembered; first manifest (`manifestStart(…, 1, null, …)`, not over a kept copy or a row that doesn't open) and old-trash purge after a sync, one try each; purge only for items due by the server's rule (strictly `deleted_at < server_time − trash_days`) AND seen in the trash by this browser for max(`trash_days`, 7) days AND clocks within 24 h, ≤ min(250, `max_import_items`) a request. Unresolved vault = popup warning, writes refused. "Use the server's copy" (`acceptServerCopy`, popup `manifest:accept`, the shared confirm text) is the local accept only, for every role: one fresh whole fetch; done if the vault is "ok" now; else `manifestAccept` on the served row and rows: "ok" → the memory entry is replaced by the served `{version, link}` (also downwards), "none" → forgotten and marked `unchecked` (the quiet popup note "This vault isn't being checked for missing or restored items." until a version is remembered again), each with `accepts` + 1 (entries merge by `accepts`, then version, then the stored one); nothing is sent. Offered whenever the judged copy is acceptable (`cause` "accept", or "forget" when the server shows no record: its own confirm text "Your server no longer shows a record of changes for this vault. …"; the popup sends which text was confirmed (`forget`), and a fresh answer that needs the other one accepts nothing); otherwise writers get "Open the web vault or the desktop app to review this vault." and readers "Someone who can edit this vault can review it." (the extension writes no baseline). The same accept drops that vault's item marks (`dropItemMarks`, only ids of the vault in the copy from before the fetch): kept newer copies give way to the server's rows (`servedRows`), "older version" marks go and counters become the served ones, items no longer listed are forgotten. If the copy shown changed while the acceptance was stored, the states are left as judged and a sync decides. A vault that still holds an item that can't be opened shows that notice afterwards. Device memory that can't be read (damaged, or `v` of another build): vaults with a manifest are "unchecked", vaults without one stay writable, nothing is overwritten; failed reads are counted across worker starts (`memoryUnread` in `chrome.storage.local`), and after 3 with a whole sync among them the popup shows "PassAuth can't read this device's record of your vaults." with "Reset on this device" (`resetDeviceMemory`, popup `memory:reset`): the record is moved aside (`device-unreadable:<server>|<account id>`), then one whole sync as a browser with no memory.

## Features (stage 1: server + extension)
- Item types: Login (multiple URLs, TOTP, passkeys), Secure Note, Card, Identity, SSH Key. Plus custom fields, folders, favorites, trash, password history. Attachments (below).
- Password and passphrase generator: options in `web/src/lib/generator.ts` (settings, defaults, limits, `generatorRequest()` → the core's `{mode, options}`; no imports, safe in a service worker) and the presentational `web/src/components/GeneratorOptions.tsx` (props `value` + `onChange`, styles in `styles/generator.css`), shared by the web vault and the extension. `ItemEditor` (shared with the extension) takes an optional `onChange(item)`, called with the draft after every edit (never for `initial`; callers debounce).
- **Password health** (web vault and desktop app, `/health` under Tools; iOS and Android apps, Settings → Password health; logic `web/src/lib/health.ts`, `ios/Shared/PasswordHealth.swift`, `android/.../data/PasswordHealth.kt`, each pure and unit-tested (`health.test.ts`, `PasswordHealthTests`, `PasswordHealthTest`); session state `web/src/lib/health-session.ts`, `ios/App/HealthViews.swift` `HealthSession`, `android/.../data/HealthSession.kt`). Covers the logins of every vault the user can read (trash, other item types and empty passwords left out). **Weak**: the core's `estimateBits` below `WEAK_BITS` = 50, the one threshold of the strength meter, sign-up and master-password checks, generator and health. **Reused**: groups of 2 or more logins with the identical password, compared in memory. **Exposed**, only when the user presses "Check for exposed passwords": Have I Been Pwned's k-anonymity range API — WebCrypto SHA-1 of each distinct password, only the first 5 hex characters are sent (`GET https://api.pwnedpasswords.com/range/{PREFIX}`, `Add-Padding: true`, no cookies, no Referer, `cache: no-store`), the 35-character suffixes are compared on the device and padding lines (count 0) ignored. Prefixes are deduplicated, at most 6 requests run at a time (15 s each), and a check's queued requests are skipped after its first failure. Answers, results and the report stay in memory and are dropped when the vault locks or signs out. Nothing about passwords reaches the PassAuth server; HIBP sees the requester's IP and, from a browser, the page's `Origin`. Offered only when `GET /api/config` `features` includes `breach_check` (admin setting, default on; the server's CSP then allows `connect-src https://api.pwnedpasswords.com` on the web vault's documents); otherwise the page says the server doesn't allow it. The desktop app sends the request from its WebView and follows the same flag. Failures are explained (offline, CSP block detected with `securitypolicyviolation`, timeout, HTTP status, an answer that isn't a range list, no WebCrypto on plain http). The item view shows quiet notes "Weak password", "Used in N other logins" and, after a check in this page session, "Found in a data breach". The phone apps send range requests from their own HTTP client, never with the PassAuth server's tokens or `X-PassAuth-Client` (iOS `PwnedPasswords.makeSession()`: ephemeral, no cookies, cache or credentials; Android `PwnedPasswords.client(api.http)`: no interceptors, cookie jar, cache or authenticator, 15 s `callTimeout`); results are dropped by `VaultRepository.onVaultClosed` (lock, log out, switching or adding an account); reuse is compared on UTF-8 bytes. A writable row opens the login's editor (Save and Back return to the page), else its page. iOS: `HealthView`, `PasswordNotes`; `PasswordHealth.swift` is not built into the macOS AutoFill extension; UI tests `HealthUITests` use the DEBUG-only `-PAStubBreachCheck`. Android: `ui/vault/HealthScreen.kt`, `BreachChecker` with a kotlinx `Semaphore` of 6.
- **Duplicate logins** (web vault, a section of Password health; logic `web/src/lib/duplicates.ts`, pure, vitest; the desktop app shows it from its next release because it reuses `web/src`; phones and extension: not planned).
