Found on the device after unlock; the server never learns which logins look alike. **A group** = 2 or more logins in the **same vault** (the user can write to it, it passed its checks, no waiting offline change on any of them), not in the trash, with a non-empty password, where every pair has the **same password** (exact), the **same user name** (trimmed, case-insensitive) and **shares a site**: at least one http(s) URL each whose registrable domain (core public-suffix list) is the same (`apple.com` = `signin.apple.com`; `alice.github.io` ≠ `bob.github.io`). Another user name or password = another account, never listed. A group where more than one login has attachments is not listed. **Keeper**: the login with attachments, otherwise the oldest (`created_at`). **Combined login**: the keeper's id, folder and created date; name chosen in the preview (keeper's preselected, any group member's selectable); every URL (exact duplicates once, each with its own match setting, app URLs kept); one 2FA secret: when the logins carry different ones the group is marked "Different 2FA codes" and the user must pick one in the preview (shown with its issuer/account label and current code), otherwise the one there is; every passkey (once per credential id); notes: equal or one empty → that one, else keeper's + a blank line + the others'; custom fields: union (same name and value once); password history: union by date, capped as the editor caps it; tags: union; favourite if any was. **Combining** (online only, after the preview's Combine): the keeper is saved with `updateItem` (`rev` counts up; manifest as any edit), then each other login is moved to the trash (restorable; manifest as any trash). If a later step fails the keeper already holds everything and the page says which logins are still to be trashed. A revision conflict on any step stops and reloads the group (nothing overwritten). UI (`pages/Duplicates.tsx`, `styles/duplicates.css`): rows with value chips ("Adds 2FA code", "Different 2FA codes"), "Combine…" preview with source letters per field, required 2FA choice with live codes, footer naming the logins that move to the trash; "Keep separate" hides a group on this device (`pa.duplicates-kept.<origin>|<account id>`, sorted id lists; shows again when members change). A chain where not every pair shares a site groups the oldest valid pair only.
- **Website matching** (web vault, a section of Password health after Duplicate logins; logic `web/src/lib/matching.ts`, pure, vitest; page `pages/Matching.tsx`). Lists logins whose websites carry their own match (Base domain, Host, Starts with, Exact), grouped by base domain (app addresses by address), and sets the checked groups' websites to "Default" so each app's Default URI match detection applies; Regular expression and Never are left alone (counted). Same filters as Duplicates (writable vault that passed its checks, not in the trash, no offline change waiting, not rolled back); each login saved once with `updateItem`, nothing saved if one changed since the list was built. Groups that are all Base domain start checked (Default can only narrow them); groups with Host/Starts with/Exact start unchecked and the confirm dialog says they may be offered on more sites of the domain.
- **Import** (client-side only; plaintext never reaches the server): export files from other password managers (JSON, password-protected JSON, CSV, `.1pux`, KDBX). Formats and field mappings: `docs/IMPORT_FORMATS.md`. Import requests respect `/api/config` `limits`: at most min(250, `max_import_items`) items and about 16 MiB of encrypted data each; items whose encrypted `data` would exceed `max_item_kib` are listed in the preview and left out (upper bound in `web/src/lib/sizes.ts`; only items near the limit are encrypted to measure them exactly). **Export**: encrypted PassAuth JSON plus plaintext JSON/CSV.
- SMTP: verification, invites, new-device alerts, email 2FA, recovery, admin test mail.
- Admin panel: off by default; on with `PASSAUTH_ADMIN_USERNAME` + `PASSAUTH_ADMIN_PASSWORD` (plain in `.env`, Argon2-hashed in memory at start; the example value or fewer than 12 characters keep it off and `GET /admin/login` reports `weak_password`), or the older `ADMIN_TOKEN` (used only when no password is set; may still be sent directly as a bearer token by scripts). `POST /admin/login` (10 tries/min per IP, Argon2 always runs so a wrong user name takes as long) returns a random session token kept in memory (1 h idle, 12 h max; a restart or `.env` change signs everyone out), `POST /admin/logout` ends it. Two-step login for the admin page (`crates/server/src/admin_security.rs`): set up on the admin page (`POST /admin/2fa/setup` → secret kept in memory 10 min, `/2fa/enable {code}` stores one `server_settings` row `admin_2fa` = {secret, last_step, recovery hashes} and returns 10 recovery codes once; `/2fa/disable` and `/2fa/recovery-codes` need a current code). With it on, `/admin/login` returns `{two_factor, pending}` (5 min, 5 wrong codes) and `POST /admin/login/2fa {pending, code}` gives the session; codes are used up with a compare-and-swap on the row (replay-safe), and `ADMIN_TOKEN` sent directly as a bearer is refused. The row is read on every check, so `passauth-server reset-admin-2fa` (deletes it; run with `docker compose exec`) works on a running server. `PASSAUTH_ADMIN_ALLOWED_NETWORKS` (IPs/CIDRs): every `/admin/*` request, including the login page's mode check, from an address outside them (visitor address as for rate limits: `IP_HEADER` from trusted proxies, else the TCP peer) gets 403. No shell or executable-path settings. Server settings live in `server_settings` and apply without a restart, replacing the env value they started from: sign-ups (`signups_allowed`, `signups_domains`), `signups_verify`, `icon_service`, `log_level` (a `tracing` reload handle, `crates/server/src/logging.rs`; presets error…trace, `custom` = a `LOG_LEVEL` filter left as is) and `smtp` (JSON of `SmtpConfig` incl. its password, or `off`; `GET /admin/settings` never returns the password, a save without one keeps it; the `Mailer` swaps its transport behind a lock). Limits (`crates/server/src/settings.rs`, one `server_settings` row each, applied at once, no env variable; `GET /admin/settings` returns them by name with `ranges {name: {min, max, default}}`, `PUT` takes any subset and refuses out-of-range or unknown values with 400): `trash_days` 0–3650 (0), `tombstone_days` 7–3650 (90), `session_days` 1–365 (90), `refresh_days` 1–365 (30), `remember_device_days` 1–365 (30), `signin_attempts_per_minute` 5–600 (30), `two_step_attempts_per_hour` 3–100 (10), `max_import_items` 500–50000 (10000), `max_item_kib` 64–4096 (2048; rotation uses the 4 MiB ceiling so a lowered limit never blocks a key replacement), `websocket_ping_seconds` 10–300 (30), `max_items_per_user` and `max_storage_mb_per_user` (0 = no limit), `event_days` 30–3650 (365), `item_versions_kept` 0–100 (10; 0 = off), `send_max_days` 1–365 (30), `min_kdf_memory_mib` 64–1024 (64), `min_kdf_iterations` 3–20 (3), `master_password_min_length` 10–128 (10), `max_vault_timeout_minutes` 0–43200 (0 = no limit), `max_attachment_mb` 1–4096 (100), plus switches `attachments` (on), `emergency_access` (on), `breach_check` (on), `event_ip` (on; off also erases kept addresses), `sends` (on), `require_two_step`, `require_secret_key`, `disable_export` (off); `GET /admin/settings` lists `switches {name: {default}}`, the overview counts `sends`, and `/api/config` `limits` adds `send_max_days` and `send_max_bytes`. `/api/config` has `limits {max_import_items, max_item_kib}`. Pool size: env `DATABASE_MAX_CONNECTIONS` (1–1000, default 10). Admin page → Limits and policies (`LimitsCard`, shown when `ranges` is present) groups them as Sign-in, Storage, Sync and trash, Account policies (switches `require_two_step` — asks first and counts the accounts without a method — `require_secret_key`, `disable_export`, then `master_password_min_length`, `max_vault_timeout_minutes`, `min_kdf_memory_mib`, `min_kdf_iterations`), Send (`sends`, `send_max_days`), and History and activity (`event_days`, `item_versions_kept` with 0 = no item history, and the `event_ip` switch, which asks before turning off because that erases the kept addresses), each a number input with its unit and range; values are checked locally, `PUT` sends only changed fields, and a 400 naming a setting is shown at that field; the Server settings card has "Check passwords against Have I Been Pwned" (`breach_check`). "Reset two-step login" (`POST /admin/users/{id}/two-factor/reset`) is for a user who lost every method: it deletes their `two_factor` rows (authenticator, email, recovery codes, passkey marker), `passkey_credentials`, the failure counter and "remember this device" tokens, logs a warning and emails the user. The master password (and Secret Key) still protect the account.
- Real-time sync over WebSocket (`/api/ws`): first `{"type":"hello","origin"}` (derived from the session with a server secret), then `{"type":"sync","revision","vaults":{id:seq},"origin"}`, `{"type":"logout"}`, and `{"type":"auth_request","id"}` / `{"type":"auth_request_answered","id"}` (sign-in approvals). Notices for one account within 300 ms arrive as one message with the newest values; `origin` is present only when every write in the message came from that one session (never for server changes), and `vaults` is `{}` for profile or account changes. Tabs or processes sharing one session share an origin, so clients compare the message's vault numbers with the ones they already have rather than skipping every message with their own origin. Sessions are checked every `websocket_ping_seconds` (default 30; a socket idle 3 × that + 5 s is closed). `GET /sync/revision` (`{revision, vaults: {id: seq}}`) is the cheap poll. The extension's service worker keeps a socket while unlocked (see Extension sync).
- **Delta sync** (migration v7, feature `delta_sync`): `GET /sync` answers as before plus an opaque `cursor` and each vault's `seq`. `GET /sync?since=<cursor>` answers `{revision, cursor, user, bundle, vaults, items, deleted, reset, delta: true}`: every vault header (a vault missing from it is gone for the account: drop it with its items), the items written after the cursor plus every item of the `reset` vaults, and `deleted` `[{id, vault_id}]` (deleted for good, or moved to another vault). Apply `deleted` first, then upsert `items` by id (an item moved between two readable vaults is in both lists), and replace all items of each `reset` vault. A vault is reset when the cursor doesn't know it, its key epoch changed, or the cursor's number is below `horizon` or above the vault's current number. An unreadable cursor gets the full response, never an error; everything is read in one snapshot. `trash=summary` sends trashed items without `item_key` and `data`. Cursor: base64url of `[1][len][sync generation]` then per vault `[16-byte id][LEB128 seq][LEB128 epoch]` (about 27 characters per vault, so hundreds of vaults fit a default 8 KiB request line); apps store it per account and send it back unchanged. The generation (`server_secrets.sync_generation`) is left out of `backup` copies and dropped by `passauth-server reset-sync`, so a restored database makes every app fetch everything once. Write responses add `vault_seq` (import: `vault_seqs` and `vault_seq` when one vault was written; rotate/remove: `vault_seq` too; a retried create that writes nothing returns the vault's current number) and `manifest_seq` (the vault's manifest version after the write, 0 = none). Every answer also has `server_time` and `trash_days`, each vault row `manifest`, `manifest_seq` and `manifest_ok`, and each `trash=summary` row without data its `data_sha256`. Every 10 minutes the sweeper deletes tombstones older than `tombstone_days` (7–3650, default 90) and raises the vault's `horizon` to the newest one it deleted. Web vault and desktop app (rules in `web/src/lib/delta.ts`, pure and unit-tested; state in `web/src/lib/vault.ts`) use it only when `features` has `delta_sync`. So does the extension's service worker (with `trash=summary`; see Extension sync). The encrypted rows of the last answer are kept in memory with its cursor, never persisted; `boot()`'s answer is kept while locked, so unlocking and the desktop "Never" restore send only `GET /sync?since=`; sign-out and sign-in drop it. A delta removes each `deleted` entry only from the vault it names (an item moved away and back is kept), then all items of `reset` vaults, then upserts `items` by id, then drops the items of vaults missing from `vaults`; a malformed delta, or one without a base, leads to one full fetch. Decrypted items are reused while their vault, `item_key` and `data` stay the same; a vault key is reopened, and its items decrypted again, when `wrapped_key`, `key_kind` or `key_epoch` change. Own item writes compare `vault_seq` with the known number: at most it → nothing to do; exactly one more → the write is shown as sent without a request (create, edit in place and restore from the sent ciphertext and the answer's `revision`; delete for good removes it); anything else (trash, move, other writes in between) runs a delta sync. Live sync keeps the `hello` origin but never skips by it; a `sync` message is skipped when its revision is at most the last answer's (that answer was read after the change), otherwise the client syncs if the message names a newer or unknown vault, or `vaults` is `{}`. The web vault shows the trash and never sends `trash=summary`. Only active memberships count; every response also lists `invitations` in full, and an accepted vault is new to the cursor, so it is sent whole (`reset`).
- **Quotas**: `max_items_per_user`, `max_storage_mb_per_user` (0 = none, the default): items and bytes (`item_key` + `data`, trash included) in the vaults a user owns, counted against the owner of the vault written. Storage includes item versions, Sends (name + data + wrapped key) and attachments; the item count doesn't. Attachments count against the vault owner once ready; while pending they count against the user who added it (only that user may `PUT` its bytes, others 403 `forbidden`). `max_attachment_storage_mb_per_user` (default 1024, 0 = none) limits the attachment part (403 `quota_exceeded`, "attachment storage limit reached"); in a shared vault the owner's quota is checked when the upload finishes, so a large upload into someone else's vault can be refused at the end. On PostgreSQL, `VaultWrite::commit` bumps revisions first (locking every member's user row in id order) and the quota check then locks the owners' rows (`FOR UPDATE`) before reading usage; Send create/update lock the owner's row first (SQLite serialises writers). Only growth is refused (403 `quota_exceeded`, "storage limit reached"): edits that keep or shrink usage, trash, restore, delete and rotation always pass. `GET /account` shows `usage {item_count, storage_bytes, attachment_bytes}` and `limits {max_items, max_storage_bytes, max_attachment_bytes}`; admin user rows show `item_count` and `storage_bytes`. Web vault, desktop and extension show `quota_exceeded` as "You've reached this server's storage limit. Delete items or ask the server's admin." (`api.ts` `QUOTA_EXCEEDED`), and Settings → Account shows a Storage card when a limit is set (with an Attachments meter from `usage.attachment_bytes` / `limits.max_attachment_bytes`). The admin page has "Keep this much disk space free" (`min_free_disk_mb`) under Limits, an Attachments group (the `attachments` switch, largest attachment, attachment storage per user), an "Allow emergency access" switch, and "Free disk space" on the Overview (from `disk_free_bytes`, hidden when null).
- **Activity log** (migration v8, feature `activity_log`, `crates/server/src/events.rs`): `events` rows written in the transaction of the change (a refused write leaves none), with actor, session, client app (`X-PassAuth-Client`), IP (while `event_ip` is on), vault, item, target user and `data` holding ids, counts, roles and method or setting names only — never vault content or setting values. Failed sign-ins to existing accounts are one `auth.signin_failed` row per account, reason and hour with a count, written in the background after the answer. Deleting an account erases the IPs of its own events (what it did and anonymous attempts on it; the deletion event has none); addresses of vault admins and the admin page on events about it stay. Admin invites are logged (`admin.invite_created`/`admin.invite_deleted` with `{email}`). Members who join a vault later see its full activity log and kept item versions: share a vault only with people who may see its past, or move the items into a new vault first. `GET /account/events` (own actions and actions on the account), `GET /vaults/{id}/events` (owner/admin), `GET /admin/events?type=&user=&vault=`; `?before=<cursor>&limit=1..200`, newest first, `{events, next}`. IPs show to the admin page, the actor, and the account owner for anonymous attempts on their account; never to vault admins for other members, never the admin page's to users. Kept `event_days`; the 10-minute sweeper deletes older ones. Types: `account.*`, `auth.*` (incl. `auth.request_created` / `auth.request_approved` / `auth.request_denied`), `session.*` (incl. `session.device_forgotten`), `vault.*`, `member.*`, `item.*`, `trash.emptied`, `attachment.*`, `emergency.*` (actor one side, target the other), `send.*` (`send.accessed` is one row per Send per hour, with no address or app), `admin.*`. Vault members below admin get 403 from the vault's events, non-members 404. Web vault and desktop app (`web/src/lib/activity.ts`, pure, vitest): `describeEvent` gives a sentence, an optional detail and a warning tone (failed sign-ins, paused two-step login, sessions signed out after token reuse, admin resets of the reader's account) for every type; unknown types fall back to their category; vault and item names are looked up on the device, else "a vault you can't open" / "an item"; a move's two events show once (`collapseMoves`). `pages/Activity.tsx` groups by day with time, app (`lib/clients.ts` `clientInfo`, shared with Signed-in devices) and IP when present. Places: Settings → Activity, Manage and share → Activity (owners and admins), admin page → Activity (filters by type or category, person, vault id; Users → Manage → Show activity).
- **Item history** (migration v8, feature `item_history`, `crates/server/src/vault/history.rs`): before an edit or trash, the item's stored row (encrypted key + data) becomes a version unless identical to the newest; `seq` = the vault write number of that row, `saved_at`/`saved_by` from the row (`items.updated_by`). The newest `item_versions_kept` per item are kept and listed (`GET /items/{id}/versions`, `/versions/{seq}`, any member); restoring is an ordinary `PUT`. Versions are encrypted with the vault key of their time, so a key replacement deletes the vault's versions and a move to another vault deletes the item's; deleting an item for good, its vault or the trash sweeper deletes them too. Lowering the setting trims at once. Web vault and desktop app (`web/src/lib/history.ts`, pure, vitest; `vault.ts` `listItemVersions`/`openItemVersion`/`restoreItemVersion`): the item view lists "Earlier versions (n)" ("Saved <time> by <you|name|email>"), noting that history starts over after a key replacement or a move; a version opens read-only (decrypted with the vault's current key) with "Restore this version" for writers — an ordinary `updateItem` with the version's content, the item's creation time and both password histories merged (newest first, each once, at most 20); the sheet warns when the version lacks passkeys the item has now; restoring a version lists the files of both versions (`mergeAttachments`), so no file is orphaned.
- **Attachments** (migration v10, feature `attachments` while the admin switch `attachments` is on and the server has a folder, `crates/server/src/vault/attachments.rs`, `files.rs`): the app encrypts each file with its own random key and lists `{id, name, size, key}` inside the encrypted item; the server keeps a row (item, vault, encrypted size, `pending`/`ready`, creator) and the ciphertext at `<folder>/<2 chars>/<id>` (0600; written to `tmp/`, fsynced, renamed). `POST /items/{id}/attachments {id, size}` → pending (write access, size ≤ `max_attachment_mb`, counts toward the vault owner's storage at once; an identical retry returns the same row); `PUT /attachments/{id}` streams exactly `size` bytes (413 the moment it runs over; never buffered) → ready; `GET /attachments/{id}` for any active member (octet-stream, `Content-Disposition: attachment`, no-store, `CSP default-src 'none'; sandbox`); `GET /items/{id}/attachments` lists them (pending included); `DELETE /attachments/{id}` with write access. At most 4 uploads per user (counted atomically under one mutex) and 32 per server at once; an upload is dropped when idle for 60 s or not complete within 60 s + size ÷ 32 KiB/s (a client sending ≥ 32 KiB/s can still hold a slot that long). Only the attachment's creator may `PUT` its bytes (403 `forbidden`). All are VaultWrites; becoming ready or being deleted stamps the item's `seq` (delta sync re-sends it unchanged). Trash keeps files; deleting the item for good, its vault or owner, and the trash sweeper delete them; a move takes the rows along (files untouched, new owner charged). Key replacement re-encrypts items, so file keys are re-wrapped, but files are not re-encrypted: a removed member who kept a file key and its ciphertext can still read that file (the same trade-off as other managers). Restoring an item version may list files deleted since; apps show them as missing. The sweeper deletes uploads untouched for an hour, files without a row and stale temp files. The switch off stops new uploads; downloads and deletes continue. The server learns each file's approximate size and time. Client flow: encrypt with a new key → `POST` the attachment → `PUT` the ciphertext → save the item listing it (if the save fails for good, `DELETE` it; to remove one, save the item without it first, then `DELETE`; a 409 `already_uploaded` on retry counts as success). Web vault (`lib/attachments.ts` pure, `lib/transfer.ts` steps, `lib/files.ts`, `components/Attachments.tsx`, tests for both): the list is `[{id, name, size, key}]` with `size` the file's own size and ids bound into the associated data exactly as written (lowercase UUIDs from `newId`; iOS must not use `uuidString` as is). A file is checked with `fileEncryptedSize(size) ≤ max_attachment_mb` MiB; upload = `POST`, then `PUT` as a request stream (`duplex: "half"`, 1 MiB at a time) where the browser allows it (Chrome needs HTTP/2), else a Blob built piece by piece with a new key and XHR progress; a 401 renews the session once; a failure or cancel deletes the attachment; the item is then saved with the file added to the list of the item as last synced (up to 3 tries), or the attachment is deleted. Uploads run one at a time, stop on lock or sign-out, and the page asks before closing while one runs. Download streams through `FileDecryptor` into a Blob released only after `finish()`; a 404 shows "This file is no longer on the server". The item view marks missing files and offers deleting finished uploads no item lists. Shown and saved names are cleaned (security review F4): Unicode Cc/Cf removed (bidi overrides and isolates, LRM/RLM, BOM, zero-width characters), whitespace runs folded, no trailing dots or spaces; saving also replaces `\/:*?"<>|` with `_`, drops leading dots, prefixes Windows device names (CON, PRN, AUX, NUL, COM0–9, LPT0–9, ¹²³, any extension) with `_`, and keeps at most 200 characters with the extension (`stripInvisible`, `cleanFileName`, `downloadName`; the desktop app cleans again natively). The editor never changes the list (an overwrite keeps the synced list); restoring a version lists both versions' files; exports leave the list out (no file keys) and say so; imports drop it. Extension popup: the item page lists files (marking those the server no longer has) and downloads one in the popup with a token the worker lends (`files:access`, renewed after a 401), decrypting with the popup's core (released after `finish()`) and saving via an object URL and `<a download>` (no downloads permission); no uploads from the popup; saves keep the list as last synced. The desktop app adds and downloads files like the web vault, through its native client (Desktop app → File transfers); `files.ts` applies the same rules on both paths (`putNative` / `getNative`, tests in `files.test.ts`).
- **Emergency access** (migration v10, keys v2 in migration 13, features `emergency_access` and `emergency_vault_keys`, `crates/server/src/emergency.rs`): view-only access to a grantor's personal vaults (owned, account-key wrapped, nobody else a member or invited) for a trusted contact on the same server after `wait_days` (1–90). invited → accepted → confirmed (`POST /{id}/confirm {sealed_key, grantor_key, vault_keys: [{vault_id, wrapped_key, key_epoch}]}`: `vault_keys` must name exactly the grantor's personal vaults, else 409 `emergency_vaults_changed` with `expected` (the ids to wrap); every wrap needs the `key_epoch` of the key it wraps (400 without), and an epoch the vault has left is 409 `vault_key_changed`; a body without `grantor_key`/`vault_keys`, from apps before v2, is 400 `update_required`; the grantor's personal vaults are locked first, before the access's row, so none is shared or re-keyed under it) → requested (grantor emailed) → approved (by the grantor with a reauth token, `x-reauth-token`, else 403 `reauth_required`; or by the sweeper once the period ends; reject ends a request or an approved access, and the contact may ask again). Shortening `wait_days` needs a reauth token too (raising it or keeping it doesn't). Either side may delete it. The grantor's `GET /emergency-access` entries add `key_version` (2), `grantor_key`, `reconfirm`, `v1_exposed` and `missing_vaults` (personal vaults without a wrap; for an accepted access, the vaults to wrap), and the answer adds the user's sealed `roster` (null until stored) and `roster_seq`; `PUT /emergency-access/roster {roster, seq}` stores it by compare-and-swap (`seq` = stored + 1, else 409 `roster_changed`; opaque base64url up to 32 KiB, 120 per hour, also while the feature is off) in `users.emergency_roster`/`emergency_roster_seq`. `PUT /{id}/vault-keys {vault_keys}` (grantor, confirmed or later, 120 per hour) only adds wraps for personal vaults that have none (a wrap is never replaced; a non-personal vault is 409 `emergency_vaults_changed`). Wraps (`emergency_vault_keys (access_id, vault_id, wrapped_key, created_at)`) go when the vault is deleted, shared (anyone invited) or re-keyed (`rotate`, remove + rotate: the same transaction), and with the access. Sharing a vault that had wraps also sets its `rotation_pending = 1` and answers `rotation_pending: true`: a contact may already hold its key (an approved access, or a server handing the key out early) and the other members never chose to trust them, so the sharing app replaces the key at once (sealed to everyone, the new invitee too). Ending an access that held a key for good (`DELETE` by either side, deleting either account) deletes its wraps, sets `vaults.rotation_pending = 1` on every vault that had one and bumps the grantor's sync revision (vault locks first, then the access; a removal that finds a wrap added meanwhile starts again, up to 4 times; account deletion locks such a vault late and still tells its owner); only the grantor's `DELETE` answer lists those vaults (`rotation_pending`). Ending an approved access while keeping the contact (reject) changes no key. Migration 13 sent every access that held a v1 sealed account key back to `accepted` without it, `reconfirm = 1`, `v1_exposed = 1`, with an `emergency.reconfirm` event (no actor) for each side; on SQLite it ran with `secure_delete` on and set `server_settings.scrub_pending`, so each start runs `VACUUM` (its own connection, outside any transaction: no free pages left, also from accesses removed before) and a `wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)` (tried again for 2 s while a reader holds the log, else a warning) until that succeeds, then clears the flag; with less free disk than twice the database (log included: `VACUUM` builds a temporary copy and writes through the log) plus `min_free_disk_mb`, or when `VACUUM` fails, it warns and waits for the next start, never failing it. So the cleared keys stay neither in the file nor in the log; PostgreSQL keeps old row versions until its `VACUUM`, and backups made before the upgrade still hold them. Approved contacts get `key_version`, `sealed_key`, the still-personal vaults that have a wrap for the access (`wrapped_key` = that wrap), their names and all items (`GET /{id}/vault`), and their attachments (`/{id}/attachments/{attachment}`); never the bundle, account key, private key, shared vaults, Sends or sessions. Limits per contact across all their accesses, counted after the grant is found: 30 views and 600 file downloads per hour. Limits, stated honestly: the waiting period is server policy, not cryptography, and an approved contact keeps what they viewed; key replacement only protects what the grantor adds afterwards. A stolen session alone can't complete the setup (confirming needs the unlocked account key) nor let a contact in sooner (approving and shortening the wait need the master password). Events `emergency.confirmed` (`vaults`), `emergency.vaults_added` (grantor only), `emergency.removed` (`rotate_vaults`). Emails link to `/settings/emergency`. Web vault and desktop app (`lib/emergency.ts` pure + tests, `emergency-client.ts`, `pages/Emergency.tsx`): Settings → Emergency access explains the feature and its limits; as grantor: add a contact (email, 1–90 days); confirm after checking their key fingerprint (`newEmergencyKey` → the access into the roster first, with the contact's key and the `grantor_key` hash, one retry on `roster_changed` → `sealFor` and `wrapVaultKey` for each vault the server lists that the app's own data also sees as personal: role owner, key kind account, members list = the account alone; anything else stops the confirmation; one refresh and retry on a 409); change the waiting period (the master password when shortening); approve (the master password) or reject a request; end an approved access; remove (out of the roster first, then `DELETE`, then the keys of the vaults the server names that the app sees as personal are replaced; the confirmation says so, including that earlier item versions go). Contacts with `v1_exposed` carry a lasting warning that they may keep the account key, also in their confirm and remove dialogs. After unlock, a change to the vault list or a key epoch, a new vault and any key replacement, `fillEmergencyKeys` (the vault's `onVaultKeysChange` hook) opens the roster, drops the accesses the server no longer lists, and adds the `missing_vaults` wraps only for accesses whose id, contact key and `grantor_key` hash it lists, with the same personal-vault check. A confirmation whose answer got lost (the access already holds a key when retried) points the roster at that key with the contact key checked in that flow instead of making a new one. A roster older than the highest version this device opened (`pa.roster.<user id>`), or one that doesn't open, stops the fill and shows a vault notice ("Your server sent an older list of emergency contacts, so PassAuth didn't change their access. Ask your server's admin."); no roster yet is an empty one. As contact: accept or decline, ask for access (the waiting time is shown), view once approved, step down (never replaces anyone's keys). `/emergency/<id>` opens an approved access read-only: `openSealedEmergencyKey(grantor.id, id, sealed_key)` → `EmergencyKey.openVaultKey(vault_id, wrapped_key)` → `decryptItem`, every handle freed at once; items are searchable with copy buttons and downloads via `/emergency-access/{id}/attachments/{attachment}`, in memory only. The apps need the server's `emergency_vault_keys` feature (an older server would take the sealed key as an account key) and say so otherwise. Phone apps have no emergency screens.
- **Approve a sign-in from another device** (migration v11, feature `login_with_device` while the admin switch `login_with_device` is on, the default; `crates/server/src/auth_requests.rs`): a new device that knows the email sends `POST /auth/requests {email, public_key, access_code, device_id, device_name, device_kind}` (no session; `public_key` an X25519 key made for this request, low-order keys refused; `access_code` 22–128 base64url characters it keeps; only SHA-256 of the code and a keyed hash of the email are stored; a `remember_token` sent by apps for servers before 0.16.1 is ignored and not stored) and gets `{id, fingerprint_phrase, expires_at}` (15 minutes). Every email gets the same answer after at least 100 ms under the same limits (10 per 10 minutes per address, IPv6 by /48; 3 per 15 minutes per email and address; 1000 per 10 minutes per server; 429); unknown, disabled and (while verification is required) unverified emails, and an account's requests past 5 per 15 minutes, are stored with no account: nobody sees them and they expire. The account's online apps get `{"type":"auth_request","id"}` and list `GET /account/auth-requests` → `{requests:[{id, public_key, fingerprint_phrase, challenge, device_name, device_kind, client, ip, created_at, expires_at}]}` (apps also re-list on connect and unlock). Phrase: `d = SHA-256("passauth/login-with-device/phrase/v1" ‖ key)`, word i (0..4) = EFF large wordlist line `u32_be(d[4i..4i+4]) mod 7776` (0-based, `crates/core/src/eff_large_wordlist.txt`), 5 words joined by spaces (vectors in the server tests). Each app shows only phrases it derives from the key it holds; showing the server's would let a server that swaps the key pass the check. The approving app asks which words the new device shows, offering `loginPhraseChoices(public_key, 3)`: the key's own phrase among 2 decoys of random keys that share no word with it at any position, derived from the request key so a request seen twice (after "Not now", or on two devices) shows the same list in the same order (decoy candidate i = the phrase of SHA-256(`passauth/login-with-device/decoy/v1` ‖ key ‖ u32_be(i)), skipping candidates that share a word position with the real phrase or repeat a decoy; the real phrase's position = u64_be(SHA-256(`passauth/login-with-device/decoy-position/v1` ‖ key)[0..8]) mod count; vectors section `loginPhraseChoices`; the decoys aren't secret, picking one is always refused) (security review F1/F6, "number matching"), plus "None of these" (denies) and "Not now" (focused first); the choices accept a press only 1 s after the sheet appeared and again after the app or window comes back. The device name and app are shown as claims ("Device (as it calls itself)", "App"): the server stores them as plain text of at most 64 characters without control or format characters, and the apps clean them again (one line, no Cc/Cf, at most 64). To approve, an unlocked app calls `approveLoginRequest(public_key, id, picked phrase, Secret Key?)` (refused with `phrase_mismatch` unless the picked words are that key's: the app then denies the request and says "Those aren't the words for this request, so it was denied.") and opens `challenge` (a server value sealed to the account's own X25519 key, AAD `passauth/login-with-device/challenge/v1:<id>`): `POST /account/auth-requests/{id} {approve:true, sealed_key, proof}` or `{approve:false}` → `{id, status}`; the app then shows the 6-digit confirmation code ("Enter this code on the new device", "123 456") until Done. The server seals `challenge` anew for every listing (fresh ephemeral key, same plaintext), so apps compare later listings by id and public key only and prove with the challenge frozen with the sheet. A stolen session can't approve (403). The first answer wins (409 `conflict`, 400 `expired`, 404 for other accounts; the same answer again from the same session is a retry), and every socket gets `{"type":"auth_request_answered","id"}`. The new device polls `GET /auth/requests/{id}` with `X-PassAuth-Access-Code` (never in the URL; 404 for a wrong or missing code; 120 a minute per address): `{status: pending|denied|expired|used}`. An approved request is exchanged once, always directly: `{status:"approved", sealed_key, …the password sign-in's session answer (remember_token null), bundle}`. Since server 0.16.1 the approval by the account's own signed-in, unlocked app is the second step, so an account with two-step login is never sent `two_factor_required` here (servers before 0.16.1 asked for two-step login unless the device was remembered; users confused its 6-digit code with the confirmation code). `require_two_step` still applies: approving apps need a method, and the new session is held to the policy like any other. These are normal sessions (device row, app, new-device email, policies); `auth.signed_in` adds `method:"device"` and `request`, and `two_step:"device_approval"` when the account has two-step login on. A new master password, "sign out other sessions", the admin revoking the sessions or disabling the account end open requests (and password sign-ins waiting for two-step login); account deletion removes them; every sign-in first writes the account row and refuses a disabled account. The sweeper marks requests expired (dropping a sealed key never taken, the address, and the remember-token hash older servers kept) and deletes them a day after they expire. The Secret Key isn't needed (the device receives the derived account key). Limits, stated honestly: with 3 choices a blind tap still succeeds 1 time in 3; an owner who approves a request they didn't start still has to give away the 6-digit confirmation code shown only on their device before the new device can use the key; two-step login no longer applies to device sign-ins, because the approving device is the second step; a server can withhold or relay requests, so the new device shows which account it got. The confirmation code stops a server that skips the real apps and seals an Account Key of its own with a bundle to match (security review M1): the new device unlocks nothing until the user types the code the real approving app showed. Core (`crates/core/src/device_login.rs`, WASM + UniFFI): the new device makes a `LoginRequestKey` (X25519, one per request, never exported), sends `publicKey()` and shows `phrase()`; the approving app offers `loginPhraseChoices(request.public_key, 3)` computed from the key it holds (never the server's `fingerprint_phrase`), sends `{sealed, code} = account.approveLoginRequest(public_key, id, picked, secretKey?)` as `sealed_key` with `proof = account.openLoginChallenge(challenge, id)`, and shows `code`; the new device calls `open(sealed_key, user_id, id)`, then `confirm(code)` with the code the user types (digits, spaces or hyphens allowed, compared in constant time; malformed input doesn't count; the third wrong code ends it for good with `too_many_wrong_codes` and wipes the keys), and only then do `.unlock(bundle)` (an unlocked `Account` without the master password) and `secretKey()` (`PA1-…` when the approving app included it, so the device can later unlock with the master password) work (`not_confirmed` before). Code: HKDF-SHA256 of the Account Key (no salt, info `passauth/login-with-device/confirm/v1`) keys an HMAC-SHA256 over u64be(len) ‖ account id ‖ u64be(len) ‖ request id ‖ the 32 raw request-key bytes; the first 8 bytes as a big-endian number mod 10^6 give 6 digits (known answer "598354" in the core tests, computed independently). Sealed key: the core sealed box with associated data `passauth/login-with-device/key/v1` ‖ len‖account id ‖ len‖request id (8-byte big-endian lengths); plaintext = Account Key ‖ optional 16-byte Secret Key; low-order keys refused. The challenge opens only with `passauth/login-with-device/challenge/v1:<request id>`, so it can't be used to decrypt the account's other boxes. The request key can't be saved, so a request dies with the process that made it (the extension keeps it in the popup, not the worker). Web vault and desktop app (`web/src/lib/device-login.ts`, pure and unit-tested; wiring in `device-login-client.ts`; screens in `pages/Login.tsx` and `components/DeviceSignIn.tsx`): the sign-in page offers "Sign in with another device" next to the master-password sign-in; it sends the request with a 256-bit access code (43 base64url characters), the app's device id, name and kind, and this app's remember token for the email, shows its key's `phrase()` with a countdown, and polls every 3 s, 5 s after 2 minutes and 10 s after 5 minutes (failures back off from 6 to 30 s, never shorter than `Retry-After`; stops on denied/expired/used, 404, cancel, leaving the page, or 16 minutes after the request). An approved session is held in memory (`vault.ts` `openDeviceApproval`, nothing stored) until the user types the code (`confirmDeviceLogin`; `Login.tsx` `DeviceCodePanel`: "Enter the 6-digit code shown on your other device", "That code doesn't match. N tries left."; anything but 6 digits is refused before it counts; the third wrong code, cancel or leaving the page signs the session out and drops everything); only then are tokens, remember token and email stored, and a received Secret Key kept only for the typed email when no different one is kept (`secretKeyToKeep`). A sealed key that doesn't open, or a session that arrives after cancel, signs the new session out again and keeps nothing. With two-step login (servers before 0.16.1 only; newer ones answer with the session directly), the request key waits in the same pending slot as a password sign-in's master keys and the normal two-step screens finish it; the page then shows "Signed in as <email>" before the vault opens. Approving apps list requests on each live-sync connection, on `auth_request`, after unlock, in Settings → Sessions, and every minute while one waits; the decision sheet shows one request at a time, frozen when it came up (`settleQueue`/`freezeRequest` in `device-login.ts`; choices and approval use only that copy, newer requests wait, and none comes up while a note shows); a listing that gives the shown id another `public_key` closes it with "This request changed, so it was closed. If you're signing in on a new device, start again there." and puts it off (a new challenge text never does); a shown request that leaves the list closes as expired (past `expires_at` locally) or changed, quietly if this app answered it. It asks "Which words does the new device show?" with the 3 choices, "None of these" (deny) and "Not now"; a pick sends `approval()` (`approveLoginRequest` with the saved Secret Key if the account uses one, plus `openLoginChallenge` of the frozen challenge; if the frozen copy won't work with this account the request is put off with the usual error, `CantApproveError`); the code note stays until Done (Escape doesn't close it); an answer from another window or device closes the sheet with a note. Admin switch: "Allow approving sign-ins from another device" under Account policies. iOS and Android (`Shared/DeviceLogin.swift`, `Shared/DeviceLoginRepository.swift`, `App/AuthRequestViews.swift`, `AuthViews.DeviceLoginView`; Android `data/DeviceLogin.kt`, `data/AuthRequestInbox.kt`, `ui/AuthRequestSheet.kt`, `AuthScreens.DeviceLoginScreen`): the same flow — the new device polls with delays 2,2,2,3,3,4,5,6,8 then 10 s (Retry-After on 429; never past 15 minutes from the request plus 1 minute grace), continues two-step login in `verifyTwoFactor` with the key in memory only (servers before 0.16.1 only), then asks for the code and stores nothing before `confirm` (details in the iOS and Android sections); the approving app's `AuthRequestInbox` lists requests on unlock and foreground, when live sync connects and on `auth_request`, drops them on `auth_request_answered`, and shows the same frozen sheet with 3 choices and the code; AutoFill extensions and Android services do nothing. Extension: the worker lists the active account's requests while unlocked (unlock/sign-in, every live-sync reconnect, `auth_request`, popup open at most every 30 s, after the first expiry) into session storage `signInRequests:<account>`, dropped with the keys; requests answered meanwhile are never brought back by an in-flight listing; while one waits, the toolbar badge is a dot in the warning colour and its title says why — nothing opens. The popup's queue follows the web vault's `settleQueue` (wording, notes and the 1 s re-arm on focus or visibility are the web vault's): the request is frozen when its sheet opens (id, key, challenge, claims, and the 3 choices worked out in the worker) and compared to later listings by id and public key only (`keepFrozenChallenge` before `settleQueue`); the worker refuses an approval whose key differs from its list (`request_changed`), runs `approveLoginRequest` (`phrase_mismatch` denies) and returns the code, which the sheet shows until Done (pressable after 1 s). New device: "Sign in with another device" moves to PassAuth's own window (`popup.html?popout=1&device=1&email=`) and runs `runDeviceLogin` with the popup's core (the key never leaves that page); the worker carries create, poll and (servers before 0.16.1 only) two-step requests pinned to the account; after `open` the window asks for the code (`confirm`, 3 tries; the third wrong one signs the session out and frees the approval), then `finishDeviceSignIn` stores tokens, session keys and remember token like a password sign-in (not a master-password entry for passkey user verification) and keeps a received Secret Key only for the typed email, never over a different one.
- **Push notifications for sign-in requests** (migration 18, feature `push` while the admin switch `push_enabled` is on, the default; `crates/server/src/push.rs`): phones with a registered push token are woken when a sign-in request reaches their account; see "Push notifications" below.
- **Send** (migration v9, feature `sends` while the admin switch `sends` is on, `crates/server/src/sends.rs`): text shared through `/send/<id>#<key>`. The app encrypts the name and text with a random 256-bit send key that exists only in the link's fragment, and keeps it wrapped by the account key (`owner_key`). The server picks the 128-bit id and stores ciphertext only (name 8 KiB, data 1 MiB, wrapped key 4 KiB); `delete_at` at most `send_max_days` ahead; optional `expires_at`, `max_views`, `disabled`, `hide_email`. Owner: `/sends` (list, create; 100 creates per hour) and `/sends/{id}` (get, change, delete). Migration 16 (`send_v2`): `sends.owner_content_key` (nullable), accepted on create (only with a password) and change (`null` clears it), returned in the owner's list and get, never on the public endpoints, counted in the Send's size; while a Send has one, a new password or none needs `name`, `data` and `owner_content_key` in the same request (400 otherwise, so an app from before can't leave the text under a password visitors no longer have); removing the password clears it. The server also keeps the text's format in step with the key (`check_format`, by the fields' first byte: `0x02` = v2): `name` and `data` are v2 exactly when the Send has an `owner_content_key`, checked on create and on every change of name, text or key, so an app from before can't rewrite a v2 Send's text with the link key alone and the key can't be dropped from under text that needs it; and a v2 Send can't go back to v1 while it keeps a password (400). Public: `GET /api/public/sends/{id}` (page info, no text; the name is withheld while a password is needed) and `POST …/access` (the text, counting one view in the same statement that checks a view is left, so `max_views` holds under concurrency). Unknown, disabled, expired, used-up, deleted, Send off or owner disabled all give the same 404. Optional password: the visitor's app derives a verifier with Argon2id over `access_salt` (opaque to the server, may carry the KDF parameters); the server keeps the verifier's SHA-256 and compares in constant time; a wrong one gives 401 `wrong_password` and counts no view. Rate limits: 60 public requests per minute per address, 10 opens per minute per address and Send (counted only after the Send is found, so unknown ids create no limiter keys), 100 wrong passwords per hour per Send (counted in the Send's row, see Rate limits). `/send/` documents are the web vault with the usual CSP plus `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` and `X-Robots-Tag: noindex, nofollow`. The sweeper deletes Sends past `delete_at`. Apps show the decrypted text as plain text, never HTML. Web vault and desktop app (`web/src/lib/sends.ts` pure + `sends.test.ts`; `send-client.ts`): Tools → Send (`/sends`) lists the account's Sends (name decrypted with the unwrapped send key, views/limit, expiry, deletion date, password, state), creates and edits text Sends (name up to 200 characters, text up to `send_max_bytes` of ciphertext, optional password, view limit, expiry, deletion within `send_max_days`, hide email; `PUT` sends only what changed), Copy link (`<origin>/send/<id>#<key>`), turn the link off and on, Delete. The public page `/send/<id>` (`pages/SendView.tsx`; `main.tsx` doesn't `boot()` there, so no session is used) reads the key from `location.hash` (also on `hashchange`), calls only the two public endpoints without credentials (`{verifier}` from `sendPasswordKeys(password, access_salt, link key)` when needed; its content key stays on the page), shows name and text as plain text with Copy, explains 404, 401 and 429, and needs a click to open (each opening counts as a view). It loads nothing from other origins, and the fragment never reaches a request or a Referer. Once read, the key is removed from the address (`history.replaceState`) and lives only in memory (a reload needs the link again; the browser's own history list may still hold the visited URL). The password keys run in a module Web Worker (`lib/send-kdf.worker.ts`, falling back to the page thread) with a progress bar; its WASM memory is freed when the worker ends (the CSP's `script-src 'self' 'wasm-unsafe-eval'` covers it: keep `'wasm-unsafe-eval'` on `/assets/*.js` and never add a `worker-src` without `'self'`). "Send created" masks the key part of the link, with Show and a Copy button that copies the full link.
- **Policies** (feature `policies`, `/api/config` `policies {require_two_step, require_secret_key, min_kdf_memory_mib, min_kdf_iterations, master_password_min_length, max_vault_timeout_minutes, disable_export}`, `crates/server/src/policies.rs`): `require_two_step` → 403 `two_step_required` for an account without a method except `GET /account`, `POST /auth/logout`, setting up a method (TOTP, email, security key) and the routes that need no session; the live-sync socket too; the last method can't be removed. `require_secret_key` → new account keys without a Secret Key get 400 `secret_key_required` (the server trusts the bundle's plaintext flag). `min_kdf_memory_mib` / `min_kdf_iterations` only raise the core's floors; weaker new keys get 400 `kdf_too_weak`; apps create new keys with memory = max(128 MiB, minimum) and passes = max(3, minimum). Sign-in is never refused: `GET /account` lists `policy_violations` (`two_step`, `secret_key`, `kdf`) for the apps to prompt (a `kdf` fix is a keys change with new parameters). Advisory, applied by the apps: `master_password_min_length`, `max_vault_timeout_minutes`, `disable_export`. The fake prelogin for unknown emails follows the KDF and Secret Key policies so it doesn't stand out. Web vault and desktop (`web/src/lib/policies.ts`, pure, `policies.test.ts`; `usePolicies()` in `api.ts`): any 403 `two_step_required` (`api()` calls `onTwoStepRequired` listeners before throwing; `twoStepGate: false` when turning off a method) sets vault status `two-step`: `pages/TwoStepRequired.tsx` offers only the Settings two-step cards and Sign out; the keys stay in memory when it came up at sign-in, unlock or while open (idle lock and desktop timeout keep running, live sync stops), so `continueAfterTwoStep()` syncs and opens the vault after the recovery codes. `policy_violations` shows a dismissible banner above the item list (`secret_key` → Settings → Security; `kdf` → Settings → Security, "Change master password" (the same password is fine): registration and every keys change read `/api/config` again and call `createAccountWithKdf` / `rewrapWithKdf` with the policy's minimums (`vault.ts` `kdfMinimums`), so new keys always meet the policy). New master passwords need max(10, `master_password_min_length`) characters; with `require_secret_key` registration forces the Secret Key, a password change turns one on and it can't be turned off; the last two-step method can't be turned off while two-step login is required. `max_vault_timeout_minutes` shortens the web vault's 15-minute idle lock; the desktop app offers only choices within it plus the limit itself (incl. On system lock, On app restart, Never, which then keeps no keys), says so in Settings, and keeps the last limit per server (`desktop.timeoutLimit`) for offline starts. `disable_export` hides Export and `/export` says the admin turned it off. The extension applies `max_vault_timeout_minutes` and shows `two_step_required` and `policy_violations` (see Extension sync). iOS and Android: registration (`createAccountWithKdf`) and every keys change (`rewrapWithKdf`) use `/api/config` read again (falling back to the stored policies; no minimums without the feature); the `kdf` notice always leads to Change master password, which accepts the same password while the violation is open; `kdf_too_weak` reads "Your server's encryption settings have changed. Try again.". On iOS, keys above 128 MiB never fit the AutoFill extension's memory limit, so it offers Face ID / Touch ID or the app instead of the master password (`masterPasswordFits`).
- **Breach check** (server side): the web vault asks Have I Been Pwned's range API from the browser (first 5 characters of the SHA-1, k-anonymity); the server never proxies it or sees hashes. Admin `breach_check` (default on): on adds feature `breach_check` and `connect-src 'self' https://api.pwnedpasswords.com` to the web vault's CSP (built per request on the HTML documents); off removes both.
- Profile pictures (migration 15, feature `avatar`): the app re-encodes the chosen image as a 256×256 JPEG (≤ 256 KiB, no EXIF/GPS), seals it with the Account Key (core `seal_avatar`/`open_avatar`, AAD `passauth/avatar/v1/<account id>`) and stores it with `PUT /api/account/avatar` (`DELETE` removes it, `GET` returns `{avatar, avatar_seq}`; the route has its own body limit, about 350 KB, since a sealed picture is larger than the general 64 KiB; `open_avatar` refuses a picture that opens to more than 256 KiB or to nothing). The server keeps only the ciphertext (`users.avatar`) and a version (`users.avatar_seq`, in `/sync` as `user.avatar_seq`, bumps the revision + live sync). Apps fetch it when the version changes, cache the opened picture per account on the device (shown on locked screens and account switchers too; the extension keeps only the sealed picture in `storage.local` and the opened one in `storage.session`, so locked accounts show initials there) and delete it with the account; no picture = initials of the profile name (else the email). Other members of shared vaults don't see it. The web vault reads the chosen file and shows the opened picture through `blob:` URLs, so the server's CSP has `img-src 'self' data: blob:`.
- Website icons: `GET /api/icons/{host}` (unauthenticated, used as `<img src>`; clients fall back to a letter avatar when an icon fails to load). The server fetches the site's `<link rel=icon>` or `/favicon.ico` defensively: public DNS names only, every resolved IP must be public and the connection is pinned to it, redirects re-validated (max 3), ports 80/443, 5 s budget, 100 KiB cap, only PNG/ICO/GIF/JPEG/WebP by magic bytes (never SVG/HTML), `CSP: default-src 'none'`. Hits cached 7 days, misses 1 day (`ICON_CACHE_DIR`, default `icons/` beside the SQLite file). Uncached fetches are rate-limited per client IP (60/min) and capped at 8 concurrent. Turned off on the admin page (or `ICON_SERVICE=off` as the starting value). `/api/config` lists the feature `icons` while the service is on (read on each request, so the switch applies at once); apps without it (or with website icons turned off) show a globe glyph for every login and don't request `/api/icons/{host}` (web `site-icons.ts` `siteIconsShown`, extension `api.ts` `siteIconsShown` for popup rows, item page, inline menu and passkey window, iOS/Android `SiteIcons.shown(wanted, api, features)`: icons when the user wants them and the server has no `api` field or lists `icons`), which answers 404 with `Cache-Control: no-store` while the service is off. Privacy: the server learns which sites' icons are requested (not which user, not vault contents); turn it off if that matters.
- Extension: login to any PassAuth server URL; autofill only on user action, into the frame that asked (inline menu) or the top frame, else a same-site iframe that claims the fill — the fill offer carries only a token, and the background checks each claiming frame (`frameMayFill`) and sends the secrets to that frame's `documentId`; every fill command carries the web origin the worker checked for that document and the content script fills only while it still has exactly that origin (`content/fill.ts` `atOrigin`; content scripts have no site rules of their own); inline save prompts; TOTP codes; acts as a WebAuthn **passkey provider** (ES256 keys stored inside the encrypted item, with fallback to browser or hardware keys).
- Extension hardening (2026-11-15 review round 1):
  - Senders are classified by `sender.origin` (`background/senders.ts`); `"null"` or an origin that doesn't match the URL is refused. Content scripts use `self.origin`.
  - The inline menu and save prompt hosts are plain `div`s with `!important` host styles. Their nonce reaches the frame by `postMessage` after load (addressed to the extension origins only, `shared/dom/frame-channel.ts`) and is bound to the first frame and document that use it (`ownsNonce`); fill, close and openPopup use it up. `use_dynamic_url` is on (content scripts' `chrome.runtime.getURL` gives the session's dynamic id; Chrome loads it and commits the frame at the extension's own origin, so `frameOrigins` posts to both); the menu frame has one fixed height (220 px) and the host carries no metadata, so a page can't read the lock state or match count.
  - `content/guard.ts`: `tampered()` compares each frame's position with where PassAuth placed it and checks transform/translate/scale/rotate/zoom/margin/pointer-events; `clickGate` counts a click only after 500 ms of pointer dwell inside the frame (restarted when it moves or resizes, and when a pointer event arrives at the same screen point as the last one but elsewhere in the frame — the page moved the frame under a resting pointer, e.g. with a `translate` on `<body>`, which the 250 ms tamper check catches only afterwards; found by a real-browser test that filled a row this way). Touch and pen (round 5): Chrome sends `pointerleave` before a tap's `click`, so taps need a confirm tap — a second tap 0.5–5 s after the first, the frame at the same screen offset (±3 px), no move, resize or loss of visibility in between (`confirmsTap`); the first tap shows "Tap again" (`againHint`, also in the save prompt). A frame touched by finger or pen sends `inline:touch` / `save:touch` with its nonce to the worker, which tells the field's document only (frames never post to the page), and the content script then checks it every animation frame; mouse users keep the 250 ms check, because a per-frame loop delayed IntersectionObserver v2's report of a page covering the frame (found in the real-browser kit) and refuses clicks when IntersectionObserver v2 is missing. Content-script listeners sit on `window` in the capture phase.
  - Browsers without IntersectionObserver v2 (Safari, Firefox; `hasVisibilityCheck()`), since 2026-11-21: the gate counts the frame as visible from when it was shown (was: never, so every click said "Click again" and keys never counted there); "shown" = IntersectionObserver v1 fully on screen (ratio ≥ 0.99); `tampered()` (every 250 ms, every frame while scrolling or after a touch) also runs `covered()` for the menu and save prompt, not the in-field icon: the top element (`elementFromPoint`) at the host's centre and 3 px inside each corner must be the host, else the frame closes. If the observer can't start where the browser has the check (Chrome), clicks still never count. Residual risk there: any layer with `pointer-events: none`, opaque or see-through, over the frame isn't detected (only Chrome's `isVisible` sees it); a click still needs 500 ms pointer rest, and fills only logins that match the page's site.
  - Regex websites match only for items in the user's own vaults (`background/site.ts` `matchesUrl(…, allowRegex)`, the core's linear-time engine). The popup's editor and item page name a pattern the core refuses, with the core's reason (`regexError`; request `regexErrors`; also "default" while the default is regex).
  - Page-supplied strings are capped (`shared/limits.ts`: user name 1 KiB, password 4 KiB — longer ones aren't offered — passkey names 256 characters). Session storage keeps one key per record (`background/pending.ts`: `save:<tab>`, `user:<tab>`, `menu:<nonce>`).
  - Vault timeout: only user actions restart the timer (each request's `activity` in the router table, `background/handlers/`; `activity.ts` restarts it); polling (`state`, `item`, `totp`, `passkey:pending`) doesn't, and the popup counts codes down locally (`shared/totp.ts` gives `expires`). Lock locks every account. `restore()` gives up after any await if the lock generation changed or the session keys are gone, so a lock always wins.
  - Passkey user verification follows `userVerification`: "required" asks for the master password in the confirmation window unless it was entered in the last 5 minutes (checked locally); "preferred" sets UV only after such a recent entry; "discouraged" never does. Unlock, sign-in and reauth record the entry in session storage; a lock clears it. With the vault timeout "Never" the unlocked vault itself counts (also keys restored from disk at a browser start; `passkeyVerified`), like the phones' unlock: the user chose not to be asked again on this device. A PIN unlock counts only under "Never". Passkey requests are bound to the account and lock generation that started them (re-checked before signing or saving) and wait at most 4 minutes. New passkey logins are named after the host, not the page-supplied `rp.name`. A request whose page has gone ends and its window closes: the bridge sends `passkey:cancel` for its open requests on `pagehide`, a request from a new document in the same tab (`sender.documentId`) replaces the old one instead of being refused, and closing the tab cancels its requests (`cancelPasskeysInTab`).
  - Secret Keys and remember-device tokens are keyed by server address and email (`accountKey`, migrated once); signing out or removing an account forgets both (the timeout's sign-out action keeps them). Two-step sign-in has "Remember this browser for 30 days" (on by default); Settings → Account security → "Forget this browser".
  - The background answers `locked` / `signed_out` codes (`shared/errors.ts`) and the popup reloads its state on them; an error boundary wraps the popup.
  - The popup masks national ID, passport and licence numbers (reveal and copy like a password) and shows the same identity fields as the web vault (`popup/identity.ts`).
  - `"externally_connectable": {"ids": []}` is declared.
- Extension popup, right-click menu and shortcuts (2026-11-15 round 3a):
  - Right-click menu (`background/menus.ts`): pure `menuModel`/`actionMenuModel`; `refreshMenus` rebuilds 150 ms after a tab switch, page load, window focus or a `pageStateChanged` storage change, only when a SHA-256 digest of the entries changed (`menuBuilt`, session; no names stored), and the match index avoids decrypting for pages without logins. Root "PassAuth" on http/https only. Signed out: "Sign in to PassAuth…". Locked: "Unlock to fill…" (fields only) · Copy a strong password · Open PassAuth. No login: "No logins for <host>" · "Add a login for <host>…" (`popupRoute`). One login: Fill "<name> · <user name>" (fields only) · Copy user name / password / verification code. Several: the same as submenus, last used first, at most 10, then "More in PassAuth…". Titles carry names and user names only (`&` escaped, `%s` broken up, 60 characters). A click is re-checked against the clicked frame's URL and the tab's current URL; fills go to `info.frameId` through `fill()` and never on a hidden site; copies go through the offscreen clipboard, are marked used and leave a page-safe note ("Password copied. It clears in 30 seconds."). `Settings.contextMenu` off removes the page entries. Toolbar icon menu: Lock now · Sync now · Generator · Open web vault.
  - Commands (`background/commands.ts`): `autofill` (Ctrl/⌘+Shift+L) fills the login used last; again within 5 s on the same tab and page fills the next one (`fillCycle`). `generate_password` (Ctrl/⌘+Shift+9) copies a password made with the saved options; `lock` has no default key. A fill asked for while locked is kept 2 min (`fillAfterUnlock`: account, tab, URL, frame); after an unlock the popup calls `afterUnlock` (the menu fills when exactly one login matches; the shortcut fills the last used). Settings shows the real keys from `chrome.commands.getAll()`.
  - Last used (`last-used.ts`, `chrome.storage.local` `lastUsed:<account>`, item ids and times only): orders suggestions, menu entries and the shortcut; fills and copies mark an item; forgotten on sign-out (incl. the timeout's) and account removal.
  - Toolbar icon: a grey padlock variant and the title "PassAuth is locked" while locked.
  - PIN unlock (`pin.ts`, `storage.ts` `pinCopies`): 6–64 digits, typed twice, after a local master-password check; not offered with "Never". The core's `Account.rewrap(bundle, pin, false)` (Argon2id with the account defaults, new salt) makes a copy kept only in session storage as `pin:<account>`; a lock keeps it; tries are counted before each check, one at a time, and the 5th wrong PIN deletes it; it is deleted on sign-out, the timeout's sign-out, account switch/add/removal, a different account bundle from the server, "Never" and turning it off, and gone with the browser session. A PIN unlock never counts as a master-password entry for passkey user verification. Threat: code running in the browser during the session could read the copy and guess about 20 bits offline at one 128 MiB Argon2id per guess — the same attacker could read the keys at the next unlock.
  - Popup: the list stays mounted while an item is open; route, search, filters and scroll are kept 2 min in `popupView` (cleared on lock, sign-out and switch); `popupRoute` is used once. One search (`popup/search.ts`: words ANDed over name, user name, websites, tags and folder; current site first, then prefixes; "N results" announced); filter chips; keyboard: ↓/↑ with one tab stop, Enter fills or opens, → opens, Ctrl/⌘+C password, +Shift user name, +Alt code. Rows: type glyphs, Shared and passkey marks, `content-visibility`, no row cap; second lines from `vault.ts` `subtitleOf` (no note text). Item page: the name as title, a favourite star, a password-history page, large type, digits underlined and symbols boxed, 4+4 codes, the ring on `--ring-*`. The editor confirms before discarding changes; generator options (`web/src/components/GeneratorOptions.tsx`) are saved to `generatorOptions` (`shared/generator-settings.ts`) and also drive the editor's dice; late generator answers are dropped. One live region for notes; sign-out asks first. An open popup reloads its list (keeping the rows on screen until the new ones arrive) when the worker rewrites or drops the active account's match index (`index:<account>`), so live-sync changes and saves show without reopening (`view-state.ts` `vaultChanged`). Settings: real shortcuts, PIN, "Hidden on these sites", right-click menu and sign-up password toggles, compact list. Type in rem; height `min(600px,100vh)`. The first install opens `popup.html?welcome=1`.
- Extension page side (2026-11-15 review round 3b):
  - Content scripts: `bridge.js` (document_start, every frame: only the passkey bridge, `content/bridge.ts`) and `content.js` (document_idle, every frame, `match_origin_as_fallback`). about:blank and srcdoc frames stand for the origin the browser says they inherited (`content/origin.ts` `pageUrl`, `background/sites.ts` `webFrame`); sandboxed and data: frames get nothing, and the WebAuthn shim and bridge don't run there. A field focused before injection is picked up.
  - Field detection (`content/fields.ts`, `forms.ts`): one `Scan` per event; fields are walked once, including open shadow roots and closed ones of custom elements (`chrome.dom.openOrClosedShadowRoot`); hint text includes name, id, placeholder, aria-label, aria-labelledby, `<label>` and title. Kinds: login; new-password (autocomplete, wording, or two password fields; of three, the first is current); otp (one-time-code, wording, or 4–8 one-character boxes); card (cc-* tokens; wording only in a form with a card number; the security code only when nothing else matches); identity (tokens; wording only in forms asking for two parts or more; a lone e-mail field is an identifier-first login). A saved login fills current-password fields, never new-password ones; codes fill split boxes.
  - Submits (`content/capture.ts`): submit events, Enter in a login field, or a click on a button in the same form or container (up to 6 levels) as a typed password, in any language; English submit words count only for buttons with no typed password near them; toggles (aria-pressed, show/hide/forgot/cancel wording, drawn over the password field) are excluded. `page:submitted` carries `keyboard`.
  - Inline menu v2: the icon shows on every detected field (cards only on https) whether locked or not and whatever matches; nothing shows on hidden sites (`hiddenSites`, "⋯ → Hide on this site", `background/sites.ts`) or with "off". Icon: 24 px, left of site controls (elementsFromPoint) and of a right padding of 24 px or more, else just outside the field (`content/place.ts`), kept in place by ResizeObserver, scroll/resize and a 250 ms tamper check. Rows (`background/inline-menu.ts`): logins last used first; on otp fields those with a code, with the code and a countdown; cards ("Visa •••• 4242 · 09/28"); identities ("Alex Morgan · Home"). Footer: "New login/card/identity" (session `popupRoute` {page: "new", kind, url: page origin, username}) and, on new-password fields with `inlineOnSignup`, "Use a strong password" (`generatorOptions` within the field's minlength/maxlength; fills the field and its confirmation; keeps a pending save marked `generated` for 10 minutes, even with "Ask to save" off; that prompt doesn't time out and shows on the next URL). "⋯": "Hide on <base domain>" (it hides the whole site; `InlineItems.hides`), "Open PassAuth". Keyboard: Alt+↓ opens with the focus in the list; ↓ moves it in ("focus" postMessage carrying the nonce); ↑/↓/Home/End; Enter fills; Esc (`inline:close {refocus}`) and Tab (`inline:return`) give it back through the worker. Listbox/option roles, label "N logins for host". An early or covered click shows "Click again"; a failed fill from a row shows the page note "PassAuth couldn't fill this page. Open PassAuth for details."; errors wrap inside the fixed frame.
  - Filling (`background/fill.ts`): `fill(id, tabId, target?)` and `fillItem(id, tabId, frameId?)` fill logins, cards (`fill:card`: https pages only, same-site frames, brand from the number when not saved) and identities (`fill:identity`: never ID, passport or licence numbers), then `markUsed`. A frame named without its document (right-click menu) is reached only through its own claim, so its real origin is checked. Values are set with focus, the native setter, input and change; the focus stays in the last field filled.
  - Save prompt: content scripts don't ask on page load; the background shows a pending save on `tabs.onUpdated` "complete" (`save-prompt.ts` `onTabUpdated`, wired in `main.ts`), and the page holds it back when it asks for a password again, except a generated password on a new URL. It takes the focus only after a keyboard submit (`SavePrompt.focus`), otherwise it announces itself (role=status); its 30 s timer pauses while the pointer or focus is inside; only Esc inside the prompt or its buttons dismiss it.
- Extension sync, policies and menus (2026-11-15 round 4):
  - Delta sync in the worker (only with `features` `delta_sync`; merge rules from `@web/lib/delta`, the extension's own in `background/sync-rules.ts`, pure, vitest): the offline copy is the merged snapshot in the shape of a full `/sync` answer (`StoredSync`: cursor, each vault's `seq`, the rows and own writes in one IndexedDB record, so a cursor never exists without its rows), written whole after each merge (`persist`: one write at a time, always the newest snapshot, none after a lock, sign-out or switch; an interrupted apply leaves the previous copy); offline-copy operations run in one queue (`storage.ts` `inOrder`). Syncs ask `GET /sync?since=<cursor>&trash=summary` (the extension never shows the trash); a malformed delta or one without a base fetches everything once. `materialize` keeps a vault's key handle while `wrapped_key`/`key_kind`/`key_epoch` stay the same and reuses decrypted items whose ciphertext didn't change; the match index (with card and identity counts), badge and menus follow. Own writes (create, edit in place, trash) compare `vault_seq` with the copy's number: the next number → applied as sent, anything else → a delta sync; one sync in flight, one queued; `/sync/revision` is judged with the vault numbers.
  - Live sync (`background/live-sync.ts`; only with `delta_sync`): while the active account is unlocked the worker keeps a WebSocket to `/api/ws?access_token=` and sends `{"type":"ping"}` every 20 s (Chrome 116+ keeps a worker running while a socket exchanges messages; checked in Chrome for Testing 157: worker and socket alive after 70 s without DevTools). `sync` messages → `vault.liveNotice` (the `hello` origin is kept, never used to skip); `logout` → `checkSession`. Every (re)connect first runs `checkForChanges` (`/sync/revision`). Reconnects back off 1 s → 60 s ±20%. Lock, sign-out, account switch and a lost session close the socket. Fallback alarm `liveSync` every 5 minutes while unlocked.
  - Right-click menu, completed: "Fill a strong password" (fields, unlocked, not on hidden sites) is always in the menu but shown only while the user is in a password field of the tab's site (`content/context-field.ts` reports focus moves and right-button presses as `page:contextField`; the worker toggles it with `contextMenus.update({visible})`, because Chrome has no "menu shown" event — on macOS a right-click into a field that wasn't focused may miss it the first time); a click asks the clicked frame for its field (`context:field`: password?, minlength/maxlength) and fills through `inline-menu.ts` `fillStrongPassword` (shared with "Use a strong password"), keeping the generated pending save. "Fill card ›" (https pages) and "Fill identity ›" list cards ("<name> · Visa •••• 4242") and identities, last used first, at most 10; a click re-checks the item, that the clicked frame is on the tab's current site (https for cards) and that the site isn't hidden, then `fillItem` to `info.frameId`. A click from a menu built before PassAuth locked keeps its item (`fillAfterUnlock` `itemId`): after the unlock exactly that login, card or identity is filled.
  - Fill problems (`background/fill-problems.ts`): a failed fill from the inline menu, the right-click menu or the fill shortcut keeps its reason per tab (session `fillProblem:<tab>` with the tab's address, 30 min; never a secret), gone when the tab navigates, reloads or closes, or a fill works; the vault list shows "Why PassAuth couldn't fill this page: …".
  - Invitations: the vault list shows "N vault invitations are waiting" linking to `<server>/settings/vaults`, dismissible per set for the browser session.
  - Policies (`background/policies.ts`, rules from `@web/lib/policies`): 403 `two_step_required` is remembered per account until a sync gets through; the popup shows "Your server requires two-step login" (Set it up in the web vault → `/settings/two-step`, Check again, Sign out…). `max_vault_timeout_minutes`: the shortest limit of the signed-in accounts' servers applies to the shared setting; Settings offers only choices within it ("Never", browser restart and system lock go) and says when a saved choice was lowered. `policy_violations` → a dismissible notice linking to the web vault (`/settings/two-step`, `/settings/security`). Server features and policies are cached per server for an hour and asked for again at every unlock, sign-in and PIN unlock (`api.ts` `refreshServerInfo`; requests meanwhile wait for it; an unreachable server keeps the last answer), so a lowered `max_vault_timeout_minutes` applies from the next unlock.
  - Quotas: `quota_exceeded` reads "You've reached this server's storage limit. Delete items or ask the server's admin." in the editor and item actions, as the save prompt's whole message (the save stays for another try), and for passkey saves as a page note.
  - Editor drafts: the popup editor keeps what is typed (web `ItemEditor` `onChange`) in `popupView` (≤ 512 KiB, incl. a new item's id), so a toolbar popup that closes comes back to it within 2 minutes; leaving, saving or discarding drops it; lock, sign-out and switch remove it.
- Extension hardening (2026-11-16 security review; local real-browser kit checks 03 modes outline/mask/maskbox/content, 20, 20b, 16, 17, 17b):
  - Host styles are pinned with `:host{all:initial !important}` plus explicit pins (display, position, z-index, outline, mask, `-webkit-mask`, `-webkit-mask-box-image`, content-visibility); placement comes through `--pa-*` custom properties (`placeHost`). `tampered()` also fails on host outlines and masks, and on `<html>`/`<body>` masks or outlines wider than 3 px, which IntersectionObserver v2 doesn't report (a page could hide the frame under an outline or mask while clicks still reached it).
  - The frames never post to the page (a page that got `e.source` could focus the frame or navigate it): Tab, Esc and touch go to the worker with the nonce. A second iframe load closes the menu or prompt, and the frames are out of the page's tab order (`tabIndex = -1`).
  - Keys in a frame count only after the content script's nonce focus request (or the focus arriving within 1 s after it), or after a click or tap that passed the gate; blur disarms. Confirm taps must be on the same control (`controlOf`).
  - The menu opens by itself only when the last trusted input within 1 s was a press on the field or its label, or Tab (`inputAimedAt`), never on a page's own `focus()` after an unrelated click.
  - Saves are offered only for a password that trusted `input` events left in its field, submitted within 1 s of a trusted input in that form or on its button (a page could otherwise fill and `requestSubmit()` its own values); PassAuth's own fills never count, a generated password still saves (`rememberGenerated`).
