  - Passkey sign-in requests (`get`) carry the page's transient activation; after a cancel, window close, timeout or page abort, requests from that tab without activation are refused at once for 30 s, doubling up to 10 min (`backedOff`); an accepted request or closing the tab clears it.
  - `inline:fill` accepts only ids that menu offered (`menu:<nonce>` `offered`, at most 500).
  - `chrome.storage.local` is restricted to trusted contexts (`setAccessLevel({accessLevel: "TRUSTED_CONTEXTS"})` at worker start and on install; content scripts read no storage; verified in Chrome 158).
  - Server strings are type-checked before the core sees them (`serverText`; `/sync` vault rows with a non-string `id`, `wrapped_key` or `key_kind` stay closed, `meta` must be a string, a non-string `role` reads as "read").
  - Errors: own wording for known codes (incl. 507 `insufficient_storage`); unknown ones show the generic sentence, the request id and "Server message: …" (at most 200 characters, cleaned).
  - Offline copy: read back with `isSyncResponse` (else a full sync) and its vault rows through `isSyncVaultRow`, like a server answer.
  - Offline changes (contract in "Offline changes"; `background/offline-rules.ts` pure rules + tests, `vault.ts` section "offline changes"): each account's list sealed in `storage.local` `offlineChanges:<id>` = `{sealed, count}` through the storage queue (count readable while locked, for the Log out / Change server warnings). A save waits when `api.ts` reports the account's last request got no answer or the browser is offline, or the request fails without an answer or with 502/503/504; saves to an item with a change always merge into it. `allEntries`/`getEntry` and the match index show the copy with the list on top (popup, search, fill, badge, right-click and inline menus; trashed items leave the list, their change is under "Waiting to sync"). Sender once per account after every `accept` that checked out, reusing `postItem`/`putItem`/`trashRow` (manifests, `settle` link rule); holds unopened, read-only or manifest-warned vaults. Reconnect (first answer, `online` event, 1-min `offlineChanges` alarm while unlocked) syncs first. Popup: row markers, status notice → "Waiting to sync" page, item box with "Discard this change", decision sheet route `conflict`. Beyond the contract: a sent create is re-sent with the same body unless the vault key changed (re-encrypting would duplicate it); an edit whose server row is in the trash counts as "deleted"; the timeout's sign-out keeps the list; no markers in the fixed-height inline menu.
  - Live sync: `{"type":"two_step_required"}` or a close 1008 `two_step_required` stops the socket and its alarm for the account and sets the 403 two-step flag (`twoStep:<account>`), so the popup shows the two-step screen; live sync starts again when the vault opens or a sync gets through.
  - Popup editor: under each website that runs as a regular expression (`resolveMatch` = regex) the editor shows `patternNote` from the popup's own core (`ItemEditor.websiteNote`, the extension's default match): the refusal reason or the "isn't tied to one website" warning; saving isn't blocked (the old refused-pattern list above Save and the `regexErrors` request are gone).
- Extension structure (2026-11-15 review round 2):
  - Service worker: `background/main.ts` (bundled as `background.js`) wires the modules and registers every chrome listener at the top level; modules have no import side effects.
  - Router: `router.ts` dispatches over one handler table, `{type → allowed senders (senders.ts senderKind: popup, inline, save, content, offscreen), needs unlock, counts as activity, parse, run}` (`handlers/popup.ts`, `content.ts`, `frames.ts`). Anything not in the table, or from another sender, is refused (`not_allowed`) before it runs. Passkey bridge requests are answered in the authenticator's shape. Feature modules: `fill.ts`, `save-prompt.ts`, `inline-menu.ts`, `nonces.ts`, `matching.ts` + `match-index.ts`, `badge.ts`, `commands.ts` (keyboard commands, the fill that waits for an unlock, openPopup), `menus.ts` (right-click menus), `pin.ts` (PIN unlock), `last-used.ts`, `timeout.ts` + `activity.ts`, `toast.ts`, `migrations.ts`, `live-sync.ts`, `sync-rules.ts`, `policies.ts`, `fill-problems.ts`, `sites.ts`, `core.ts` (the WASM core, loaded once; router, passkey bridge, badge, menus, commands and tab events wait for it), `site.ts`, `sign-in-requests.ts`, `device-sign-in.ts`, `attachments.ts`; content `context-field.ts`; popup `notices.tsx`, `sign-in-requests.tsx`, `device-sign-in.tsx`, `files.tsx`, `core.ts` (the popup loads the core only for a sign-in request's key and for decrypting files). Site logic in the tests is the real core (`test/setup.ts`).
  - Protocol: `shared/protocol.ts` has one `{type: Rpc<req, res>}` map per channel (popup, passkey window, inline frame, save frame, content, bridge, content commands, offscreen). Handler tables are checked with `satisfies`; pages and content scripts send through `channel<C>()` (`callPopup`, `callContent`, …); content and frame messages pass runtime guards (`checks.ts`).
  - Errors and logging: replies are `{ok:false, code, message}` from `AppError{code, message, pageSafe}`. Toasts take only `PageSafeText` (`shared/page-text.ts`), so vault data can't reach a page by accident. Logging is `shared/log.ts` (debug when `chrome.storage.local` `debugLog` is true).
  - Storage: `local.update(fn)` reads and writes inside the storage queue. Old layouts are migrated once behind `schemaVersion` (`migrations.ts`, at worker start).
  - Wake cost: key restore and the first vault load are single-flight, and syncs are coalesced. The account record carries `secretKeyEnabled`. A match index in session storage (`index:<account>`: item id, websites and rules, own-vault flag; no names or secrets; cleared with the keys) lets the badge and inline-menu counts skip decrypting after a worker restart.
  - Type checking: one TypeScript project per context (`extension/tsconfig/`: worker = WebWorker, no DOM; content = DOM + chrome; main-world = DOM only; pages = DOM + React + chrome; test; web = `@web` code with the web vault's settings, seen as declarations), with `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` and `verbatimModuleSyntax`.
  - Lint and format: Biome (`biome.jsonc`; `pnpm lint`, `pnpm format`). Packaging: `pnpm package` builds a reproducible zip (`package.mjs`: fixed times, sorted entries).
  - Real-browser checks (2026-11-15, Chrome for Testing 158 with a throwaway server, local-only kit in the session scratchpad, not in the repo): sign-in, inline menu placement/keyboard/click gate, click hijacking, save prompt incl. the shared-vault planting case, strong passwords on sign-up, the timeout with a code open, passkeys incl. user verification, live sync, policies, cards over https, drafts, right-click menu contents, same-site iframes, PIN unlock. Round 5 added confirm taps (emulated touch), sign-in approval both ways, files, icons, the passkey window's "Click again" and policies applied at unlock; 19 checks pass.
  - Tests: vitest with a fake `chrome` and IndexedDB, a fake core and an in-memory API 2 server that speaks delta sync, a fake WebSocket (`test/fake-socket.ts`) (`extension/test/`; `pnpm test`), including every request type against every kind of sender (`router.test.ts`), the service-worker wake cost (`vault-wake.test.ts`) and the golden vectors (`vectors.test.ts`).
- **Save rule** (the same in every client that offers to save; one implementation, the core's `plan_save`, wrapped by the extension's `background/site.ts` (prompt wording in `shared/save-text.ts`), iOS `Shared/SaveLogic.swift` and Android `autofill/SaveRule.kt`). `U` = typed user name (trimmed, compared case-insensitively, may be empty), `P` = typed password, `C` = logins matching the page; "own" = writable and in the user's personal vault. (1) Logins in `C` with user name `U` (for an empty `U` only own ones count: an empty user name proves nothing): one already has `P` (any vault) → nothing to offer; else the first own one → update it; no own one (only shared or view-only logins have `U`) → a new login. (2) None and `U` not empty: an own login with an empty user name (only a password was saved; prefer the one whose password is `P`) → update it; none → new login. (3) None and `U` empty: `P` already saved for the page → nothing; exactly one own login → update it; otherwise a new login. An update writes the password (old one to the password history) and the user name only when one was typed. No rule ever selects a login outside the user's own vault: anyone on the server can add a user to a vault without consent and plant a login there, with an empty user name or with the victim's email as user name, which would otherwise collect what the user types (see Sharing; rule 1 changed on 2026-11-15). Clients with their own prompt (extension save frame, iOS save sheet, Android sheet) show "Save in": the own logins for the page, then writable shared-vault logins with user name `U` (`U` not empty), labelled with their vault and never preselected, then "New login"; while a shared one is selected the prompt warns "Saving here shares this password with everyone in <vault>." (`sharedWarning`). A shared login is written to only when the user picks it, and saves without a prompt never use one. What the prompt showed is what is applied, and only a target that this pending save offered is accepted. Android's save dialog line (`SaveRule.hint`) follows the same rule. The cases are pinned by the shared golden vectors (`testdata/vectors.json`, below). A rule-3 update is never applied without that prompt (where none can be shown, a new login is saved instead). Pending saves are bound to the account that captured them and dropped on lock, logout and account switch. The extension's prompt is an extension-origin frame (like the inline menu), so the page never sees vault item names or user names, and form capture needs a user gesture.
- **Add this site to an existing login** (extension 0.9.0; iOS/macOS AutoFill and Android autofill 2026-11-17): one account used on several sites or apps. Rules (extension `background/add-site.ts`, Swift `Shared/AddSite.swift`, Kotlin `autofill/AddSite.kt`): only logins the user can edit whose websites don't already match the place; what is added is the page origin `https://host` (Android apps: `androidapp://<package>`; web only for trusted browsers / asset-linked WebViews) with match "default"; nothing changes when it already matches. Entry points: extension popup "Add" → "Existing login…" (picker + confirm); AutoFill lists (iOS, macOS, Android) "Add this site to a login…" → picker → confirm → add, then fill; a failed add never fills. Save prompts (extension, iOS save sheet, Android sheet; macOS has no save requests): only when the plan has no targets for the site, up to 3 OWN-vault writable logins with the same user name (trimmed, case-insensitive) AND exactly the same password are offered as "Add this site" rows, never preselected, with the warning "Only add it if <host> uses the same account as <their site>"; re-checked before applying (else re-planned). The extension's page toast never names the login. iOS: a silent save that would create a new login shows the sheet instead when such a login exists.
- **Prompts** ("decision sheet", every client): brand bar · context row (site tile, sentence-case title saying what is decided, domain beneath) · one grouped card of choices with a radio mark · verification directly above the actions · exactly one filled primary button, other actions as quiet text buttons. Tokens: spacing 4/8/12/16/24/32, radius 8/12/16 (web `app.css` `--space-*`/`--radius-*`/`--text-*`, iOS `PA.Space`/`PA.Radius`, Android `Theme.kt`). Shared parts: web `components/ui.tsx` (`Modal`, `ConfirmDialog`, `SheetActions`, classes `.sheet-*`, `.choices`, `.choice*`, `.tile`, `.btn-text`, `.setting*`, `.switch` — the extension popup depends on these names), extension `popup/sheet.tsx`, iOS `UI/DecisionSheet.swift`, Android `ui/components/DecisionSheet.kt`. Extension windows (passkey, unlock, pop-out) open centred over the requesting browser window and sized to their content (`shared/windows.ts`, maths in `shared/window-place.ts`).
- **API 2 conventions**: `/api/config` has `api: 2` and a `features` list (`key_epoch`, `idempotent_create`, `error_codes`, `revoke_others`, `trash_retention`, `delta_sync`, `breach_check` while on, `vault_invitations`, `activity_log`, `item_history` while `item_versions_kept` > 0, `policies`, `forget_device`, `emergency_vault_keys`, `signed_sharing`, `avatar`, `account_wraps_v2`, `send_v2`, `vault_manifest`, `sends` while on, `attachments`, `emergency_access`, `login_with_device` while on, `icons` while the icon service is on, `push` while sign-in request push notifications are on, …); clients detect features from that list, never from version numbers (web `loadPublicConfig`/`hasFeature`, cached per server and refreshed at start-up, sign-in and unlock by `refreshPublicConfig`; `usePublicConfig` follows `onPublicConfig`; sync reads `knownPublicConfig` so a refresh never delays it). Errors are `{error, message, request_id}`; messages keep their old text so older clients that compare it still work, and clients check `error` first (web `hasErrorCode`, iOS `ApiError.hasCode`, Android `ApiException.hasCode`). Codes: 400 `bad_request` / `expired` / `invalid_code` / `update_required`; 401 `unauthorized` / `invalid_credentials` / `session_expired`; 400 `secret_key_required` / `kdf_too_weak`; 401 `wrong_password` (Send); 403 `forbidden` / `email_not_verified` / `reauth_required` / `account_disabled` / `signups_closed` / `two_step_required` / `sends_disabled` / `quota_exceeded` / `attachments_disabled` / `emergency_access_disabled` / `login_with_device_disabled`; 404 `not_found`; 405 `method_not_allowed`; 409 `vault_key_changed` / `revision_conflict` / `item_exists` / `vault_exists` / `account_exists` / `last_vault` / `already_enabled` / `conflict` / `attachment_exists` / `already_uploaded` / `upload_in_progress` / `emergency_vaults_changed` (with `expected`) / `roster_changed` / `identity_exists` / `key_pins_changed` / `member_roster_changed` / `members_changed` (with `key_epoch`, `unknown`, `identity_changed`, `role_changed`, `missing`) / `push_disabled`; 400 `bad_signature` / `signing_identity_required`; 413 `too_large`; 429 `rate_limited` (always with `Retry-After` in seconds); 500 `internal` (a panicking handler answers this and the server keeps running); 507 `insufficient_storage` (the free-disk reserve; never retried automatically). Clients word every code they know themselves (web `apiErrorText`); for an unknown code they show a generic sentence, the request id ("Reference: …", only if it matches `[A-Za-z0-9_.:-]{1,64}`) and the server's `message` only as "Server message: …" (one line, Cc/Cf removed, at most 200 characters), so a compromised server can't put its own instructions in the apps' voice (security review F11). The attachment quota ("attachment storage limit reached") has its own wording. The desktop app's native-client errors (e.g. the 128 MiB answer cap) are shown in its own words (`UnreachableError.detail`). Every response carries `x-request-id` (a well-formed client value is kept); the debug log span has method, path (never the query string), request id and client app. Every client sends `X-PassAuth-Client` (web `web/<version>` via Vite `define`, desktop `desktop/<version>` via `platform.clientId()`, extension, `ios/`, `macos-autofill/`, `android/` — Android only through `serverRequest()`, never on asset-link fetches). Safe retries: a create whose id exists with the same vault, key and data succeeds with the stored revision; editors and save sheets keep a new item's id across retries, and a 409 `item_exists` means an earlier try landed, so the client updates that item instead of adding a second one; web import batches keep their ids and exact body for up to 3 tries (network, 5xx, 429 after `Retry-After`). `/sync` and `/sync/revision` are compressed with gzip or brotli when asked, never auth routes or errors. Server values are type-checked before they reach the core or the screen (security review F5): web `types.ts` `isSyncResponse` (user id, email, name, `email_verified`, `bundle.account_id`), `isSyncVaultRow` (`id`, `meta`, `role`, `wrapped_key`, `key_kind` strings; `rotation_pending`, `key_epoch`, `seq` numbers when present; used by `delta.ts` `check()`), `readPrelogin`, `readLoginFinish`, `expectStrings` for OPAQUE fields, `readMembers` (a malformed row stops a key replacement instead of skipping that member), `readFoundUser`, `readRecoveryCodes`; a malformed delta triggers a full fetch, a malformed full answer is a sync error. Behind that, every WASM export checks its string parameters itself (`expected a string`; `crates/core-wasm` takes `JsValue` with `unchecked_param_type`, review L1), and `generate-vectors.mjs --check` calls every export with non-strings (1508 calls) and requires clean errors and no WASM memory growth. Byte parameters are checked the same way: they take only a genuine `Uint8Array` (or a subclass such as `Buffer`, also one from another realm, checked through the `%TypedArray%` `Symbol.toStringTag` getter rather than `instanceof`), otherwise `Error("expected bytes")`. wasm-bindgen's own `&[u8]` turned a string into zeros and coerced a plain array. The same run refuses strings, plain arrays, other typed arrays, `ArrayBuffer`s and forged `Uint8Array`s (the prototype or tag on something else) for each of the 9 byte parameters, accepts one from a `node:vm` realm, and round-trips a file through `fileEncrypt`/`fileDecrypt` and `FileEncryptor`/`FileDecryptor`.
- **Trash retention**: admin setting `trash_days` (0–3650, 0 = keep forever, the default; also in `/api/config`). Every 10 minutes the sweeper deletes items that have been in the trash longer than that, vault by vault under the same lock as other writes (a restore in progress wins); members re-sync. Vaults with a manifest are skipped: their members' apps delete what is due with `POST /vaults/{id}/trash/purge` (see Vault manifests), so there `trash_days` is applied by the apps and old trash stays until an app of a member with write access opens the vault.
- **Regular-expression websites** count only for logins in the user's own vaults (`key_kind` `account`), in every client, also when "default" resolves to regex: extension `matchesUrl(…, allowRegex)`; iOS/macOS `UrlMatcher.matches(…, allowRegex:)` via `AutofillMatcher.candidates(_:in:defaultMatch:)` (`VaultData.isPersonal`; AutoFill list, one-time codes, save rule); Android `AutofillMatcher.matches(…, allowRegex)` / `candidates(target, vault, …)` / `saveCandidates` (fill datasets, TOTP and save sheets, Credential Manager password entries and their re-check in `CredentialActivity`). A login whose vault is unknown counts as someone else's; a shared login's other websites match as usual. Reason: a vault member could plant a slow or catch-all pattern. With the regex default, a "default" entry that is a plain address (it parses and serializes to itself, one trailing "/" aside) or an app address (`androidapp://…`, any "://" address that serializes to itself) is never a pattern but matched as Domain (security review M2: a saved `https://bank.com` used to match `https://evil.example/https://bank.com`); everything else stays a pattern. Patterns are searched in origin plus path, which the page's owner chooses, so only `^https://<host with \.>[/:$]` is safe; explicit patterns are not auto-anchored (existing patterns keep working). A page whose origin plus path exceeds 2048 bytes matches no pattern (I3). `resolveMatch(url, match, default)` says what an entry runs as (never "default"); every item editor shows `regexError`, or the pattern warning when `regexWarning(pattern)` returns one (not anchored / host not pinned), exactly when it is "regex", and never blocks saving: "This pattern isn't tied to one website, so it could match other sites. Start it with ^https://example\.com/ (your site's address)." Web: `lib/patterns.ts` `patternNote`, `ItemEditor.websiteNote` (each app supplies its own core and default; the web vault has no default setting, so it passes "domain").
- **Site logic in the core** (`crates/core/src/site`, 2026-11-15; replaces the clients' own copies as they switch): `normalize_host` (WHATWG host parser from the `url` crate: UTS #46 nontransitional IDNA to ASCII, lower case, canonical IPv4/IPv6, one trailing dot dropped), `public_suffix` / `registrable_domain` / `is_public_suffix` / `base_domain` (what "domain" matching compares: the registrable domain, else the host itself), `matches_url` / `Page` (pages must be absolute http(s) URLs; saved URLs keep the default https scheme, review L4) / `downgrade` / `regex_error` / `regex_warning` / `resolve_match`, the save rule (`plan_save`, `chosen_target`), passkey "Save in" (`passkey_save_targets`, `passkey_default_target`) and RP IDs (`effective_rp_id`). WASM: `normalizeHost`, `publicSuffix`, `registrableDomain`, `isPublicSuffix`, `baseDomain`, `publicSuffixListVersion`, `matchesUrl`, `matchUrls`, `downgrade`, `regexError`, `regexWarning`, `resolveMatch`, `planSave`, `chosenSaveTarget`, `passkeySaveTargets`, `passkeyDefaultTarget`, `effectiveRpId` (throws an Error named "SecurityError"). UniFFI: the same functions, records `SavedUrl`, `SaveCandidate`, `SaveTarget`, `PasskeyLogin`, `PasskeyTarget`, enums `SavePlan`, `SaveReason`, error `RpIdError` (Kotlin `RpIdException`). Match types travel as the item strings; an unknown one never matches, an unknown default counts as "domain". Saved addresses are websites only with http(s) (no scheme = https): `androidapp://…` never matches a page and is never a passkey target. iOS, macOS AutoFill and Android switched on 2026-11-15 (round 6): `UrlMatcher`, `SaveLogic` / `SaveRule`, `PasskeyLogic` / `PasskeyTargets` and Android `WebAuthn` are thin wrappers that keep only app concerns (the default match setting, the shared-vault regex rule, entries, wording, Android's save hint); Swift `PublicSuffixes`, `unsafeRegex` and Kotlin `PublicSuffixes` / `Idna` are deleted; the extension (round 5) wraps the core in `background/site.ts` (keeps `LoginUrl` shapes, the default match and own-vault regex, `matchingEntries` = one `matchUrls` call per page; only strings are passed to the core (the exports also refuse non-strings themselves); `passkeys.ts` `effectiveRpId` turns the core's SecurityError into `PasskeyError`) and its `background/{psl,urls,save-plan,passkey-targets}.ts` are deleted; `UrlMatcher.parse` (display, server address, identity store) converts IDN hosts with `normalizeHost`. Android JVM unit tests run the real core: a host build of `crates/core-ffi` (`target/debug/libpassauth_ffi.{dylib,so}`, made by `scripts/build-android-core.sh`) loaded through JNA (`jna.library.path` in `app/build.gradle.kts`); without it the test task stops with a message.
- **Public Suffix List**: the full upstream list (ICANN and private sections, wildcard and exception rules, implicit `*` for unknown top-level domains), kept byte for byte as `crates/core/src/site/public_suffix_list.dat` (MPL-2.0, credited in the README). `build.rs` embeds the rules without comments; they are parsed once on first use (about 3 ms in WASM). So x.myfritz.net, x.myds.me, x.dedyn.io, x.cloudapp.net and x.<region>.compute.amazonaws.com are sites of their own, and none of these suffixes is accepted as an RP ID (review S3). Update: take an upstream commit at least two weeks old, replace the file, set `PUBLIC_SUFFIX_LIST_COMMIT`, `PUBLIC_SUFFIX_LIST_DATE` and `LIST_SHA256` in `psl.rs` (a test checks the checksum and runs the list's own test cases), then regenerate the vectors.
- **Regular-expression engine**: one linear-time engine for every client, `regex-lite` (rust-lang/regex; chosen over `regex` to keep the WASM ~315 KB smaller). Patterns are case-insensitive with ASCII classes and case folding, tested against the page's origin and path (at most 2048 bytes); at most 300 UTF-16 units and 64 KiB compiled; the last 200 compiled patterns are cached. Look-around, back-references and JavaScript-only forms such as `x{}` are refused and `regexError(pattern)` says why. There is no unsafe-pattern scan any more (review S13). `crates/core/tests/vectors.rs` runs `testdata/vectors.json` against the core and lists each difference with its reason (`KNOWN`); the vectors are to be generated from the core when the clients switch. WASM is built with `--remove-name-section --remove-producers-section` (`scripts/build-wasm.sh`).
- **Golden vectors**: `testdata/vectors.json` (version 2) is generated by `node testdata/generate-vectors.mjs` from the Rust core's WASM build (it builds into a temporary folder with `scripts/build-wasm.sh`, or uses `--wasm <dir>`; `--check` fails when the file is out of date). It pins `normalizeHost`; base domains and the standard public suffix (com for example.com) by the full list, with `publicSuffixList` = `publicSuffixListVersion()`; URL matching for every match type incl. unknown types and defaults, `androidapp://` addresses (which never match) and the private-section suffixes myfritz.net, myds.me, dedyn.io, cloudapp.net and EC2 regions; the downgrade rule and `regexError` messages (null = the pattern runs); the save plan, passkey "Save in" targets (an app address is no target) and RP IDs (those suffixes refused); and `signedSharing`: fixed accounts with their identity records, identities (statement bytes, hashes, fingerprints), share statements and signatures, member rosters (exact JSON and signed bytes), key commitments, roster chains and heads (incl. `rollback`, `roster_forked`, `roster_unauthorized`), member comparisons, roster roles, key pins and pin checks, epoch checks, epoch pins inside the key pins and their merge; the generator rebuilds every canonical byte string itself and verifies every signature with node:crypto Ed25519 and SHA-256, independently of the core. While generating, it asserts that no shared-vault regex matches, no plan selects a shared login, no shared login is preferred, and that `matchUrls`, `chosenSaveTarget` and `passkeyDefaultTarget` agree with the single-case functions. The file is checked natively by `crates/core/tests/vectors.rs` (exact agreement) and by the extension (`extension/test/vectors.test.ts`: through the wrappers, the match index, RP ID errors as the page sees them, and the suffix-list version of `extension/src/wasm`; `extension.yml` runs `generate-vectors.mjs --check --wasm extension/src/wasm`), `ios/Tests/GoldenVectorTests.swift` (iOS and macOS AutoFill) and Android `GoldenVectorsTest.kt` through their bindings and wrappers. There are no known differences any more.

## Firefox extension

Same code as the Chrome extension; `node build.mjs --firefox` (`pnpm build:firefox` / `pnpm package:firefox`) builds `extension/dist-firefox/` with the manifest made by `extension/firefox-manifest.mjs` from `public/manifest.json` (so both list the same scripts, permissions, commands and version):
- `background.scripts` (event page, `type: module`) instead of `service_worker`; no `offscreen` permission/page, no `minimum_chrome_version`, `externally_connectable` or `use_dynamic_url` (Firefox's moz-extension UUID is already random per install). `match_origin_as_fallback` and `world: "MAIN"` are kept (Firefox 128+).
- `browser_specific_settings.gecko`: id `passauth@fahed-alhammadi.github.io` (permanent on AMO, never change it), `strict_min_version` 140 (ESR), `data_collection_permissions.required` = `authenticationInfo` + `personallyIdentifyingInfo` (vault + account email go to the user's own server; Mozilla counts any data leaving the browser). Desktop only (no `gecko_android`; web-ext lint warns about Android, harmless).
- Runtime differences (`shared/browser.ts` `isFirefox()` = moz-extension URL): clipboard written by the background page itself (`background/clipboard.ts`; clear = timer + `clipboard-clear` alarm, since an idle event page unloads), closed shadow roots via `element.openOrClosedShadowRoot` (`content/fields.ts`), `senders.ts` accepts `moz-extension:` pages, "default password manager" = `privacy.services.passwordSavingEnabled` only, shortcuts via `commands.openShortcutSettings()` (else instructions), browser name in UI text and the device name.
- AMO rebuilds from source and expects identical output: `scripts/firefox-source.sh` packs only the files the build reads + `README.md` (= `extension/FIREFOX_SOURCE.md`) + `BUILD_INFO.txt` (tool versions); `scripts/build-wasm.sh` remaps the checkout and cargo paths in the .wasm (`--remap-path-prefix`, via `CARGO_ENCODED_RUSTFLAGS`, which replaces `.cargo/config.toml`'s rustflags, so the getrandom cfg is repeated there). Checked 2026-11-21: a rebuild from the archive in another folder gave the same zip bytes.
- Listing metadata for the first upload + reviewer notes: `extension/amo-metadata.json`. Not verified yet: sign-in/fill/passkeys against a real server in Firefox (smoke test only: install, welcome page, background messaging, clipboard copy, Rust core, MAIN-world shim).

## Safari extension (desktop app, macOS)

The browser extension runs in Safari on a Mac from inside the desktop app: `PassAuth.app/Contents/PlugIns/PassAuthSafari.appex` (bundle id `io.passauth.desktop.safari`), released with the desktop app (`vX.Y.Z`, dmg + updater). No App Store app and no iPhone/iPad version: on iOS the PassAuth app's AutoFill extension fills in Safari. A Safari web extension in a Developer ID app is listed by Safari when the extension is sandboxed (tested 2026-11-21 with a notarized test app, also with an unsandboxed containing app like the desktop app; an unsandboxed extension is silently ignored: pluginkit doesn't list it).
- Extension: the same code, `node build.mjs --safari` (`pnpm build:safari`) → `extension/dist-safari/` with the manifest from `extension/safari-manifest.mjs`: non-persistent `background.scripts` event page (DOM: the background writes the clipboard itself, as in Firefox), version = `desktop/package.json`, `strict_min_version` 17.0, `nativeMessaging` added. Left out: `webauthn.js` + `bridge.js` (passkeys stay with the macOS AutoFill extension; two providers would mean two prompts), `offscreen`, `contextMenus` and `commands` (Safari on a Mac has both: not tried yet), the optional `privacy` permission, `match_origin_as_fallback` (nothing fills in about:blank/srcdoc frames), `use_dynamic_url` (frame URLs fixed per install; the frames still check their nonce), Chrome-only keys.
- Runtime (`shared/browser.ts` `isSafari()` = `safari-web-extension:` URL): `chrome.idle`/`commands`/`contextMenus` uses are optional (`main.ts`, `menus.ts`); Settings and the welcome page leave out shortcuts, the right-click menu and "default password manager" (`browserFeatures()`); the popup asks to allow PassAuth on a website whose address Safari hides from it (`needsSiteAccess`).
- Native side (`desktop/macos/Safari/`, target `PassAuthSafari` in `desktop/macos/project.yml`, built and signed by `desktop/macos/build.sh` after AutoFill): `SafariWebExtensionHandler` answers `runtime.sendNativeMessage(…, {type:"handoff"})` (`extension/src/shared/app-handoff.ts`) with server, email and Secret Key from the desktop's existing AutoFill hand-off item (`io.passauth.desktop.handoff` in `<team>.io.passauth.desktop.autofill`; `Handoff.swift`), never tokens or keys; the extension still signs in itself. The build phase `desktop/macos/scripts/copy-safari-extension.sh` copies `extension/dist-safari` into the appex (`PA_EXTENSION_DIST`). Entitlements: `Safari/Safari.entitlements` (sandbox, its App ID, the AutoFill Keychain group: restricted → Developer ID profile `Keys/MACOS_SAFARI_PROVISIONING_PROFILE.provisionprofile`, secret of the same name, expires 2027-12-05 with the cert), ad hoc `Safari-adhoc.entitlements` (sandbox only) for unsigned builds.
- CI (`desktop.yml`): the "macOS extensions" check builds the Safari target; the release build runs `pnpm build:safari` before `build.sh`, embeds `PassAuthSafari.appex` via `bundle.macOS.files` and checks it is sandboxed and carries `manifest.json`. Without the secret the app is built without the Safari extension (warning).
- Not verified yet: Safari listing it inside the real PassAuth.app, the hand-off at runtime, background restarts, inline menu and save prompt frames, Safari's per-site permission.

## Push notifications (sign-in requests)

Server `crates/server/src/push.rs`, relay `relay/` (Cloudflare Worker), iOS and Android apps (see their sections).

- A signed-in phone app registers the push token of its session with `PUT /account/push {platform:"apns", token, environment:"production"|"sandbox"}` (iPhone; token = 64–200 hex characters, stored lower case) or `{platform:"fcm", token, environment:"production"}` (Android; FCM registration token, 100–512 of `[A-Za-z0-9_:-]`, kept as given: case-sensitive) → 204 (400 otherwise; 409 `push_disabled` while the switch is off) and forgets it with `DELETE /account/push` → 204 (also while off).
- Table `push_tokens` (TEXT/BIGINT only): `session_id` (primary key: one token per session, a new one replaces it), `user_id` (indexed), `platform`, `token` (indexed; registering a token deletes any other row with it, so a phone that signs in again or to another account is woken once, for the new session), `environment`, `created_at`, `updated_at`.
- Rows go with their session: sign-out, refresh-token reuse, revoking one or the other sessions, forgetting a device, a new master password, the admin revoking the sessions or disabling the account (`push::drop_orphans` in the same transaction), account deletion (all of the user's rows), session expiry (the sweeper drops rows without a session every minute, and every row while the switch is off); turning the switch off on the admin page deletes every row, and the apps register again once `push` is listed.
- Sending: exactly where the account's apps get `{"type":"auth_request"}` (a request with an account behind the email, within the 5 shown per 15 minutes), `auth_requests::create` starts a task after the request is committed (`push::notify_auth_request`); the answer to the new device never waits for it and keeps its 100 ms floor, so nothing about the email shows in its timing (only starting the task differs, inside the floor).
- The task reads the user's tokens of live sessions (joined with `sessions`, at most 20, newest first) and sends one `POST <push_relay_url>/v1/notify {platform, token, environment, kind:"auth_request"}` per token (`apns` and `fcm` rows) (rustls with the built-in roots, HTTP/1.1, `User-Agent: PassAuth-Server/<version>`, 10 s each, all at once): 2xx = sent; 410 `unregistered` deletes that row; 429 is logged at debug, 400/502/other answers and connection failures at warn; the token is never logged.
- The relay (Cloudflare Worker, default `https://passauth-push.uaealhammadi.com`, run by the publisher, who holds the APNs key; `GET /v1/health`) signs an APNs request (topic `io.passauth.app`, push type alert, priority 10, expires in 15 minutes, collapse id `passauth-signin`, thread `signin`, default sound) with a fixed text ("Sign-in request" / "Someone wants to sign in to your PassAuth account on a new device. Open PassAuth to review.") and custom payload `{"passauth":{"kind":"auth_request"}}`: no request id, server, account or email. For `fcm` it signs in to Google with the Firebase service account (secret `FCM_SERVICE_ACCOUNT`, project `passauth-7312f`; RS256 assertion → OAuth access token for the `firebase.messaging` scope, reused until 5 minutes before expiry, renewed once on 401) and sends an FCM HTTP v1 **data-only** message `{data:{kind:"auth_request"}, android:{priority:HIGH, ttl:"900s", collapse_key:"passauth-signin"}}`: no text at all goes through Google; the Android app shows the same fixed notice itself. FCM 404/`UNREGISTERED`, `SENDER_ID_MISMATCH` and a 400 naming the registration token → 410; 429 → 429; others → 502.
- The relay sees the device token, the environment, the time and the server's IP address. Tapping opens the app, which unlocks and shows the usual sign-in request sheet from the authenticated listing; the approval protocol is unchanged, so a relay (or anyone who forges a notification) can only make a phone look at its own list.
- Admin page (Rules → Features): "Push notifications for sign-in requests" (`push_enabled`) and "Push relay address" (`push_relay_url`: `https://` with a host, or `http://` to localhost/127.0.0.1/[::1] for tests and a relay on the same host; no user name, query or fragment; saved without a trailing `/`); `GET /admin/settings` also returns `push_relay_default`.
- `.env` seeds them until the admin saves one: `PASSAUTH_PUSH=off` (default on) and `PASSAUTH_PUSH_RELAY_URL` (optional; an invalid value stops the start with a clear error). The apps register only while the account's server lists `push` (details in the iOS and Android sections).

## Offline changes (waiting to sync)

Apps that keep an offline copy let the user **create an item, edit an item or move an item to the trash** while the server can't be reached; the change waits on the device and is sent later. Scope: iOS app (and saves from iOS AutoFill), Android app (and saves from its autofill and credential services), desktop app, browser extension. Not: the web vault in a browser tab and the macOS AutoFill extension (their writes fail as before). Everything else (moves between vaults, restore, delete for good, empty trash, sharing, attachments, Send, settings) still needs a connection: "You're offline. This needs a connection." No server change: the apps use the ordinary item requests (`POST /items` with the app's own item id, `PUT /items/{id}` with `revision`, `DELETE /items/{id}` = trash), with manifests as for any write.

**When a change waits.** A save goes to the list instead of the server when the app already knows it is offline (the OS says so, or the last request couldn't reach the server), or when the request fails without an answer from the server (no network, DNS, connection refused or reset, TLS handshake, timeout) or with 502/503/504. Never for any other answer (4xx, 500, 507): those are shown as now. The editor closes as after a normal save; the item shows "Waiting to sync".

**The list** (per account; JSON `{"version": 1, "changes": [...]}`, each change):
`{"id": <random change id>, "kind": "create"|"edit"|"trash", "vault_id", "item_id", "base_revision": <the server revision the change started from; null for create>, "item": <the decrypted item as the editor saved it (create/edit); null for trash>, "sent": {"key", "data", "key_epoch"} | null (the exact body of the last attempt that may have reached the server), "created_at": <ms>, "state": "waiting"|"held"|"conflict", "reason": <text shown for held/conflict, else null>}`.
Kept only sealed with the account key: core `seal_offline_changes` / `open_offline_changes` (WASM `sealOfflineChanges` / `openOfflineChanges`, AAD `passauth/offline-changes/v1/<account id>`, ≤ 8 MiB), so it needs an unlocked account to read or change, like the vault itself. At most **500** changes: then "Too many changes are waiting. Connect to the internet before making more." Stored next to the offline copy: iOS = the shared app-group container under the existing cross-process lock (AutoFill adds, the app sends); Android = SecureStore; desktop = the desktop's own storage seam (`web/src/lib/platform.ts`; off in the browser web vault); extension = `storage.local` `offlineChanges:<account id>`.

**One change per item.** A new change to an item that already has one is merged into it: create + edit = create with the new item; edit + edit = edit (first `base_revision`, newest item); edit + trash = trash (first `base_revision`); create + trash = the change is removed (nothing reaches the server), unless `sent` is set (it may exist on the server), then it becomes a trash with `base_revision` null and `sent` kept. A trashed item can't be edited offline (restore needs a connection).

**Showing it.** Lists, search, fill/autofill and item pages show the offline copy with the list applied on top (created items appear, edits show the new content, trashed items are in the trash). Each changed item carries a small marker: "Waiting to sync" (waiting), "Not sent: <reason>" (held), "Changed on another device" (conflict). The sync status says "N changes waiting to sync" / "N changes need your decision". An item page with a change has **Discard this change** (confirm: "Your change to this item will be lost."), which removes it from the list. The item's rev memory (item edit counter) is not changed by waiting changes.

**Sending** (one sender per account at a time; while the account is unlocked; after every sync whose checks passed and when the connection comes back; oldest first; stop at the first failure without an answer and try later):
- Hold (state `held`, reason shown, sent again when things change) when the vault is gone, the user can no longer write to it, or the vault has a warning (signed-sharing checks, consent pending, manifest `rollback`/`mismatch`). Never send into a vault that didn't pass the checks of this sync.
- **create**: the server copy has no item with this id → encrypt `item` now with the vault's current key and epoch (core `encryptItem`), next manifest with `added` = that row, `POST /items`; save `sent` before the request. 409 `item_exists`: the stored row has `data` = `sent.data` → done; otherwise give the item a new id once and try again. The server copy already has the id with `data` = `sent.data` → done.
- **edit**: the server row is gone (deleted for good) → conflict "deleted". Its `revision` = `base_revision` → `updateItem(current row, item)` (so `rev` counts up from the stored one), `PUT` with that revision and the next manifest. Its `data` = `sent.data` → done. Otherwise → conflict.
- **trash**: the row is gone or already in the trash → done. `revision` = `base_revision` (or `base_revision` null and `data` = `sent.data`) → trash request with the next manifest. Otherwise → conflict.
- **A landed attempt isn't the end of the change.** A server row whose `data` equals `sent.data` means that attempt was stored; the change is done only when its current `item` equals that stored item (decrypted; `rev` and `format` ignored). If the user changed it since (a merge after the attempt), it goes on as an edit at the row's revision (a trash: trashed at that revision). A successful send removes the change only if it still holds what was sent; a change merged in during the request stays, with the new `sent`. A list that can't be opened is never overwritten (writes are refused, "Changes waiting on this device can't be read."), except when it doesn't decrypt for this account, which drops it with a notice.
- `revision_conflict` → sync, decide again (at most 3 times, then conflict). `manifest_conflict` / `vault_key_changed` → sync, try again (at most 3 times, then held). Any other error → held with the app's wording of the error code. Success → apply the answer to the offline copy exactly like an online write (manifest link rule included) and remove the change.

**Conflicts: the user decides, every time** (nothing is sent until they do). Decision sheet "Changed on another device": both versions (this device's and the server's), with the fields that differ marked (passwords hidden until shown), then:
- edit: **Keep mine** (`updateItem` over the server's current row, sent as an edit at its revision), **Keep the other** (remove the change), **Keep both** (send mine as a new item in the same vault with a new id, name + " (offline copy)"; the server's stays as it is).
- edit of an item deleted on another device: **Save mine as a new item** / **Discard mine**.
- trash: **Move to trash anyway** / **Keep the other**.

**Leaving.** Lock keeps the list (it can't be read until the next unlock). Log out, Change server, removing the account from the app and deleting the account warn first when the list isn't empty ("N changes haven't been sent and will be lost.") and then delete it. Several accounts: one list each.

## Android app (`android/`)
- Gradle project with two modules. `:core` is an Android library holding the UniFFI bindings (`io.passauth.core`) and `libpassauth_ffi.so` (arm64-v8a, x86_64), both generated by `scripts/build-android-core.sh` and gitignored, plus JNA. `:app` is the app (`io.passauth.app`, minSdk 34, compile/target 36). Crypto only ever comes from the Rust core; items, bundles and KDF params cross as JSON in the server/web shape (`app/.../data/Item.kt` mirrors `web/src/lib/types.ts`).
- Build: `scripts/build-android-core.sh && cd android && ./gradlew :app:assembleDebug :app:testDebugUnitTest` (JVM tests don't load the native library). Release: `./gradlew :app:bundleRelease`, signed only when `ANDROID_UPLOAD_KEYSTORE_PATH`, `ANDROID_UPLOAD_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD`, `ANDROID_UPLOAD_KEY_ALIAS` and `ANDROID_UPLOAD_KEY_PASSWORD` are set. Debug builds use the `io.passauth.app.debug` id.
- Sign-in request notifications (`app/.../push/`): Firebase Cloud Messaging set up by hand (`firebase-messaging`, no google-services plugin, no analytics; `FirebaseInitProvider` removed, auto-init/analytics/ad-id/data collection off in the manifest). `android/app/google-services.json` (gitignored; CI writes it from secret `GOOGLE_SERVICES_JSON`, tag builds fail without it) is read by Gradle (`buildSrc/FirebaseClientConfig.kt`), which picks the client matching the variant's applicationId and writes `FIREBASE_APP_ID`/`FIREBASE_API_KEY`/`FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID`/`FIREBASE_SENDER_ID` BuildConfig fields; without the file (or without Google Play services) push is hidden and everything else works. `PushRegistrar` registers `{platform:"fcm", token, environment:"production"}` once per token and account while the server lists `push` and the switch (Settings → Account security, on by default once the `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` permission is granted) is on; log out DELETEs first (`VaultRepository.beforeSignOut`, 3 s cap). The relay sends a data-only message; `PassAuthMessagingService` posts the fixed notice on channel `sign_in_requests` (high importance); a tap opens `MainActivity`, which fetches pending requests after unlock (same sheet as before). Offer card in the vault + Notifications row on the first-launch permissions screen, as on iOS.
- Version: `versionName` = `android/VERSION` (the Android app's own version; `scripts/release.sh android X.Y.Z`), `versionCode` = major·1 000 000 + minor·1 000 + patch (`android/buildSrc/src/main/kotlin/AppVersion.kt`, shared with the unit tests).
- Data layer (`app/.../data`): `VaultRepository` mirrors the extension's `background/vault.ts` (prelogin → OPAQUE → 2FA → bundle unlock → `/sync` → decrypt; create/update/trash; lock/logout; up to 5 accounts). `ApiClient` pins each request to one account and rotates refresh tokens on 401. `SecureStore` keeps tokens, settings and per-account data in a DataStore file encrypted with an Android Keystore AES-GCM key, plus an encrypted copy of the last `/sync` response (ciphertext) so the vault opens offline. Unlocked keys live only in memory as Rust handles.
- Delta sync (`data/DeltaSync.kt`, pure, JVM-tested in `DeltaSyncTest`; mirrors iOS `Shared/DeltaSync.swift`), only when `/api/config` `features` has `delta_sync` (kept in `AccountData.features`/`featuresAt`, asked again after 6 h, cleared by Change server; a stale list is harmless because a server without the feature ignores `since`). The encrypted offline copy is a `SyncSnapshot`: the last `/sync` answer with every later change merged in, in the shape of a full answer, so the cursor and each vault's `seq` are written in the same file write (tmp + rename) as the items they describe. Syncs ask `GET /sync?since=<cursor>&trash=summary` (the app never shows the trash). `SyncMerge.apply`: an answer without `delta: true` replaces the copy; a delta drops `deleted` rows (only from the vault each names), drops every row of the `reset` vaults, upserts `items` by id, then drops rows of vaults missing from `vaults`; headers, revision, cursor, user and bundle come from the answer. Validation as before plus: only trashed rows may lack `item_key`/`data`; an unreadable answer is an error, not "offline", and leaves copy and cursor as they were. Decryption is incremental (`decryptVault(…, previous)`): a vault key is kept while `wrapped_key`, `key_kind` and `key_epoch` are unchanged, otherwise opened again (replaced keys destroyed); names are decrypted again only when `meta` changed, items only when their ciphertext changed or their vault key was reopened. After the app's own create, in-place update and trash, `vault_seq` decides (`SyncDecision.afterWrite`): the copy's number + 1 → applied to the copy as sent (`SyncMerge.applyLocal`; cursor and revision kept); equal → nothing; anything else → a delta sync. Public syncs go through `SyncQueue` (one in flight, at most one waiting). Storage (Settings → Account security, only when the admin set a limit): `GET /account` `usage`/`limits` as "120 of 500 items" / "1.2 MB of 50 MB" with bars (`AccountUsage`), and 403 `quota_exceeded` → "You've reached this server's storage limit. Delete items or ask the server's admin." (editor, autofill save sheet and toasts, passkey saves).
- Unlock: master password (+ stored Secret Key), or biometrics: `Account.toSessionBytes()` (64 bytes) is wrapped by a per-account Keystore key that requires strong biometric auth and is invalidated when biometrics change. Vault timeout and action like the extension (`LockManager`); with "never" the session bytes are kept in the encrypted store.
- Hardening: no backup (`allowBackup=false`, extraction rules exclude everything), https only except `localhost`/`10.0.2.2` (network security config), `FLAG_SECURE` while unlocked or showing the Secret Key, clipboard copies marked sensitive and cleared after the user's delay, no secrets in logs.
- Autofill service (`app/.../autofill`, `PassAuthAutofillService`): `StructureParser` reads the AssistStructure (visible enabled text fields, `webDomain`/`webScheme`, only fields of the focused site's frame; `StructureParser.collect` works on `StructureNode`s so it is JVM-tested); `FieldClassifier` finds username/password/one-time-code fields from autofill hints, input type, HTML attributes and id/hint text (patterns of the extension's `fields.ts`). One-time-code fields (2026-11-19): OTP hints (`one-time-code`, `smsOTPCode`, `emailOTPCode`, `2faAppOTPCode`, any hint containing "otp"), otp/totp/2fa/verification names, a "code" field that is short (`maxLength` 4–10, from `maxTextLength` or HTML `maxlength`) or digits-only (never postal/promo/coupon/captcha… codes), or a 6-digit number box with nothing else said. A screen with only a code field gets one "Verification code" dataset per matching login with an authenticator key (fresh code at selection). Compatibility mode (`res/xml/autofill_service.xml` `<compatibility-package>`, maxLongVersionCode 10000000000, only browsers on the privileged allowlist: Samsung Internet, Edge, Brave, Opera/Opera Mini, DuckDuckGo, Vivaldi, Firefox/Fenix/Focus, Whale, HeyTap, Yandex, Vanadium, Silk, Seznam, Waterfox); Chrome is left out: it ignores compatibility mode and asks autofill services itself once "Autofill using another service" is on: Android reads such a browser's page through accessibility and sets the web domain on the address bar only. `collect` treats a text leaf (no HTML info) carrying its own web domain as the address bar: never a field; its domain is the page's only when no web content reported one and all address bars agree, and the page's fields are then the ones without a domain. Known address-bar resource ids (`FieldClassifier.URL_BAR_IDS`) are never login fields. Trust is unchanged: web content only from allowlisted browsers. Chrome (135+) uses a separate autofill service only with Chrome → Settings → Autofill services → "Autofill using another service": Settings → Autofill and the first-launch screen show a "Chrome" row (only when Chrome is installed) whose button opens Chrome's autofill settings (`ACTION_APPLICATION_PREFERENCES` + `CATEGORY_APP_BROWSER` + `CATEGORY_PREFERENCE`, package set; else Chrome itself). `FillTarget.resolve` decides what may match: web content in a browser on the privileged allowlist (package + signing cert) or in a WebView whose domain's asset links list the app → by website (`UrlMatcher`, default-match setting; scheme missing → https; Android gives no path, so "exact"/"starts with" see `scheme://domain/`); any other app → logins with the website `androidapp://<package>`, plus (2026-11-19, `AppLinks.kt`) logins saved for a website that vouches for the app: candidate base domains come from the package name (`com.instagram.android` → `instagram.com`, reversed labels without filler parts like android/app/mobile/client/lite; or a distinctive package part equal to a login domain's first label, `com.amazon.mShop…` → `amazon.de`), kept only when the vault has logins there, at most 3; `AutofillEngine.linked` checks each one's asset links at `https://<domain>/` then `www.<domain>` (relation `get_login_creds` or `handle_all_urls`, package + installed signing cert, `AssetLinksVerifier` cache first, parallel fetches, ≤2 s and within the 5 s reply budget; the service gives it what is left until 4 s after the request) and only verified domains become `FillTarget.App.linkedDomains` (matched by base domain; never/regex websites don't count). A package-name guess alone never fills or saves anything (any app can pick a package name): it only puts those logins first in the picker. The save rule sees linked logins as the app's (a changed password updates the website login); new logins from apps are still saved with `androidapp://<package>`. A browser's own UI and PassAuth itself → nothing. Web logins never match apps and vice versa; the extension's https→http downgrade refusal applies. The PassAuth server's own web vault is an ordinary website (a login saved for the server's address is offered and saved there). Unlocked: one Dataset per login (drop-down + keyboard inline suggestions via `androidx.autofill`) plus "Open PassAuth". Locked: one "PassAuth is locked" entry whose authentication IntentSender opens `AutofillAuthActivity`, which unlocks (master password or biometrics) and returns the datasets in `EXTRA_AUTHENTICATION_RESULT`. Logins with a TOTP get per-dataset authentication: picking one runs `AutofillAuthActivity` (see-through, no UI when unlocked), which returns the filled Dataset with a fresh code (also filled into a one-time-code field) and copies it when "Copy verification code automatically" is on — the only reliable hook for running code at the moment of selection. Save: `SaveInfo` (password required, username optional, save when the form disappears, "Never" button → never-save list keyed by base domain or `androidapp://<package>`); `onSaveRequest` applies the save rule (see Features; `SaveRule.kt`) when unlocked: new logins and rule-1/rule-2 updates silently, a rule-3 update only after the "Update login?" sheet (opened through the IntentSender); Android's save dialog carries a `CustomDescription` line saying whether a saved login will be updated or a new one added (chosen by conditions on the typed user name; only when the vault was unlocked at fill time and the place already has a login). Locked → returns an IntentSender to unlock first (credentials stay in memory, never in the intent), then an "Update login?" / "Save login?" sheet with "Save in" when there is something to choose; if Android can no longer open that sheet (the filled activity is gone), the pending save is applied at the next unlock of the same account within the 5-minute in-memory window (`SaveRule.withoutSheet`: a rule-3 update becomes a new login). Pending saves are bound to the account, removed on every exit and cleared on lock, logout and account switch; the consent sheets set `setHideOverlayWindows(true)` (permission `HIDE_OVERLAY_WINDOWS`). Passkey "Save in" (all clients) preselects only a personal-vault login for the exact host, else "New login"; shared-vault logins are listed with their vault's name. Respects "Ask to save and update logins". Always the active account. "Choose a login…" (apps; websites keep "Add this site to a login…", both `AddSite.offerEntry`): on a sign-in form (password field, or for an app also a user-name-only step) with no matching login, opens the picker (unlock first if needed; search; the package-name guesses first), then "Use <login> for <app> from now on?": "Fill and remember" adds `androidapp://<package>` to the login (existing AddSite rules: editable logins only, shared-vault warning) and fills it; "Fill once" fills without changing the login. `<queries>` lists launcher apps (MAIN/LAUNCHER) so `AutofillEngine.appLabel` and the passkey prompts can read app names (a login saved from an app is named after the app, not its package).
- Host names: `data/Idna.kt` converts like the WHATWG URL parser (UTS #46 nontransitional; CheckBidi/CheckJoiners on, hyphen and DNS-length errors ignored, forbidden domain code points refused) for `UrlMatcher` and `WebAuthn.canonicalDomain` (RP IDs), so "straße.de" is "xn--strae-oqa.de" (`java.net.IDN`, IDNA2003, gave "strasse.de"). The app uses `android.icu.text.IDNA` behind `Idna.Engine`; all-ASCII names without `xn--` labels are only lower-cased. JVM tests install ICU4J 79.1 (`testImplementation`) with `Idna.use` (`TestIdna.install()`).
- Credential Manager provider (`app/.../credentials`, Android 14+, `androidx.credentials`): `PassAuthCredentialService` offers a `CreateEntry` for ES256/platform passkey requests, `PublicKeyCredentialEntry`s for matching passkeys (rpId, allowCredentials or discoverable) and `PasswordCredentialEntry`s (same matching as autofill); locked → an `AuthenticationAction`. `CredentialActivity` does the work after the user picks: origin check (`Origins`): privileged callers' origin from `CallingAppInfo.getOrigin(allowlist)` with `res/raw/privileged_allowlist.json` = Google Password Manager's published list (https://www.gstatic.com/gpm-passkeys-privileged-apps/apps.json, linked from the provider guide; Chrome stable/beta/dev/canary, Firefox, Edge, Brave, Samsung, …) and the RP ID checked against the origin like the extension's `effectiveRpId`; apps get `android:apk-key-hash:<b64url sha256(cert)>` and must be listed in `https://<rpId>/.well-known/assetlinks.json` (`delegate_permission/common.get_login_creds`, package + cert fingerprint; https, no redirects, 5 s, 256 KiB, cached 1 h; `include` not followed) or the request is refused. User verification: unlocking in the flow counts, otherwise BiometricPrompt (biometric or screen lock). excludeCredentials is checked after consent (InvalidStateError). Keys and signatures come from the core (`passkeyCreate`/`passkeyAssert`); clientDataJSON is built with `passkeyClientDataJson` for apps (and hashed), while browsers' `clientDataHash` is signed as given and the built JSON is only the placeholder the guide asks for. Responses are WebAuthn JSON; passkeys are stored like the extension's `storePasskey` (same fields, counter 0, discoverable), on the same site+username login or a new login for `https://<rpId>`.
- Settings → Autofill shows whether PassAuth is the autofill service and the passkey provider, with buttons to Android's settings, the Chrome row (see autofill), plus default URI match, TOTP copy, clipboard clearing, "Ask to save and update logins" and the never-save list. Debug-only test hooks (gradle properties, never in release builds): `passauth.testAssetLinksBase` (asset links from a local server) and `passauth.testPrivilegedApp` (trust one more browser, e.g. a Chromium snapshot). `:samples:login` is a debug-only test app (login form + Credential Manager calls), not part of the app or its bundle.
- Account deletion (Google Play requirement): Settings → About → Delete account. The master password is proven to the server with an OPAQUE login with `purpose: "reauth"` on the signed-in session (like the web vault's `reauth`), then `DELETE /api/account` with `x-reauth-token`; the account is then forgotten locally like Log out (biometric key, offline copy, stored Secret Key and two-step remember token removed; the last account keeps only the server address).
- Item editor TOTP field: type, "Paste" (clipboard) or "Scan QR code" (CameraX preview + ImageAnalysis, latest frame only, decoded in memory by ZXing core; no Play services; frames are never stored and the luminance buffer is wiped on close; the preview is a TextureView inside a dialog that inherits FLAG_SECURE). The `CAMERA` permission is requested only on tap; denial shows how to type or paste the key and a link to Android settings. `otpauth://totp/…` URIs are stored whole (scheme lower-cased for the core), plain base32 keys cleaned (upper case, no spaces); `OtpAuth` mirrors the core's TOTP checks so accepted keys always produce codes. HOTP and `otpauth-migration://` are refused with an explanation.
- Item page: "Password history" (count) opens the previous passwords (`password_history`, newest first, masked with reveal and copy). The overflow menu moves an item to another vault where the user is owner, admin or writer: re-encrypted with the target vault key under the same id and `PUT /api/items/{id}` with the new `vault_id` and the old revision (the web vault's `updateItem(entry, item, vaultId)`).
- Live sync (`LiveSync`): while MainActivity is started and the active account is unlocked, a WebSocket to `/api/ws?access_token=…` (OkHttp, 25 s pings). `{"type":"hello","origin"}` is remembered (`LiveSync.origin`) but never used to skip messages. `{"type":"sync","revision","vaults"}` syncs (`SyncDecision.onLiveSync`) when `vaults` is empty (profile or account change) or names a vault whose number is above the copy's or that the copy doesn't have, whoever wrote it (this device's own writes are already in the copy); without `vaults` (older servers), when the revision is above the copy's. `{"type":"logout"}` makes the app check its session (an ended one signs the account out through the refresh-token path). Disconnects on background, lock or account switch; reconnects with backoff (1 s doubling to 60 s, jitter), each time first calling `refreshIfStale` (`/sync/revision`, `SyncDecision.isStale`: with vault numbers any other revision or any different, new or missing vault number; without them a newer revision), so an expired access token is refreshed and missed changes are caught up. The item editor keeps the item as it was when editing started, so a live update during editing surfaces as the usual revision conflict instead of being overwritten.
- Background sync (`data/BackgroundSync.kt`; Settings → Vault options → "Background sync": Off/30 min/1 h/2 h (default)/6 h/12 h, `Settings.backgroundSyncMinutes`): WorkManager unique periodic work `background-sync` (interval = setting, flex ≤ 15 min, constraints connected + battery not low; `PassAuthApp` re-schedules it from the state flow on start, setting change, sign-in and last sign-out) runs `VaultRepository.refreshAllCaches()` in the app's own process: the active account, if unlocked, gets a normal `syncLocked()`; every other signed-in account gets `refreshCacheLocked` = `DeltaSync.refresh` through the usual `ApiClient` (same refresh/401 path, so no parallel refresh that the server would flag as token reuse): with delta sync and a copy that has a cursor, `/sync/revision` first (current → only `lastSync` is updated), otherwise or when stale `GET /sync?since=…&trash=summary` (or the whole `/sync`) merged into the copy without decrypting anything; written only when it is a valid snapshot with the bundle and not older than the copy, with `lastSync`/`bundle`/name. Nothing is decrypted or kept in memory; the next unlock is cache-first and sees the fresh copy. Retried only when every account hit a network error.
- Export: Settings → Vault options → Export vault. Formats come from the core (`exportVaultEncrypted` (≥10-character export password, Argon2id), `exportVaultJson`, `exportVaultCsv` (logins and notes only)), with warnings for the unencrypted ones. The master password is checked locally against the bundle first; the file is written only to the location the user picks with `ACTION_CREATE_DOCUMENT` (`passauth-export-YYYY-MM-DD[-protected].json` / `.csv`).
- Account security (Settings → Account security, `ui/settings/AccountSecurityScreens.kt` + `SecurityViewModel`; request logic in `data/AccountSecurity.kt`, JVM-tested with a fake server and fake crypto, `CoreSecurityCrypto` = the core): "Change master password" mirrors the web vault's `changeKeys` (reauth → `Account.rewrap` → `/account/keys/start` → `opaqueRegisterFinish` → `/account/keys/finish` with `x-reauth-token`), with the web meter (Weak <50 bits, asked again before use; Fair/Good/Strong at 50/65/80) and a Secret Key choice (keep / turn on / turn off; also a separate Turn on/off with the master password). Afterwards `Stored.afterKeyChange` replaces the stored bundle and Secret Key (removed when turned off) and drops the two-step remember token; the session, biometric blob and "never" keys hold the account key, which a re-wrap keeps, so they stay valid; a sync refreshes the offline copy. A new Secret Key is shown once (Copy, Emergency Kit PDF via `PdfDocument` saved with SAF, "I saved it" before Done). "Two-step login" reads `GET /account`: authenticator setup (QR drawn from ZXing's encoder, grouped setup key, Copy, `otpauth://` intent), email codes, regenerate recovery codes and turn a method off (both after reauth); recovery codes are shown once in a dialog that back/outside can't close until "I saved them" (Copy all, Share, Save). Secrets stay in the ViewModel (never the saved-instance state) and are dropped when the screens are left.
- Signed-in devices (Settings → Account security, `SessionsScreen`): `GET /account/sessions` with each session's device name, app from `client` (`SessionText`, the web vault's names: "Chrome extension 0.6.0", "Desktop app 0.7.0", "Web", "Android app 0.6.0", "Mac AutoFill 0.7.0"; device kind without a client), sign-in date and last use; "This device", "Sign out" for the others (`DELETE /account/sessions/{id}`) and "Sign out other sessions" (`POST /account/sessions/revoke-others`, only when `/api/config` `features` has `revoke_others`), each confirmed in a `DecisionSheet`. Rate limits: `ApiException.retryAfter` (429 `Retry-After`, seconds or HTTP date; a non-JSON 429 is a rate limit too, `failedResponse`) → "Too many attempts. Try again in N seconds/minutes." in `errorMessage`/`securityErrorMessage` (the extension's rounding), else the general text.
- Invitations, policies and devices (2026-11-15 round 5; `data/Invitations.kt` `InvitationsTest`, `data/Policies.kt` `PoliciesTest`): as on iOS — invitations kept in the `SyncSnapshot`, names opened from the sealed key in `decryptVault` (destroyed at once, cached), a dismissible notice, a "Vault invitations" row and `ui/settings/InvitationsScreen.kt` (sharer fingerprint, Accept/Decline in a `DecisionSheet`); policies in `AccountData.policies`, `policy_violations` notices, master-password minimum max(10, policy), `require_secret_key` forced on, `Stored.timeoutLimit` limiting the timeout choices and the `LockManager` (a saved choice above it lowered, `Settings.vaultTimeoutLowered`), `disable_export` hiding Export; the two-step gate (`ApiClient.onTwoStepRequired`, `syncOrGate`, `TwoStepRequiredScreen`, `continueAfterTwoStep`; live sync off while gated); "Forget…" per session (`AccountSecurity.forgetDevice`; `current: true` → `signOutLocally`).
- Web vault shortcuts open the account's server in the browser with path routes: Vault options → `/settings/vaults`, `/import`, `/vault`.
- Permissions (`app/.../ui/permissions`): on a first launch (never shown and no server connected yet; flag in `Stored.onboarding`, shared by all accounts) "Set up PassAuth" comes before the connect screen and lists only what the app uses, each optional with its own button: camera (runtime permission), autofill service and passkey provider (`SystemServices`, Android's settings screens), fingerprint / face unlock (`BiometricManager`; nothing to grant, so choosing it there offers biometric unlock once after the next sign-in or unlock, with one biometric confirmation; "Set up" opens enrollment when none is enrolled). "Continue" or "Skip for now" mark it shown. There is no Settings → Permissions page (removed 2026-11-19, user: later changes go through Android's settings; the camera is still asked when scanning a QR code, and a permanent denial offers Android's app settings); `PermissionModel` maps the camera to Allowed / Not allowed / Not asked / blocked ("Open settings" → app details) from granted + `shouldShowRequestPermissionRationale` + whether PassAuth ever asked (`onboarding.cameraAsked`, also set by the TOTP scanner). No notifications, location or contacts. Android 17's local network permission applies only to apps targeting SDK 37+ (with target 36 it comes with INTERNET), so it's not listed yet.
- Sign in with another device (2026-11-16 security round; `data/DeviceLogin.kt`, `data/AuthRequestInbox.kt`, `data/ShownText.kt`, `ui/AuthRequestSheet.kt`, `ui/auth/AuthScreens.kt` `DeviceCodeScreen`): new device: `DeviceLoginFlow` never waits more than 15 minutes after making the request, whatever `expires_at` says (plus 60 s of grace while the server can't be reached). An approval, from a poll (or, from servers before 0.16.1 only, after two-step login), is opened into an `ApprovedLogin` held in `VaultRepository.deviceCode`; nothing is unlocked, signed in or stored until the user enters the 6-digit code shown by the approving app (`confirmDeviceCode`, "Confirm the sign-in"): a wrong code says how many tries are left, and the third signs out the session the server opened and drops the keys. Once confirmed, the account unlocks, the remember-this-device token is kept, and a received Secret Key is stored only under the email typed on this device, only when the server signed in that same email, and never over a different stored key (`DeviceLogin.keepSecretKey`). Approving app: `AuthRequestInbox` runs a pure `ApprovalQueue`: one request at a time, frozen when its sheet opens (id, key, challenge, claims and the three `loginPhraseChoices`), newer requests wait, approved/denied/answered-elsewhere requests never come back from a stale listing; a listing that drops the shown id or gives it another key closes the sheet with a note to read first (the challenge text is never compared; the frozen challenge is opened for the proof); the Compose sheet is keyed by id + key only and answers run in the app's scope (rotation keeps them). Claims are shown as the device calls itself (`ShownText`, cleaned, 64 max). The choices take a tap 1 s after the sheet appears or the app resumes; "Not now" gets focus first. Wrong words are refused by the core (`PhraseMismatch`): the app denies the request and shows a blocking note with OK (a snackbar hid under the next sheet); the right words send `{sealed_key, proof}` and show the code ("123 456") until Done (Back and swipe can't close it).
- Overlays (security review F8): every decision sheet keeps other apps' overlays hidden (`SecureActivity.hideOverlays`, a reference-counted `Window.setHideOverlayWindows`; `ServiceActivity` holds it for its whole life).
- Errors (`data/ServerErrors.kt`, `ServerErrorsTest`): the app's own words for every known code (incl. 507 `insufficient_storage` and the attachment quota); otherwise a generic sentence, the request id as "Reference: …" and at most 200 cleaned characters of the server's message ("Server message: …"); `ApiException` carries the request id; the core's new `CoreException` cases are mapped; JSON parse errors (which quote the server's answer) are never shown. Turning on an authenticator app or email codes asks for the master password (+ Secret Key when not kept) in the setup card and sends a fresh reauth token. Live sync's `two_step_required` (message, or close 1008) raises the two-step gate and stops reconnecting. The item editor shows `regexError` or `regexWarning` under a website when `resolveMatch` is "regex"; invitations show "Fingerprint your server lists for <email>" + "Accept only invitations you expected."
- Accessibility: icon buttons have content descriptions, card titles and page titles are headings, label + value rows are read as one node before their actions, masked secrets read as "hidden", text drawn inside fixed shapes (avatar, TOTP ring) doesn't scale with the font, select fields wrap. Checked at font scale 2.0.

- Item extras (`data/Item.kt`): `Item.extra` and every nested type (`Login`, `LoginUrl`, `Passkey`, `Card`, `Identity`, `SshKey`, `Field`, `PasswordChange`, via `KeepUnknownSerializer`) keep fields the app doesn't model (`attachments`, newer fields) through edits, autofill updates and key replacement; a non-list `attachments` value is kept as written.
- Send (Settings → Send; `data/Sends.kt` `SendActions`, `ui/settings/SendsScreen.kt`, `SendsTest`): owner side only, same rules and words as `sends.ts`; the Argon2 password verifier runs off the main thread; the link's key stays hidden until Show. Links aren't opened in the app.
- Attachments (`data/Attachments.kt`, `FileTransfer.kt`, `AttachmentManager.kt`, `ui/vault/AttachmentsCard.kt`, `AttachmentsTest`): `FileEncryptor`/`FileDecryptor` in 1 MiB pieces; uploads are an exactly-sized one-shot streamed PUT, queued one at a time and stopped on lock (their attachment deleted); the item save is tried 3 times, else the attachment is deleted. Downloads decrypt to `cacheDir/attachments/<random>/<name>`, kept only after `finish()` and only while the same account stays unlocked (lock generation; download jobs are cancelled on lock); open/share via FileProvider (`${applicationId}.files`, that folder only, read grant) or the system save dialog; copies are deleted on lock, at app start, when the app stops and after 10 minutes. "Delete them" for unlisted uploads syncs first. The editor keeps the synced file list; exports strip it.
- Emergency access (`data/Emergency.kt`, `EmergencyClient.kt`, `ui/settings/EmergencyScreen.kt`; `EmergencyTest`/`EmergencyClientTest` port every case of the web's two test files): both roles as in the web vault. Roster memory per server + user id in `Stored.rosters` (`RosterMemory`), untouched by sign-out; each operation pins its account. The fill runs after any sync that changes vault ids, roles, kinds or key epochs. Removal and "Replace keys…" re-encrypt every item (full `/sync`, trash included, item `revision` sent) through `VaultRepository.rotateVaultKeys`; a vault whose name can't be decrypted is not rotated. The grantor's vaults open read-only in memory and never feed autofill.
- Signed sharing (`data/SignedSharing.kt`: `SharingSession` = checks, consent, invitations, `planRotation`; `RotationRules` pure; tests `SignedSharingTest`, `SignedSharingVectorsTest`): `VaultRepository.verifySharing` runs before `decryptVault` at every sync (full, delta, live, background, local writes); only `VaultCheck.Ok` vaults are opened, so refused or waiting vaults never reach the list, Autofill, Credential Manager or save targets. "Me" = the core-authenticated account id (`legacyIdentityJson`); a sync naming another account is refused. Memory `Stored.signing[server|accountId]` = `SigningMemory {pinsSeq, pinsSealed, rosters, signed, ownIdentity}`, written only through `merge`, survives sign-out, removed with the account. Admission via `pinsObserve`; consent shows in Vault invitations (`ConsentCard`, Accept/Decline; Decline writes a tombstone; `replaced` has no Decline); Settings → "Shared vaults and keys" lists refused vaults by name, changed keys, Mark as verified / Trust the new key for the shown identity hash, and the own fingerprint. Key replacements happen only from Emergency access and `rotation_pending`, always through `MemberReviewSheet` for roster version 1 and missing members (digest + confirmed user ids), from the state verified in the same sync (`RotationRules.checkBase`); emergency "personal" excludes vaults with a signed history (`sharedBefore`). Phones don't share, re-sign or accept unsigned new vaults (except in the trust-memory-missing state). Under R8, `KeepUnknownSerializer` keeps unknown nested item fields (release-checked 2026-11-16). Final round (2026-11-16): Accept binds to the check shown (`ConsentVault.token`, `Invitations.sameAsShown`; a changed check closes the sheet) and key pins are saved before the server is told (`committing{}`, rollback on failure); grandfathering reads the earlier offline copy when the session opens (`Session.earlierVaults`); identity hashes this phone generated are remembered (`SigningMemory.generated`) so a lost upload answer still counts as created, and `identity_exists` skips grandfathering for that sync; a failed key-pins fetch = offline (no writes, no grandfathering, vaults not admitted are UNCHECKED); an accepted shared vault that disappears from a verified sync is tombstoned (own account vaults excluded, a `left` tombstone would refuse them for good); no Decline while trust memory is missing; signed invitations name the checked signer; `email_conflict` trust needs the compared tick; memory keys use the normalized server address. Round 4 (2026-11-16): accepts use `pinsAccept` with one observation builder (`signed_share`, `paused`, `chain_hashes` whenever a chain was fetched, also for invitations); consents name a replaced key's signer (pinned email first) and show the key id when unsigned; Decline only for signed, non-replaced consents (`declineConsent(shown)`), unsigned ones are accepted by key id when allowed (`SigningMemory.missing` with reason device/memory) or held with "Ask the vault's owner to sign this vault."; until the first signed sync is done unsigned consent rows are held (`VaultCheck.Held`, Settings → "Still being checked"); tombstones need a 404 from `GET /vaults/{id}/members` or absence from two consecutive server syncs (`SigningMemory.absent`), and a decline never tombstones a vault in use; the earlier copy for grandfathering is `SigningMemory.usable` (vaults shown ok) with candidates recorded once at identity generation; `noPinsFound` only when the server answers none; unreadable trust memory refuses shared vaults, opens the server's pins read-only for own vaults (offline: own vaults pass unchecked — a stated limit) and blocks writes; with no memory, no signals and an unknown `/api/config`, Android leaves the checks off (the fresh-device limit below). Round 5 (2026-11-16): unsent key pins are kept on the phone (`SigningMemory.unsentSealed`/`unsentSeq`) until a PUT succeeds and are merged on top of every loaded copy; newer server copies merge with `pinsMergeLastKnown`, an `identity_mismatch` pauses sharing with no merge and no writes; own rotations are recorded with `pinsRecordRotation` and require readable, loaded pins; declines use `pins_decline` (nothing for consent rows or vaults listed this sync, version 0 otherwise) and Decline is offered only where the core says `declinable`; invitations send `invitation: true` and show the rejoin/held notes; sessions are restored only through `matchesBundle` (`AccountBundles`) and bundles are stored only when they match; a missing `key_pins_seq` triggers a fetch; removals are confirmed only by a 404.
