- Security round 7/8/9 (2026-11-18): own `account` wraps are written v2 (`wrapForBound` at `key_epoch + 1`) at key replacement (also emergency-access rotation) when that sync's `user.account_wraps_v2` is `true`, else v1 (registration stays v1; the app creates no other vaults); `pinsLegacyWrapAllowed` gets the row's epoch and checked `account` rows send `bound_wrap` when opened from a v2 wrap. Send v2 (`Sends.FEATURE_V2`): a password Send is encrypted with `sendPasswordKeys`' content key, `owner_content_key` lets the owner read/edit it, a password change re-encrypts both fields, removing it goes back to v1; without `send_v2` a password is neither set nor changed ("Update your PassAuth server to protect a Send with a password."), so no Send is written v1 with a password and a v2 Send never goes back to v1 while it keeps one; a Send whose fields and `owner_content_key` disagree is unreadable and not rewritten; unreadable v2 Sends keep Copy link/Turn off/Delete. Item rollback (`data/ItemRevs.kt`, `ItemRevsTest`): highest `rev` per item in `vault/<account>.revs` next to the offline copy (deleted with it; an item is forgotten only when this phone purged it or it is absent from a vault whose manifest passed for that answer, `ItemRevs.gone`; at most 20,000, the ones seen last), checked with `itemRevCheck` in `decryptVault` on every shown sync (full, delta, live, after writes); a locked/background refresh decrypts nothing and keeps the replaced rows in `SyncSnapshot.held` (≤2 per item, local only) for the next unlocked check; a rollback keeps the newer copy (stored under its own row) when there is one, marks the item ("Your server sent an older version of this item.") and the vault list shows the notice; the mark stays for as long as the row is that kept copy (`ItemRevs.isKept`: across syncs, own writes, lock and unlock) until it is saved again or the server sends that version or a newer one, and a kept copy whose item the server stops sending (without a manifest saying so) waits in `held` (`ItemRevs.waiting`, ≤ 200). The memory files (`.revs`, `.manifests`, `.trashseen`; `SealedFile`) tell "missing" from "unreadable": an unreadable one is never read as empty or written over (vaults keep their state or wait unchecked, nothing is started or purged).
- Vault manifests and required signed sharing (0.12.0; `data/Manifests.kt`, `data/ItemOpening.kt`, tests `ManifestsTest`, `ItemOpeningTest`, vectors in `GoldenVectorsTest`): every copy shown is checked in `VaultRepository.show` (full, delta, live sync, the cached copy at unlock). **Two copies per account** (`CopyFiles`, `noBackupFilesDir/vault`): `<id>.bin` is the copy this phone checked with the keys (written only from a checked server answer or its own write, the only one ever opened, shown or filled from, also by the autofill and credential services, which share the repository); the keyless WorkManager refresh writes its result to `<id>.next` and never over it. At unlock the checked copy is shown, then `adoptRefreshed` judges `<id>.next` with the keys like a delta, asking the server nothing: it replaces the checked copy only when every vault is `Ok`/`None` (manifest and chain; a vault two or more versions ahead needs the log, so it isn't taken), every vault's key passes signed sharing and no item is older than remembered (`Manifests.adoptable`); otherwise it is deleted and the next sync decides. Writing the checked copy deletes it. After its own write the copy takes the manifest it sent, and remembers it, only when the answer's `manifest_link` equals the link of what was sent (a retried create that sealed its manifest again syncs instead), also under the vault's old number when other things were written there, followed by a sync. Memory `{version, link, accepts}` (and `not_checked` on a forgotten entry, see below) per vault in `vault/<id>.manifests` (store key): an ordinary advance only goes up and never replaces a link at its version; copies are joined by `accepts` first, then version (`Manifests.joined`); written only after the copy that carries it is stored; the all-zero link is never remembered. A vault two or more versions ahead gets `GET /vaults/{id}/manifest-log` (`Manifests.fetchLog`, ≤ 25 requests, entries passed to the core as they came; 404/400 or a log that doesn't connect = mismatch; when it can't be fetched the whole answer is not taken: `ManifestLogUnavailable`, the copy and states shown before stay). A warning is raised only by the server's whole answer (`SyncSnapshot.whole`): a delta, the cached copy or an offline copy that doesn't pass is `Unchecked` (no warning, writes sync first) and a failing delta is followed by one full fetch (`showAnswer`); a warning that stands is stored with the copy (`SyncSnapshot.unresolved`, local only), so neither a later delta nor a restart fetches everything again. **"Use the server's copy"** (`useServerCopy`, `Manifests.serverCopy`; offered to everyone when `manifestAccept` gives `ok`/`none` for the served rows, else to members who can write; readers then see "Someone who can edit this vault can review it."): one full fetch, then either a local accept by any role (memory replaced with the served head, also downwards, or forgotten when the server shows no manifest; `accepts + 1`; no request) or, for writers, a baseline at exactly row + 1 naming `manifestLink(row)` (memory replaced with it when the answer's `manifest_link` is its link; 409 = again, 3 times; 429 = the usual rate-limit message, warning kept); a last full fetch then takes the vault's items as served: the edit counters and kept newer copies are dropped for the items this phone's own copy had in that vault (`ItemRevs.acceptedIds`), never for an id only that fetch lists there. When the server shows no manifest for a vault this phone remembers one of (`Judged.noRecord`, `VaultInfo.manifestNoRecord`) the confirmation is its own text ("Your server no longer shows a record of changes for this vault. …"; an acceptance confirmed with the ordinary text isn't carried out as a forget: "This vault changed on another device. Try again.", and the warning then offers the right text), the forgotten entry is marked `not_checked`, and the vault carries the quiet line "This vault isn't being checked for missing or restored items." until a manifest is remembered again. A restored server (a lower `revision` and lower vault numbers than the copy) is synced and judged like any answer: an unlocked sync never keeps its copy because the answer is older (`SyncDecision.isStale` with vault numbers, `syncLocked`); only the locked refresh and `adoptRefreshed` refuse an older copy, and they show nothing. **Rows that don't open** (`ItemOpening.open`, called for every row in `decryptVault`; the cases are told apart by the core's error: `DecryptFailed`/malformed text = not decrypted, `Invalid("json: …")` or the app's own reader failing = decrypted but unknown content): (a) only the served `item_key` fails and the phone has a row with the same id, vault and `data` whose key opens it (stored copy, copy shown, a waiting version): the item is used as before and the copy keeps the phone's key (nothing warned or held; a key replacement re-encrypts from that key, `Manifests.rotationRows`); (b) it decrypts but this app can't read it: listed as "Item from a newer version of PassAuth" / "Update PassAuth to see this item.", no warning, "Delete for good" for owners and admins only (`Manifests.mayDeleteNewer`, own confirmation; not listed when already in the trash), counted in manifests, no obstacle to a first manifest; (c) it doesn't decrypt: listed as "Item that can't be opened" (with the name of an earlier version the phone holds) and its vault shows "An item in this vault can't be opened." (`ManifestState.Unopenable` when the manifest passes): **no write is held for it** (`Manifests.writable`), members who can write get "Delete for good" (`deleteUnopenable`: `DELETE /items/{id}/permanent` with the next manifest; confirm text says the item may still open elsewhere), no first manifest is started over such a row, the notice stays after a baseline or accept while one is there, and such a vault gets no new key on this phone (every item would have to be encrypted again). Trashed rows are summaries on this app (no key or data), so a trashed row that doesn't open can't be told and doesn't hold off a first manifest here; a trashed row the copy does hold with key and data is checked and listed like a live one. **Unreadable memory**: vaults with a manifest are `Unchecked` (writes refused with "PassAuth couldn't check this vault with your server yet. Try again in a moment."), vaults without one stay writable, nothing is started, purged or written over it; after 3 failed reads in a row across a finished sync (`UnreadableMemory`; any of the three files counts: `.manifests`, the item edit counters `.revs` or the trash times `.trashseen` alone too) the list says "PassAuth can't read this device's record of your vaults." with "Reset on this device" (`resetDeviceMemory`: the unreadable files are moved aside as `*.unreadable`, the phone goes on as one that remembers nothing). Answers with a `seq`, `manifest_seq`, `key_epoch` or `deleted_at` outside 0..2^53−1 are refused; `/sync?trash=summary` rows keep `data_sha256`, a cached row without it is fetched again in full once; a newer copy kept after an item rollback is counted by the hash of the served data (`served_sha256`, local only, never taken from a server) and takes the served `revision`. Writes (`ItemWrites`: create, edit, move, trash, key replacement, also from the autofill and credential services) carry the next manifest; `manifest_conflict` / `manifest_required` sync and retry (3 sends), never the overwrite dialog. First manifest (always version 1; never over a vault with a kept newer copy, an item marked as served older or a row that doesn't decrypt) and old-trash purge (one request each, one chunk per sync) only after a foreground sync, never from the background worker; old trash goes only when the server's rule says so (strictly `deleted_at < server_time − trash_days·86400`), this phone has itself seen the item in the trash for max(`trash_days`, 7) days (`TrashSeen`, `vault/<id>.trashseen`) and the server's clock is within 24 h of the phone's (`Manifests.dueTrash`, overflow-safe); a key replacement re-encrypts exactly the checked copy (`Manifests.rotationRows`: the fresh full `/sync` only supplies the trashed items' data and must match the copy row by row; kept newer copies are what is re-encrypted); the emergency view builds the rows it counts from the fields it read (`Emergency.manifestRows`), a row without `data`/`item_key`, with a `deleted_at` that is no time, or that doesn't open is a mismatch, and a vault without a manifest is shown with "This vault has no record of changes yet, …"; a warned vault takes no writes and no new key. Signed sharing's own writes at a sync (tombstones of vaults no longer listed, key pins, the status shown) happen only for an answer that is kept (`SharingChecks.commit`). Sends: a v1 Send that has a password takes a new name or text only together with its password on a server with `send_v2` ("Enter this Send's password to change its text."). A server whose `/api/config` lacks `signed_sharing` is refused in `setServer`, sign-in, sign-up and before every `/sync` (`ServerSupport`; the stored copy stays readable); a config that can't be read counts only with a feature list kept from before (`ServerSupport.decide`: none kept = can't connect; the list survives the record reset after an account is deleted), and the signed-sharing checks are always on, whatever the list says.

- Offline changes (contract in "Offline changes"; `data/OfflineChanges.kt` = pure rules, JVM-tested in `OfflineChangesTest`; `ui/vault/OfflineChangeViews.kt` = change card and conflict sheet): `VaultRepository` keeps the checked vault internally and publishes `vault` with the list on top (`publish`, `OfflineChanges.overlay`), so list, search, item pages, autofill and Credential Manager all see the changes. The list is sealed with `seal_offline_changes` in `SecureStore.offlineChanges(id)` (`vault/<id>.offline`, store-key sealed too, deleted with the copy), opened at unlock (`loadOffline`); its count (a plain number) is `AccountData.pendingChanges` for the warnings while locked. `createLocked`/`updateLocked`/`trashItem` queue when the app knows it is offline (`VaultData.offline` or the OS network callback in `PassAuthApp`), when the item already has a change, or on `UnreachableException`/502–504, keeping the last attempt as `sent`. Sender (`requestSend` → `sendOne`) after every checked sync and on `connectionBack`, under the repository mutex, reusing the same write paths (manifests, `afterWrite`). Android has no trash view: a waiting trash hides the item, and an edit of an item trashed elsewhere is a "deleted" conflict. Change server is reached only after Log out, which warns.
- Identifier-first save (`autofill/IdentifierFirst.kt`, `IdentifierFirstTest`): a user-name-only screen answers with `SaveInfo` `FLAG_DELAY_SAVE` and the user name in `clientState`; the password screen's save then offers user name + password in one prompt (save rule unchanged). Credential Manager password save: `PassAuthCredentialService` answers `BeginCreatePasswordCredentialRequest` with a create entry → "Save in" sheet (unlock first when locked) → save rule. Debug sample `:samples:login` `IdentifierFirstActivity`.

## iOS app (`ios/`)
- Xcode project generated by XcodeGen from `ios/project.yml` (`PassAuth.xcodeproj` is not committed). Targets: `PassAuthCore` (framework: the UniFFI Swift bindings `ios/Core/Sources/PassAuthCore.swift` plus the static `PassAuthCoreFFI.xcframework`, device arm64 + simulator arm64, both generated by `scripts/build-ios-core.sh` and gitignored), `PassAuthKit` (framework, extension-safe API only: the data layer, shared with the AutoFill extension), `PassAuth` (SwiftUI app, `io.passauth.app`, iOS 17+, iPhone and iPad), `PassAuthAutoFill` (credential provider extension `io.passauth.app.autofill`, embedded in the app; theme and form components come from `ios/UI`, compiled into both), `PassAuthTests` (XCTest, hosted) and `PassAuthUITests` (XCUITest). Swift 6 language mode with complete concurrency checking (the generated bindings build in Swift 5 mode). Crypto only ever comes from the Rust core; items, bundles and KDF params cross as JSON in the server/web shape (`Shared/Item.swift` mirrors `web/src/lib/types.ts`: lenient decoding, nulls written, unknown `format` omitted).
- Build: `scripts/build-ios-core.sh && cd ios && xcodegen generate && xcodebuild -scheme PassAuth -destination 'platform=iOS Simulator,name=iPhone 17' test`. Signing: automatic with `DEVELOPMENT_TEAM = JEXQBK3SQ5` in `project.yml` (`ios/Local.xcconfig`, gitignored, can override it locally). Release: `.github/workflows/ios.yml` archives on macOS with the App Store Connect API key (Admin; cloud-managed certificates via `-allowProvisioningUpdates`) and uploads with `ios/ExportOptions.plist` (app-store-connect, destination upload) on each `ios-X.Y.Z` tag or a manual run with "upload". Info.plist has no `ITSAppUsesNonExemptEncryption`: App Store Connect asks the export compliance questions (uses encryption, not exempt, standard algorithms; France excluded); `YES` without the `ITSEncryptionExportComplianceCode` App Store Connect issues is rejected at upload. Device signing also needs the App Group `group.io.passauth.app`, the Keychain group and the AutoFill credential provider capability on both App IDs (`io.passauth.app`, `io.passauth.app.autofill`).
- Version: XcodeGen's `preGenCommand` runs `ios/scripts/version.sh`, which writes `ios/Version.xcconfig` (gitignored) from `ios/VERSION` (the iOS app's own version; `scripts/release.sh ios X.Y.Z`): `MARKETING_VERSION` = that version, `CURRENT_PROJECT_VERSION` = major·1 000 000 + minor·1 000 + patch (as Android's versionCode; `Shared/AppVersion.swift` mirrors it for the tests, which also check the built Info.plist against `ios/VERSION`).
- Data layer (`ios/Shared`): `VaultRepository` (`@MainActor`, `@Observable`) mirrors the Android repository and the extension's `vault.ts` (prelogin → OPAQUE → 2FA with remember-device → bundle unlock → `/sync` → decrypt; register; create/update/trash/restore/delete forever with revision conflicts reported as `ConflictError`; lock/logout; up to 5 accounts). Operations are serialised by an async lock; Argon2id and decryption run off the main thread. `ApiClient` (ephemeral `URLSession`, no cache/cookies, redirects refused) pins each request to one account and rotates refresh tokens on 401.
- Storage (`SecureStore`): accounts, settings and device id in `state.bin`, and each account's last `/sync` response in `vault/<id>.bin`, in the App Group container (`group.io.passauth.app`, falls back to Application Support without the entitlement), encrypted with ChaCha20-Poly1305 under a random Keychain key (file name as associated data), file protection complete, excluded from backup. Tokens, Secret Keys, two-step remember tokens and (vault timeout "never") session bytes live in one Keychain item. All Keychain items are `WhenUnlockedThisDeviceOnly`, non-synchronizable, in the shared access group `$(AppIdentifierPrefix)io.passauth.shared` listed in both targets' entitlements and copied into their Info.plist (`PAKeychainGroup`): `JEXQBK3SQ5.io.passauth.shared` when signed with the team, `io.passauth.shared` in unsigned simulator builds (the simulator honours the embedded entitlements). A missing `state.bin` (fresh install) deletes leftover Keychain items. The state is read once the UI is up.
- Delta sync (`Shared/DeltaSync.swift`; `DeltaSyncTests`, also in the macOS AutoFill tests, and `ContractTests` against `ScriptedServer`): as on Android (same merge, validation, incremental decryption and `vault_seq` rules; `SyncQueue` behind `sync()`). `vault/<id>.bin` holds the encoded `SyncSnapshot` (sealed, written atomically), so cursor, vault numbers and items always go together. The app and its AutoFill extension share that copy, so neither uses `trash=summary` (the app shows the trash). Local apply also covers restore and delete for good; a move syncs. Server features: `AccountData.features`/`featuresAt` (6 h). The AutoFill extension's `refreshIfStale` (iOS and macOS) asks `/sync/revision` with vault numbers and then a delta from the copy it opened, decrypting only what changed. Storage section (`StorageSection`, only with a limit) and `quota_exceeded` → `quotaExceededMessage` as on Android (app, AutoFill save sheet, passkey saves; also macOS).
- App ↔ extension (two processes on the same files and Keychain item): every `SecureStore.update` re-reads `state.bin` and the secrets under a cross-process `flock` (`.state.lock`, 2 s timeout) before writing, so neither side overwrites the other; the app calls `reload()` when it becomes active. Only the app creates the state, the store key or wipes them (`Role.appExtension` never does). Refresh tokens rotate and a reused one revokes the session, so a refresh runs under a per-account lock (`.refresh-<id>.lock`, 25 s wait, 20 s request), re-reads the tokens, and calls `/auth/refresh` only if the stored access token is still the rejected one (otherwise the other process already rotated them). The app holds that lock inside a background task (iOS kills apps suspended while holding a shared-container lock). A refresh that can't reach the server no longer signs the account out.
- Unlock: master password (+ stored Secret Key), or Face ID / Touch ID: `Account.toSessionBytes()` in a Keychain item with `SecAccessControl` `.biometryCurrentSet` (invalidated when enrolment changes; the app then turns biometric unlock off and asks for the master password). Vault timeout and action like the extension and Android (`LockManager`: continuous clock, touches tracked by a non-interfering window gesture recognizer, "Immediately" fires when the scene goes to the background, "On app restart" keeps keys in memory only, "never" keeps session bytes in the Keychain).
- Hardening: privacy cover over the window whenever the scene isn't active while unlocked or while the new Secret Key is shown; clipboard copies are `localOnly` (no Universal Clipboard) with `expirationDate` = the user's clearing delay; ATS https only with `NSAllowsLocalNetworking`, and the app accepts plain http only for local hosts (localhost, loopback, private IPv4, link-local, `.local`); no logging.
- UI (SwiftUI, native patterns with the brand: navy navigation bars, accent `#0866ED`/`#3D8BFF`, the logo drawn as a vector from `assets/passauth-logo.svg`, light/dark/system theme): sign-in (server, email, master password, Secret Key, two-step with remember device, create account with Emergency Kit export), unlock, TabView with Vault (search, filter menu: all/favorites/trash, type, folder, vault; swipe and context actions; pull to refresh; website icons from `/api/icons/{host}`), item page (copy, reveal, live TOTP with ring, open website, passkeys, restore/delete forever for trashed items), item editor sheet (all item types, labels above boxes, TOTP via typing, paste, camera or image, revision-conflict choice: keep mine or use the other version), Generator (core password/passphrase; options in the shared settings `Settings.generator`, so AutoFill generates with them; the old app-only user defaults are migrated once) and Settings (Account, Account security, AutoFill (status, ask to save and update logins, never-save list, default URI match, TOTP copy, clipboard clearing), Vault options, Appearance, About).
- AutoFill extension (`ios/AutoFill`, `ASCredentialProviderViewController`; capabilities ProvidesPasswords, ProvidesPasskeys, ProvidesOneTimeCodes (iOS 18), ShowsConfigurationUI; entitlement `com.apple.developer.authentication-services.autofill-credential-provider` on app and extension). Its own process: it opens the vault itself (master password, Face ID / Touch ID via the shared biometric Keychain item, or the kept session bytes of vault timeout "never") from the on-device copy of the last sync (shown at once, synced in the background), and forgets it when iOS ends the extension; it can't sign in. Matching (`AutofillMatcher`): iOS gives a page URL (Safari, `.URL`) or a domain (apps with associated domains, `.domain` → `https://<domain>/`, so "exact"/"starts with" see that), matched with `UrlMatcher` and the default-match setting like Android's web `FillTarget`; app websites (`androidapp://`) never match, an https login is never offered on an http page; the account's own server (the web vault's sign-in page) is treated like any other website, so its login is suggested there and written to the identity store. The list shows suggestions for the site, the other logins, search, and (passkey request) the RP's passkeys newest first; picking a login fills it and, with "Copy verification code automatically", copies the current TOTP (`localOnly`, expiring after the clearing delay). One-time codes (iOS 18): list of matching logins with a TOTP, `completeOneTimeCodeRequest`.
- Identity store (`CredentialIdentitySync` + `IdentityMapper`): after every sync, settings change (default match) and in the extension's configuration screen, the active account's logins replace the `ASCredentialIdentityStore` entries (only when PassAuth is enabled): passwords (`ASPasswordCredentialIdentity`), passkeys (`ASPasskeyCredentialIdentity`: RP ID, user name, credential ID, user handle) and TOTP logins (`ASOneTimeCodeCredentialIdentity`, iOS 18), with the item id as record identifier, never a secret; favorites rank higher. Websites map by their effective match: base domain → `.domain` identity of the base domain, host → `.URL` of the origin, exact / starts with → `.URL` of the address; regex, never, app and non-web websites and anything covering the server's host are left out (iOS matches identities itself and doesn't tell the extension the page when one is picked). Emptied on log out (also the timeout's) and when the active account after a switch or log out is locked; kept while locked (iOS shows the user names in the QuickType bar). A picked suggestion fills without UI only when the vault opens without the user (timeout "never") and the item is still there; otherwise `userInteractionRequired` → unlock UI → fill. Passkeys always go through the UI.
- Passkeys: registration (`prepareInterface(forPasskeyRegistration:)`) asks "Save a passkey?" for the RP ID and user name iOS verified, refuses non-ES256 requests, checks iOS 18 `excludedCredentials` after consent (`matchedExcludedCredential`), creates the key with the core's `passkeyCreate` (same format as the extension and Android), stores it like `storePasskey` (sync first; same site + user name's writable login, else a new login for `https://<rpId>`; iOS doesn't give the RP name or display name, so RP ID and user name stand in) and returns the attestation with the OS's `clientDataHash`. Assertion (identity from the sheet, or the list for `prepareCredentialList(for:requestParameters:)` honouring `allowedCredentials`) signs the given `clientDataHash` with `passkeyAssert`. User verification: unlocking in the flow counts, otherwise `LAContext` `.deviceOwnerAuthentication`.
- Extension memory: iOS caps credential provider extensions (around 120 MB, device dependent). Argon2id with the default 128 MiB costs a 151 MB peak in the app process (22.9 MB baseline, `KdfMemoryTests`) and 166 MB in the extension (37.8 MB with the unlock screen up; debug builds expose it to `AutoFillUITests`), so on devices master-password unlock is offered in the extension only if `os_proc_available_memory()` exceeds the account's `memory_kib` + 16 MiB; otherwise it asks for Face ID / Touch ID (enabled in the app) or points to the app. The simulator has no extension limit (`os_proc_available_memory` = 0, so it's allowed there).
- Settings → AutoFill shows whether PassAuth is enabled (`ASCredentialIdentityStore.getState`), "Turn on AutoFill" (iOS 18 `ASSettingsHelper.requestToTurnOnCredentialProviderExtension`) and "Open AutoFill settings" (`openCredentialProviderAppSettings`, app settings as fallback), plus default URI match, TOTP copy and clipboard clearing; becoming enabled re-writes the identities.
- Simulator: the iOS 27.0 runtime can't reach `com.apple.SafariFoundation.CredentialProviderExtensionHelper` (launchd_sim lookup fails for Safari, Apple's own agent and PassAuth alike), so the identity store doesn't work there: no QuickType suggestions, no identities in the passkey sheet, and the app reports AutoFill as off. Everything else runs on the simulator (`AutoFillUITests`: enable in Settings with the configuration UI, Safari password list → unlock → fill → TOTP pasted, passkey create and sign-in via "More from PassAuth…"); the identity store paths need a device (`TEST_RUNNER_PA_IDENTITY_STORE=1`).
- Account deletion (App Store guideline 5.1.1(v)): Settings → About → Delete account (its own page: warning, master password, Secret Key if not stored). Like Android: OPAQUE login with `purpose: "reauth"` on the signed-in session, `DELETE /api/account` with `x-reauth-token`, then the account is forgotten locally (biometric item, offline copy, Secret Key unless another account uses the email, two-step remember token, AutoFill identities; the last account keeps only the server address).
- Item editor TOTP: type, `PasteButton`, "Scan QR code" (`AVCaptureSession` + `AVCaptureMetadataOutput` `.qr`: the output decodes, the app never sees or keeps frames; camera permission asked only on tap, `NSCameraUsageDescription` = website/privacy.html Camera section; denial explains typing/pasting and links to Settings; no camera → notice) or "Choose image" (`PhotosPicker`, no library permission; decoded in memory with `CIDetector` QR, also inverted, `Shared/QrDecoder.swift`). Parsing as Android's `OtpAuth` (URIs kept whole, base32 cleaned, HOTP and `otpauth-migration://` refused).
- Item page: "Password history" (count) → previous passwords newest first, masked, reveal, copy (`password_history`; saving a changed password prepends the old one, max 20). "Move to another vault" (owner/admin/write targets): `VaultRepository.moveItem` → re-encrypt with the target vault key under the same id, `PUT /api/items/{id}` with the new `vault_id` and the old revision. The editor keeps the entry it started from in `@State` (the sheet is rebuilt on every sync), so a change from elsewhere surfaces as the revision conflict.
- Live sync (`App/LiveSync.swift`, pure parts in `Shared/LiveSyncProtocol.swift`): while the scene is active and the active account unlocked, `URLSessionWebSocketTask` to `/api/ws?access_token=…` on its own ephemeral session (open/close from the delegate, redirects refused), pings every 25 s. `hello` origin remembered but unused, `sync` decided by `SyncDecision.onLiveSync` (vault numbers; older servers by revision), backoff (1 s → 60 s, 20% jitter, `refreshIfStale` before each reconnect) and the "logout" session check as Android; syncs go through the repository's `SyncQueue`. Disconnects on background, lock, log out and account switch. `two_step_required` (message or close 1008) stops reconnecting and shows the two-step setup screen (`twoStepRequiredBySocket`).
- Background sync (`App/BackgroundRefresh.swift`; Settings → Vault options → "Background sync": Off/30 min/1 h/2 h (default)/6 h/12 h, `Settings.backgroundSyncMinutes`): `BGAppRefreshTask` `io.passauth.app.refresh` (`UIBackgroundModes` fetch, `BGTaskSchedulerPermittedIdentifiers`, handler registered in the `AppDelegate` adaptor), scheduled with `earliestBeginDate = now + interval` when the scene goes to the background, when the setting changes and after each run (iOS picks the real moment); cancelled when off or no account is signed in. The run calls `VaultRepository.refreshCacheLocked` for every signed-in account: the active unlocked account gets `syncLocked`, the others `DeltaSync.refresh` through the usual `ApiClient` (same refresh path + cross-process FileLock): `/sync/revision` first when the server has delta sync and the copy a cursor (current → only `lastSync`), else or when stale the changes (or everything) merged into the copy, written only when valid with the bundle and not older than the copy; nothing is decrypted and the identity store isn't touched. Protected data unavailable (device locked) → nothing to do, reschedule; the expiration handler cancels the work so no shared-container lock is held at suspension.
- Export: Settings → Vault options → Export vault. Formats from the core (`exportVaultEncrypted` ≥ 10-character password, `exportVaultJson`, `exportVaultCsv`), warnings for the unencrypted ones, master password checked locally against the bundle first, file kept in memory and written only where the user picks with `fileExporter` (`passauth-export-YYYY-MM-DD[-protected].json` / `.csv`); the copy the exporter stages in the app's temporary directory is deleted after export or cancel and at launch. The exporter is an in-process remote view: the scene stays active, so even vault timeout "Immediately" doesn't lock behind it (UI-tested); no grace period needed as on Android.
- Web vault shortcuts open the account's server in Safari with path routes: Vault options → `/settings/vaults`, `/import`, `/vault`.
- Account security (`App/AccountSecurityViews.swift`, data in `Shared/AccountSecurity.swift`, as the web vault's Security and Two-step login tabs): "Change master password" (current password (+ Secret Key if not stored), new + confirm with the master-password strength scale (Weak < 50 bits asks again before it's used, Fair < 65, Good < 80, Strong), "Use a Secret Key" (keep / turn on / turn off; new password may stay empty for a Secret Key change only), alert saying other devices are signed out) → `changeMasterPassword`: reauth → `Account.rewrap` → `/account/keys/start` → OPAQUE register finish → `/account/keys/finish` with `x-reauth-token`; a lost answer is checked against `/sync`'s bundle so a new Secret Key is never lost. Then on the device: the stored bundle (app and AutoFill unlock derive from it), the Secret Key (new one stored, removed when turned off), the two-step remember token (the server forgets them) and the session; the account key and key pair don't change, so the Face ID item and the "never" session bytes stay valid (the latter is rewritten), then a `/sync` refreshes the offline copy and the identity store. A new Secret Key is shown once (copy, Emergency Kit PDF via the share sheet (`App/EmergencyKit.swift`), "I saved my Secret Key" before Done, no swipe-away). "Two-step login": status from `GET /account`; authenticator setup sheet (QR code from Core Image, setup key in groups with Copy, "Open in authenticator app" = the `otpauth://` URL, `.oneTimeCode` 6-digit field), email codes (send, verify), recovery codes shown once (Copy all, Share, "I saved them", `interactiveDismissDisabled`), new recovery codes and turning a method off after the master password (`reauth` sheet, destructive button). Errors: wrong code, HTTP 429 "Too many attempts, try again later.", network (`securityErrorMessage`). Secrets live only in view state, cleared when the screens close; the app-switcher cover is on while they're shown. Turning on the authenticator app or email codes asks for the master password (+ Secret Key if not kept) in the form; `enableTwoFactor` sends `x-reauth-token`, keeps the token (`ReauthHold`) across mistyped codes while the password field is unchanged, and replaces it once when it has expired.
- Signed-in devices (`App/SessionsView.swift`, data and wording in `Shared/Sessions.swift`, `VaultRepository.accountSessions`): as on Android, with confirmations in a `PromptScaffold` decision sheet. Rate limits: HTTP 429 says "Too many attempts. Try again in N seconds/minutes." when the server sent `Retry-After` (`ApiError.retryAfter`, `tooManyAttemptsMessage`, also in `errorMessage` for sign-in, unlock syncs and two-step sign-in), else "Too many attempts, try again later.". AutoFill matching (`AutofillMatcher`) counts regular-expression websites only for logins in the user's own vaults. Choosing a login in a shared vault under a passkey's "Save in" shows `WarningNote` "Saving here shares this passkey with everyone in <vault>." (`PasskeyLogic.sharedWarning`; `WarningNote` lives in `ios/UI/Theme.swift`, so the macOS sheet `MacPasskey` shows it too). Tests include `SessionsTests`, the `ContractTests` rate-limit cases and `AutofillTests.testRegexWebsitesOnlyFromOwnVaults`.
- Invitations, policies and devices (2026-11-15 round 5; `Shared/Invitations.swift`, `Shared/Policies.swift`, pure, `InvitationPolicyTests` in the iOS and macOS AutoFill tests; `App/InvitationViews.swift`): `/sync` `invitations` are read leniently (`SyncInvitation.read`: bad entries dropped) and kept in the `SyncSnapshot`; every answer replaces them. Only the app opens them (`VaultRepository.isApp`): each sealed key is opened with `openVaultKey(…, "sealed")` just to read the vault's name, dropped at once, and the name cached; a failure means "can't be opened" (Decline only). A dismissible notice above the vault list, a "Vault invitations" row with the count in Settings and `InvitationsPage` (name, sharer, role, the sharer's key fingerprint with the out-of-band check, Accept…/Decline… in a decision sheet; accept = POST `/vaults/{id}/invitation/accept` then sync; decline = DELETE `/vaults/{id}/invitation`, 404 fine). Policies: `/api/config` `policies` kept with the account (`AccountData.policies`), refreshed after sign-in, unlock and restore; `GET /account` `policy_violations` become notices (two-step login, change master password; `kdf` links there only when the core's default keys meet the minimum, else says the app can't do it yet). `Stored.timeoutLimit` = the strictest `max_vault_timeout_minutes` of the signed-in accounts; Settings offers only choices within it ("On app restart" and "Never" never), `enforceTimeoutLimit` lowers a saved choice (also at launch, before restoring "Never" keys) and Settings says so. `disable_export` hides and blocks Export. Master-password minimum max(10, policy) in Change master password and Create account; `require_secret_key` forces "Use a Secret Key". Two-step gate: `ApiClient.onTwoStepRequired` marks the account on any signed-in 403 `two_step_required`; `TwoStepRequiredView` replaces the vault (authenticator and email setups, a web-vault link for security keys, Sign out; "Continue" after the first method); a successful sync clears it; live sync stays off while gated; the AutoFill extensions say "Your server requires two-step login" when they have no copy. Forget device: sessions carry `device_id`; "Forget…" per row (feature `forget_device`), confirmed in a decision sheet; `current: true` signs out locally and drops the remember token. Invitee side (2026-11-16): the sharer's fingerprint is shown as "Fingerprint your server lists for <email>" with "Accept only invitations you expected." (the sealed key is anonymous); sharer names and emails are cleaned.
- Sign in with another device (2026-11-16 security round; `Shared/DeviceLogin.swift`, `Shared/DeviceLoginRepository.swift`, `App/AuthRequestViews.swift`, `AuthViews` `DeviceLoginView`/`DeviceCodeView`; tests `DeviceLoginTests` (also macOS), `DeviceLoginContractTests`, UI `DeviceLoginUITests` against a :8091 server): new device polls 2,2,2,3,3,4,5,6,8 then 10 s; the countdown and wait never pass 15 min from the request plus 1 min grace (`DeviceLogin.deadline`). The approval (after two-step login if any, which only servers before 0.16.1 ask for) is held in memory until the user types the 6-digit code the approving app shows (`ApprovedLogin.confirm`; "That code doesn't match. N tries left."; the third wrong code signs the new session out in the background, drops the keys and shows "Too many wrong codes…"; cancel at any step also signs out a session already made). Only then: unlock, tokens, remember token, and a received Secret Key, kept only for the email typed here, only if the answer names that account, never over a kept one (`DeviceLogin.keepsSecretKey`). Approving app: `AuthRequestInbox` over the pure `ApprovalQueue` freezes the shown request (id, key, challenge, name, app) and its `loginPhraseChoices(key, 3)`; newer requests wait; while choosing, a listing that drops it or changes its key closes it in the sheet ("This request changed…", or the expired wording past its time). Re-listed challenges are compared by their opened value (`AuthRequest.challengeProof`, `DeviceApprovals.sameRequest`), never by text; a frozen challenge that doesn't open says "This request can't be checked, so it wasn't approved…". The sheet can't be swiped away and its buttons act only on their own request id: "Which words does the new device show?", 3 phrases tappable 1 s after the content appeared and again after the app comes back, "None of these" (deny), "Not now" (keyboard and VoiceOver focus first), then "About this request" (device as it calls itself, app, IP, time; `ShownText.clean`, 64 max). A pick calls `approveLoginRequest(frozen key, id, phrase, kept Secret Key)` + `openLoginChallenge(frozen challenge)`: mismatch → denied + "Those aren't the words…"; success → "Enter this code on the new device" with "123 456" until Done.
- Errors (`Shared/Errors.swift`, `ErrorTextTests`, also macOS): `ApiError.requestId` (body `request_id`, else `x-request-id`, shown only when id-shaped); `apiErrorText` uses the app's wording for every known code and legacy reason (507 `insufficient_storage`: "Your server is running out of storage space…"; the attachment quota); unknown codes get "Your PassAuth server refused this request." / "Something went wrong on your PassAuth server." + "Reference: <id>." + "Server message: …" (cleaned, ≤ 200); `localizedDescription` is the same text; the core's `CoreError` cases `WeakKdf`, `KdfTooStrong`, `PhraseMismatch`, `WrongCode`, `TooManyWrongCodes`, `NotConfirmed` are mapped.
- Item editor: under a website that runs as a pattern (`resolveMatch` == "regex"), the core's `regexError` or the `regexWarning` sentence (`UrlMatcher.patternNote`); saving isn't blocked. The "Regular expression" default-match help (iOS Settings, macOS `MacViews`) says plain addresses match by base domain.
- Accessibility: icon buttons labelled, masked secrets read as "hidden", label + value rows combined, fixed-shape text (avatar, TOTP ring) doesn't scale, button rows stack when they don't fit (`ViewThatFits`). Checked at Accessibility XXL (`testLargeText`).
- Tests: account security against a recording fake server (`AccountSecurityTests`: change-password request order, reauth header and local state, unlock with the new password only; TOTP enable, wrong code, 429; disable / new codes only with a reauth token; the shown-once guard) and `AccountSecurityUITests` (:8091: change password with a new Secret Key, lock/unlock, authenticator setup, recovery codes, sign in with two-step, turn off). Unit tests for OTP/QR parsing, export names and formats, live sync messages/backoff/URL, move request shape and password history (`Phase3Tests`), save decisions, password rules → generator and `saveLogin` against a recording fake server (`SaveGenerateTests`). `Phase3UITests` (needs the :8091 server; a QR image in Photos via `xcrun simctl addmedia`) acts as a second device through `UITests/TestServer.swift` (Rust core linked into the UI test bundle: register, 2FA, vaults, item edits). Registration is rate-limited (10/hour/IP): restart the throwaway server between many runs.
- Saving logins (iOS 26.2, capability `SupportsSavePasswordCredentials`; the deployment target stays iOS 17, everything behind `@available(iOS 26.2, *)`): Safari's "Use Strong Password?" panel then lists "Save in PassAuth", and after a sign-up / password change form is submitted iOS calls `performWithoutUserInteractionIfPossible(savePasswordRequest:)`. Not offered (cancelled as `userCanceled`) when "Ask to save and update logins" is off, the page's base domain is on the never-save list (`Settings.neverSave`, same key as the extension and Android), the request has no website (app identifier) or no password, or for the `generatedPasswordFilled` event (the submit that follows brings the user name). Decision: the shared save rule (see Features; `Shared/SaveLogic.swift` `plan`, `targets`, `plan(target:)`); a new login is named after the host (without `www.`), website = page origin (`https://<domain>` for an app's associated domain), match "Default". Without UI only when the user already chose to save (`userInitiated`) and the vault opens by itself (timeout "never"); otherwise `userInteractionRequired` → `prepareInterface(for: ASSavePasswordRequest)` → unlock if needed → "Save login?" / "Update login?" with user name, revealable password, "Save in" (writable logins for the page + "New login", preselected from the plan; an untouched choice is re-planned on the freshly synced vault), Save/Update, Not now, Never for this site. `VaultRepository.saveLogin(…target:)` syncs first (so the plan uses the server's revisions; offline → error shown), then creates/updates; the identity store is rewritten by the sync and awaited (`CredentialIdentitySync.flush`, at most 3 s) before `completeSavePasswordRequest`.
- Password generation (iOS 26.2, `SupportsGeneratePasswordCredentials`, with UI by default): `performWithoutUserInteraction(generatePasswordsRequest:)` returns `ASGeneratedPassword`s (`.strong`, `.alphanumeric` when the strong one has symbols and the site doesn't require them, `.passphrase` when the rules accept it; the user's preferred type first) without touching the vault; `prepareInterface(for: ASGeneratePasswordsRequest)` shows a sheet (type picker, regenerate, "Use this password"). Rules: the password field's `passwordrules`, else Safari's quirks for the site, else the confirmation field's (`Shared/PasswordRules.swift`: `required`/`allowed` classes incl. custom `[…]`, `minlength`, `maxlength`, `max-consecutive`). They map onto the core generator: classes the site doesn't allow are turned off, required ones on, length = the user's clamped to the site's range; when the site allows only some special characters, the core's symbols are replaced by those (system CSPRNG); every result is checked against the rules (retried up to 50 times). Rules the core can't express (a required set no whole class covers, a maximum under 8, nothing usable) fall back to the user's settings (the sheet says so).
- Seen on the iOS 27.0 simulator (`AutoFillUITests` AF6–AF8, page `tests/e2e/site/ios-signup.html`): focusing a new-password field shows Safari's "Use Strong Password?" panel with "Save in PassAuth" and "Fill Strong Password"; filling calls `performWithoutUserInteraction(generatePasswordsRequest:)` (no unlock) and fills PassAuth's first result into both fields; submitting then opens PassAuth's save sheet (unlock → "Save this login?"). A typed password brings iOS's own "Save Password? Choose where to save" alert (PassAuth / Passwords / Not Now) first; choosing PassAuth opens the sheet ("Update the password?" for a known user). iOS never called `prepareInterface(for: ASGeneratePasswordsRequest)` there (the suggestion pill only reopens the panel), so the generation sheet is compiled but not seen yet. `ASCredentialIdentityStore` calls may never answer on that simulator: identity writes are awaited at most 3 s.
- Permissions (`App/PermissionsViews.swift`, rules in `Shared/Permissions.swift`): first launch shows "Set up PassAuth" once (before "Connect to your server"; not on devices that already have accounts; shared `Settings.permissions.introShown`) listing only what the app uses, each with its own button, then Continue / Skip for now; there is no Permissions page in Settings (user decision 2026-11-19: later changes go through the Settings app). AutoFill (`ASSettingsHelper.requestToTurnOnCredentialProviderExtension` on iOS 18, else the AutoFill settings; identity store state with a 2 s timeout, unknown when it doesn't answer), Face ID / Touch ID (`evaluatePolicy` brings up the system prompt; denied = `biometryNotAvailable` with the hardware present; "allowed" remembered, after which the first sign-in or unlock offers "Unlock with Face ID?" once), camera (`AVCaptureDevice`), local network (no API, TN3179: a short `NWBrowser` browse for `_passauth._tcp` (`NSBonjourServices`); `kDNSServiceErr_PolicyDenied` = not allowed, "ready" once the app is active again after the prompt = allowed, else unknown; the last result is kept and re-checked when the screen is shown). Denied → "Open Settings". UI tests skip the screen with `-PASkipPermissions` except `testFullFlow` and `PermissionsUITests`.
- Not yet: conditional passkey registration, iOS store CI. Camera scanning needs a device (the simulator has no camera).

- Item extras (`Shared/Item.swift`): `Item` and every nested struct keep unknown fields (`extra`, `AnyKey`) and `attachments` as raw entries, so edits on the phone never drop data written by newer apps.
- Send (`Shared/Sends.swift` = `sends.ts` + `send-client.ts`, `SendsTests`; `App/SendViews.swift`): Settings → Send lists, creates, edits (only changes sent), copies/shares links (key masked until "Show the key"; share sheets never offer Copy), turns links off/on and deletes text Sends; the password verifier is derived off the main thread; links aren't opened in the app.
- Attachments (`Shared/Attachments.swift`, `AttachmentsTests`; `App/AttachmentViews.swift`): files from Files (read in place) or Photos, checked against `max_attachment_mb`, encrypted 1 MiB at a time with `FileEncryptor` into `tmp/attachments` (complete file protection) and uploaded from disk on a transfer session without the 60 s total cap; then the item is saved with the entry (3 tries, else `DELETE`). Downloads (`AttachmentDownloads`) stream to disk and decrypt with `FileDecryptor`; the file is shown in the share sheet only if the lock generation and account are unchanged, and deleted when the sheet closes. Closing the vault cancels uploads and downloads, dismisses share sheets and deletes temp files (also at launch). Server and item numbers are read with bounded conversions (`JSON.wholeInt64`).
- Emergency access (`Shared/Emergency.swift` = `emergency.ts`, `Shared/EmergencyClient.swift` = `emergency-client.ts`; `EmergencyTests`/`EmergencyClientTests` port every web case; `App/EmergencyViews.swift`): both roles as in the web vault. Roster memory in the Keychain (`emergency-roster.<user id>`), kept across sign-out; each operation pins its account (`EmergencyPin`). Removal replaces personal vault keys through `VaultRepository.rotateVaultKeys` (item `revision` sent, up to 3 tries on `revision_conflict`). The grantor's vaults open read-only in memory and never reach AutoFill.
- Signed sharing (iOS app and macOS AutoFill; `Shared/SignedSharing.swift` = checks, memory, wording; `Shared/SignedSharingRepository.swift` = requests, pins, reviews, rotation; tests `SignedSharingTests`, `SignedSharingVectorTests` (also in the macOS target), `SignedSharingRepoTests`, UI test `SignedSharingUITests`): "me" = `Session.meId` (the core-authenticated account id); a `/sync` naming another account is refused. Device memory = Keychain item `signed-sharing.<local account>`, tied to the core account id and server, merged on every write under the app-group lock file `.signed-sharing.lock` (app and AutoFill extensions). Checks are sticky and run in `VaultRepository.open` for every copy shown; every row goes through `pins_observe`; refused vaults (`VaultData.refused`) are absent from the list, AutoFill identity store, passkey, save and move targets and are listed in Settings → Shared vaults; consent vaults (`added`/`rejoin`/`replaced`) appear on the Vault invitations page (Decline only where `declinable`: pins first with `pinsDecline` (`currentRow`), then the server; consent rows are never tombstoned; only a 404 counts as already gone). Key replacement only from Emergency access ("Replace the keys now" on `rotation_pending`, removing a contact): a vault without a trusted roster shows the "Check the members" review sheet (`MemberReviewSheet`), missing members are confirmed per user id, and removals replace only vaults the same members answer shows as personal (`rotateVaultKeys(…, personalOnly:)`); a vault with only the account and no roster is re-keyed unsigned (`rotatePlain`). Mark as verified / Trust the new key only for the identity hash shown. iOS doesn't share or re-sign vaults. Final round (2026-11-16): device memory also keeps `pending_identity`/`pending_record` (a retried upload sends the same identity; a `PUT` answer without `created: true` leaves the state unknown) and `missing`/`missing_device`; a refused Keychain read is "unknown" (`Keychain.lookup`): shared vaults are refused with "PassAuth couldn't read what this device knows about your shared vaults…" and nothing is written; writes wait when the lock isn't free and are retried; unsent pins of the app and the AutoFill extensions are merged with `merge_pins` under the lock; grandfathering follows rules 1–4 (earlier offline copy read once per unlock; new device: "This device had no earlier copy of your shared vaults, so each one shared before signing needs your OK once. Accept only the ones you expected."); withheld newer pins keep the device's list ("Your server didn't send your newer list of known keys. PassAuth keeps using the list on this device."), a device without pins refuses shared vaults (`pins_unavailable`); Accept is bound to the entry shown (epoch pin, signer hash, roster hash; "This invitation changed. Check it again."; the sheet closes when its entry changes); Decline is hidden for vaults waiting for trust memory or a new device; headings lead with the pinned signer email, else "someone listed by your server as …"; Trust the new key pins the shown email; v1 review pins use the emails the review showed; failed tombstones are written at the next sync. Round 4 (2026-11-16): accepts use `pins_accept` (`Acceptance.observation`, with `signed_share`, `paused`, `chain_hashes` for vaults, invitations and re-joins); `sameDecision` also binds the signer's pinned and listed email, the email to pin and `own`; upgraded pins keep their email and verified mark; Decline only where `Invitation.canDecline` (signed consents), unsigned ones are accepted by the key id shown when allowed or locked with "Ask the vault's owner to sign this vault."; until the first signed sync is done (`memory.proven`, identity known, grandfathered list present) unsigned consent rows are held (`Refusal.checking`: "PassAuth is still checking this shared vault…", list notice "A shared vault isn't shown until PassAuth finishes checking it."), the identity upload is retried every sync; the earlier copy is the memory's `seen` list (vaults shown usable; apps from before use the offline cache once), candidates are recorded when the identity is generated and dropped for good once settled; pending tombstones are persisted (`memory.tombstones`) and never hit a usable vault; iOS writes no observed-removal tombstones; unreadable memory checks own vaults against this unlock's or the server's pins read-only; newer server pins are merged with the device's and written back; a 429 key replacement says when to retry. Round 5 (2026-11-16): the authenticated account id is kept as `AccountData.accountId` (sign-in, master-password unlock); Face ID, "Never" and the macOS timed unlock restore through `restoreSession` + `matchesBundle`, and bundles are stored only through `bundleMayBeStored`; device memory of another user on the same server is unreadable (a master-password sign-in clears it); own key replacements are recorded with `pinsRecordRotation`; pending tombstones are written with `pinsDecline` (version 0); people new to the pins are pinned only after a version-1 review; newer server pins merge with `pinsMergeLastKnown` and `identity_mismatch` pauses sharing with no writes.
- Security round 7/8/9 (iOS app + iOS/macOS AutoFill, shared `ios/Shared`; tests `Sec789Tests` (also in the macOS AutoFill target) + `ContractTests`): own wraps v2 (`newVaultKeyBound` at `key_epoch + 1`, `rotatePlain` and signed rotation, also emergency rotation) only while the last full sync's `user.account_wraps_v2 == true` (`Session.accountWrapsV2`, memory only); `pinsLegacyWrapAllowed` gets the row's epoch; `checkVault` sends `bound_wrap` for an opened v2 `account` row (observe and accept). Send v2 (`Sends.swift`, server `send_v2`): password Sends via `sendPasswordKeys`/`sendEncryptV2` + `owner_content_key`, `sendOpen` for both formats, password change/removal re-encrypts name and text. Item `rev` (`Item.rev`, written only when > 0): per-account memory `vault/<id>.revs` beside the offline copy (sealed, max-merged across processes, pruned to the copy's ids, deleted with it); `VaultRepository.open` checks every decrypted row (full, delta, live, local change); a rollback keeps the newer copy when the vault key is unchanged (row kept in the offline copy, server revision kept for edits), marks `Entry.rolledBack` (item warning) and `VaultData.rollback` (vault-list notice). Background refresh of a locked account stores ciphertext unchecked (checked at the next unlock, without a newer copy then).
- Vault manifests and required signed sharing (iOS app + iOS/macOS AutoFill; `Shared/VaultManifest.swift` = rules and wording, `VaultRepository` = requests; tests `VaultManifestTests` and the `vaultManifest` (checks, `accepts`, `badMemories`, logs passed as they came) / `itemRev.limits` vectors (also in the macOS target), `ManifestContractTests` against a live fake server that keeps each vault's chain and log, takes only the next version (first manifests and baselines too), answers every write with `manifest_link`, and refuses a write that isn't built on the current manifest). **Check:** a copy is opened and checked first and shown or stored only afterwards (`evaluate` → `commit`): `open` decrypts, `checkManifests` checks each usable vault's rows against its manifest with what this device remembers. The row's three fields (`manifest`, `manifest_seq`, `manifest_prev`) travel together through answers, deltas, the stored copy and own writes. A manifest one version above the remembered one that doesn't name it is a mismatch at once; `need_log` (two or more versions ahead) makes the app fetch `GET /vaults/{id}/manifest-log?after=<remembered version>` (pages followed, 25 requests at most; the entries go to the core as the server sent them, which skips what isn't an entry) and check again; no log from the server (404/400), more pages than that, or a log that doesn't connect is a mismatch; a log that can't be fetched (no answer, 5xx, 429) fails the whole sync, so the previous copy, cursor and verdicts stay and nothing new is shown with an old `ok`. The check runs whether or not the server lists `vault_manifest`, so a dropped feature or manifest is a rollback. A warned vault is also checked with nothing remembered (`VaultManifest.accept` = the core's `manifestAccept`): `VaultInfo.manifestAcceptable` says the served manifest describes the served items (or none is served), which decides what "Use the server's copy" does. **Only the server's answer warns** (`CopySource`): the copy on the device (cache-first unlock, an AutoFill request, offline) is checked, but what doesn't check out there is `unchecked` (no warning, shown as before; a write syncs first and otherwise fails with "PassAuth couldn't check this vault with your server yet. Try again in a moment."), because the copy may only be older than what the other process already saw; and no item is marked "older version" from it. **Except what a server answer already warned about** (round 3, N1): every stored copy carries, as a field only this device sets (`local_warned`, `LocalWarnings`), the ids of the vaults the answer it came from left `rollback`/`mismatch` and of the items it marked as older without a newer copy to keep. Whoever opens that copy later (the app after a lock and unlock or a restart, the iOS AutoFill extension, also its fill without interface, the macOS extension) keeps those warnings without asking the server: a listed vault that doesn't verify `ok` (or `none`, after its memory was forgotten) stays warned, with `manifestAcceptable` computed as always, and a listed item whose counter is still below the remembered one stays marked. Vaults and items not in the list keep the quiet rule above. The list is rewritten with every stored copy, so an answer that checks out clears it; while a vault shown is warned every sync fetches everything (a delta over a copy that is wrong stays wrong). After a delta everything is fetched once before a vault gets a warning it didn't have, and an answer behind what this device remembers is asked for again in full (twice at most in all). A restored server's answer with a lower `revision` is judged like any other (`SyncDecision.isStale` syncs on any other revision or vault number; nothing drops an answer for being lower). This device's own write only touches the vault(s) it wrote to (`CopySource.local`: that vault is `ok` at the manifest sent, whose link is remembered; every other vault and every item mark keeps its state, checked with this process's own memory, never what the other process stored since; the copy's `user.key_pins_seq`, read before this session wrote its key pins, is raised to what this device wrote, so its own copy is never taken for "an older list of known keys"). **Background App Refresh never replaces the checked copy** (`refreshCacheLocked`, `adoptRefreshed`): it has no keys, so what it fetches is kept next to the copy (`vault/<account>.next`, sealed the same way; the next refresh goes on from it) and the offline copy (`.bin`) only ever holds what an unlocked app or extension checked. At the next cache-first unlock (app, iOS and macOS AutoFill, also the fill without interface) the checked copy is opened and shown as before; then, with the keys, the kept answer is checked like a server answer after a delta, without asking the server anything (`CopySource.refreshed`), and adopted as the copy only when every vault is `ok` or `none`, no item is older than this device remembers, nothing in it fails to open or to pass signed sharing's checks that the shown copy didn't already, and it isn't behind the copy. It is judged against the device memory as stored at that moment (read again, before the kept answer is), and only when the stored copy is still the one this process opened (N10: the app may have written since the extension opened it). Anything else (also a vault that would need its log): it is deleted, the checked copy and the newer rows in it stay, and the next sync with the server decides, with the usual warning rules. A sync with the server deletes it too. A refresh that runs while the active account is unlocked is an ordinary sync without the upkeep (no first manifest, no old trash from the background). **Device memory** (`SecureStore`, one cross-process `FileLock` `.memory.lock` around each read-merge-write; read before the copy or answer it is checked against, written after the copy that carries it): per vault `{version, link, accepts}` (`ManifestMark`), sealed in `vault/<account>.manifests` next to `.revs` (the macOS extension has its own). Merged by `accepts` first, then the higher version, never another link at the same version; `accepts` counts this device's acceptances, so a process that still holds what an acceptance gave up can't bring it back. An entry without version and link (after a forget) remembers nothing but keeps its count; the all-zero link, half an entry and the first format (bare numbers) are nothing remembered; entries of gone vaults stay. A file that is there but can't be read is "unreadable", never empty: nothing is checked against it (a vault with a manifest is `unchecked`, a standing warning stays, a vault without a manifest stays writable), nothing is written over it, no manifest is started, nothing a background refresh kept is adopted. The app's `.trashed` file counts as part of that memory for the way out (not for the checks). After three failed reads in a row across a sync that worked (`memoryProblem`) the vault list (and the macOS AutoFill window) says "PassAuth can't read this device's record of your vaults." with "Reset on this device" (confirm: "PassAuth will trust what your server shows now and start a new record on this device."), which moves the unreadable files aside (`….unreadable`, deleted with the account's copy) and goes on as a device that remembers nothing. Item edit counters (`ItemRevMemory`: counter, vault, last seen; the earlier `{id: rev}` file still reads) are forgotten only on authenticated absence (`ItemRevCheck.settle`): the item is gone from a vault whose manifest matched that server answer, or this device deleted it for good itself; otherwise kept, capped at the 20,000 most recently seen. **Fields only this device sets:** `served_sha256` (on a newer row this device kept after the server sent an older version: the hash of what the server sent, which the check and the next manifest use for that row) and `local_warned` (the kept warnings above) are dropped from every server answer (`SyncMerge.apply(local:)`), so a server can't name the hash its own row is checked with or clear or plant a kept warning. A kept row stays marked ("Your server sent an older version of this item.", `Entry.rolledBack`, the vault notice) for as long as the copy holds it, across syncs, own writes, another vault's acceptance and a lock and unlock, until it is saved again, the server serves that version, or its own vault is accepted; no first manifest is started in a vault with such rows or with rolled-back items. Summary rows (macOS `trash=summary`) are checked by `data_sha256`, which this device's own trash sets when it drops the data; rows cached before the hashes are `incomplete` (no warning) until the next sync fetches everything once (in a full answer they are a mismatch). Numbers the apps count with (a vault's `seq`, `manifest_seq`, `key_epoch`, a row's `deleted_at`) above 2^53 − 1, below 0 or not whole make the answer unreadable; nothing is added to a server's number without an overflow check. **Writes:** create, edit, move (both vaults), trash/restore/delete (revision + `manifest_seq`/`manifest_prev`/`manifest` in the query), AutoFill saves, add-site, passkeys, attachment list changes and key replacement (plain and signed: `VaultManifest.rotation`, a new manifest under the new key and epoch that names the old one by `manifestLink`) carry the vault's next manifest from the core's builders, which name the manifest they replace. An own write is applied to the copy, and its link remembered (`ManifestEntry.mark`), only when the answer's `manifest_link` is the link of the manifest sent (the version alone isn't enough: a create sent twice gets the first attempt's answer); any other link, none, or an answer that says the vault's number didn't move: sync and check. `manifest_conflict`/`manifest_required` are never the "overwrite?" conflict: sync, check, the item's own rules, resend, 3 sends at most. A vault with a warning refuses writes on the device ("This vault can't be changed until its warning is resolved.", also in the AutoFill sheets, which show the warning under the list). AutoFill never hands over a login unseen while the copy warns about it (`VaultData.fillNeedsReview`: its vault is warned, or it is an older version with no newer copy kept): the fill without interface answers "interface required", and a suggestion that opens the interface shows the list with the warning line (`fillWarning`) for the user to pick from instead of filling at once. **Rows that don't open** (round 3, T1; the same three cases in every app, told apart by the core's error: a failed decryption against its JSON error for data that decrypted): (a) only the served `item_key` fails and this device holds the row with the same id, vault and `data` that it opened before (the copy shown, read from the stored copy at unlock): it goes on with the row it has and the copy keeps that key (`OpenedVault.rekeyed`); nothing is listed, warned or held (an honest server never changes a key without the data). (b) The row decrypts but isn't an item this app can read: listed in `VaultData.newer` as "Item from a newer version of PassAuth" / "Update PassAuth to see this item."; no notice, no hold, "Delete for good" for the vault's owners and admins only (`deleteNewer`, own confirmation, same manifest write as an item that can't be opened; a key replacement stopped by such rows says to update or have an owner delete them), counted in manifests like any row, no obstacle to a first manifest. (c) It can't be decrypted (also a row that came with its data but without its key, and in the iOS app, which asks for whole rows, one without its data): listed in `VaultData.unopenable` as "Item that can't be opened" (with the name this device last read for it, when it did), and its vault shows the notice "An item in this vault can't be opened." (`VaultInfo.unopenable`). The verdict stays the manifest's own (the row is counted by its data) and **no write is held for it**. Members who can edit get "Delete for good" on the row (confirm: "This item can't be opened on this device. It may still open on another device or in a newer version of PassAuth. Deleting it removes it for everyone."; `deleteUnopenable`: `DELETE /items/{id}/permanent` with the vault's next manifest without the row as synced, live rows too; the plain request in a vault without a manifest; refused while the vault's manifest is wrong, which "Use the server's copy" settles first). No first manifest is started over a case-(c) row; the notice stays after an acceptance while the row remains. The macOS AutoFill window shows the notice (the delete is done in the web vault or the desktop app). **"Use the server's copy"** (`useServerCopy`, `Manifests.serverCopy`; offered to everyone when `manifestAccept` gives `ok`/`none` for the served rows, else to members who can write; readers then see "Someone who can edit this vault can review it."): one full fetch, then either a local accept by any role (memory replaced with the served head, also downwards, or forgotten when the server shows no manifest; `accepts + 1`; no request) or, for writers, a baseline at exactly row + 1 naming `manifestLink(row)` (memory replaced with it when the answer's `manifest_link` is its link; 409 = again, 3 times; 429 = the usual rate-limit message, warning kept); a last full fetch then takes the vault's items as served: the edit counters and kept newer copies are dropped for the items this phone's own copy had in that vault (`ItemRevs.acceptedIds`), never for an id only that fetch lists there. When the server shows no manifest for a vault this phone remembers one of (`Judged.noRecord`, `VaultInfo.manifestNoRecord`) the confirmation is its own text ("Your server no longer shows a record of changes for this vault. …"; an acceptance confirmed with the ordinary text isn't carried out as a forget: "This vault changed on another device. Try again.", and the warning then offers the right text), the forgotten entry is marked `not_checked`, and the vault carries the quiet line "This vault isn't being checked for missing or restored items." until a manifest is remembered again. A restored server (a lower `revision` and lower vault numbers than the copy) is synced and judged like any answer: an unlocked sync never keeps its copy because the answer is older (`SyncDecision.isStale` with vault numbers, `syncLocked`); only the locked refresh and `adoptRefreshed` refuse an older copy, and they show nothing. **Rows that don't open** (`ItemOpening.open`, called for every row in `decryptVault`; the cases are told apart by the core's error: `DecryptFailed`/malformed text = not decrypted, `Invalid("json: …")` or the app's own reader failing = decrypted but unknown content): (a) only the served `item_key` fails and the phone has a row with the same id, vault and `data` whose key opens it (stored copy, copy shown, a waiting version): the item is used as before and the copy keeps the phone's key (nothing warned or held; a key replacement re-encrypts from that key, `Manifests.rotationRows`); (b) it decrypts but this app can't read it: listed as "Item from a newer version of PassAuth" / "Update PassAuth to see this item.", no warning, "Delete for good" for owners and admins only, counted in manifests, no obstacle to a first manifest; (c) it doesn't decrypt: listed as "Item that can't be opened" (with the name of an earlier version the phone holds) and its vault shows "An item in this vault can't be opened." (`ManifestState.Unopenable` when the manifest passes): **no write is held for it** (`Manifests.writable`), members who can write get "Delete for good" (`deleteUnopenable`: `DELETE /items/{id}/permanent` with the next manifest; confirm text says the item may still open elsewhere), no first manifest is started over such a row, the notice stays after a baseline or accept while one is there, and such a vault gets no new key on this phone (every item would have to be encrypted again). Trashed rows are summaries on this app (no key or data), so a trashed row that doesn't open can't be told and doesn't hold off a first manifest here; a trashed row the copy does hold with key and data is checked and listed like a live one. **Unreadable memory**: vaults with a manifest are `Unchecked` (writes refused with "PassAuth couldn't check this vault with your server yet. Try again in a moment."), vaults without one stay writable, nothing is started, purged or written over it; after 3 failed reads in a row across a finished sync (`UnreadableMemory`; any of the three files counts: `.manifests`, the item edit counters `.revs` or the trash times `.trashseen` alone too) the list says "PassAuth can't read this device's record of your vaults." with "Reset on this device" (`resetDeviceMemory`: the unreadable files are moved aside as `*.unreadable`, the phone goes on as one that remembers nothing). Answers with a `seq`, `manifest_seq`, `key_epoch` or `deleted_at` outside 0..2^53−1 are refused; `/sync?trash=summary` rows keep `data_sha256`, a cached row without it is fetched again in full once; a newer copy kept after an item rollback is counted by the hash of the served data (`served_sha256`, local only, never taken from a server) and takes the served `revision`. Writes (`ItemWrites`: create, edit, move, trash, key replacement, also from the autofill and credential services) carry the next manifest; `manifest_conflict` / `manifest_required` sync and retry (3 sends), never the overwrite dialog. First manifest (always version 1; never over a vault with a kept newer copy, an item marked as served older or a row that doesn't decrypt) and old-trash purge (one request each, one chunk per sync) only after a foreground sync, never from the background worker; old trash goes only when the server's rule says so (strictly `deleted_at < server_time − trash_days·86400`), this phone has itself seen the item in the trash for max(`trash_days`, 7) days (`TrashSeen`, `vault/<id>.trashseen`) and the server's clock is within 24 h of the phone's (`Manifests.dueTrash`, overflow-safe); a key replacement re-encrypts exactly the checked copy (`Manifests.rotationRows`: the fresh full `/sync` only supplies the trashed items' data and must match the copy row by row; kept newer copies are what is re-encrypted); the emergency view builds the rows it counts from the fields it read (`Emergency.manifestRows`), a row without `data`/`item_key`, with a `deleted_at` that is no time, or that doesn't open is a mismatch, and a vault without a manifest is shown with "This vault has no record of changes yet, …"; a warned vault takes no writes and no new key. Signed sharing's own writes at a sync (tombstones of vaults no longer listed, key pins, the status shown) happen only for an answer that is kept (`SharingChecks.commit`). Sends: a v1 Send that has a password takes a new name or text only together with its password on a server with `send_v2` ("Enter this Send's password to change its text."). A server whose `/api/config` lacks `signed_sharing` is refused in `setServer`, sign-in, sign-up and before every `/sync` (`ServerSupport`; the stored copy stays readable); a config that can't be read counts only with a feature list kept from before (`ServerSupport.decide`: none kept = can't connect; the list survives the record reset after an account is deleted), and the signed-sharing checks are always on, whatever the list says.

- Offline changes (contract in "Offline changes"; `Shared/OfflineChanges.swift` = pure rules, `Shared/OfflineSync.swift` = list storage, waiting saves, sender, decisions; tests `OfflineChangesTests`, `OfflineQueueRepoTests`; UI `App/OfflineViews.swift`): `VaultRepository.served` is the server's copy and `vault` that copy with the account's list on top (`OfflineQueue.overlay`; `Entry.pending` = marker), so lists, search, item pages, AutoFill fills and the identity store show waiting changes. A create/edit/trash waits when the app knows it is offline (NWPathMonitor, or the copy marked offline by a request without an answer), when its request gets no answer or 502/503/504, or when the item already has a change; the last attempted body is kept as `sent`. The list is `vault/<id>.offline` in the app group, sealed by the core with the account key and wrapped with the store key, changed under the memory lock; the count (a plain number) is `AccountData.offlineChanges` for the warnings while locked. AutoFill saves sync first, add to the list and never send. The app sends (active account only) after each checked sync, single-flight, oldest first, through the normal write path (manifests, `afterWrite`); a returning connection or foreground triggers that sync. Beyond the contract: the vault timeout's Log out keeps the list; Move and Delete forever ask to send or discard a waiting change first; restoring an item trashed offline drops that change. macOS AutoFill compiles the code with `OfflineQueue.enabled` false.

## Desktop app (`desktop/`)
- Tauri 2 shell around the web vault: `desktop/src-tauri` (Rust, its own Cargo workspace so the root workspace, Docker image and `cargo test --workspace` never see it) and `desktop/src` (TypeScript entry that imports `web/src` through the `@web` alias, like the extension; React deduped). The vault UI, crypto (`web/src/wasm`, built by `scripts/build-wasm.sh web/src/wasm`) and all pages are the web vault's; nothing is forked.
- Web seams (`web/src/lib/server.ts`, `platform.ts`, `prefs.ts`): `api_fetch` (desktop) returns `{status, headers, body}`, with `headers` limited to `content-type`, `retry-after` and `x-request-id` (`RESPONSE_HEADERS`); `desktop/src/server.ts` builds the `Response` from them (null body for 101/103/204/205/304), so 429 waits and request ids work as in the browser (the web `ApiError.requestId` is the error body's `request_id`, else `x-request-id`, and a 500 `internal` names it as a reference for the admin). the web vault calls `server.apiUrl()` / `server.wsUrl()` / `server.fetch`, keeps its secrets (tokens `pa.session`, `pa.email`, remembered Secret Key `pa.sk.<email>`, 2FA `pa.remember.<email>`) in `secretStore`, and reads `isDesktop()` / `getDesktop()` hooks. In the browser and extension `server.base` is `""`, `secretStore` is localStorage and no hooks are registered, so behaviour is unchanged (`/api`, `location.host`). Desktop: `configureServer({ base, fetch })` where `fetch` is the `api_fetch` command (only Rust sends API requests; the WebView itself opens only the live-sync WebSocket, the site icons and the breach check, and once a server is chosen its CSP allows no other host, see Hardening); `secretStore` is an in-memory mirror persisted through `hooks.secrets` to the OS keychain (`keyring` crate, service `io.passauth.desktop`, one entry per `<host>|<key>` so two servers never share a session; `Root.tsx` preloads them before `boot()`; without a keychain backend the app falls back to WebView localStorage under the same names and warns once); the live-sync WebSocket is a normal WebView socket to `wss://server/api/ws` (the server checks no Origin). Hooks: `saveFile` (native save dialog + Rust write; WebViews don't implement `<a download>`; toasts "Saved" / "Couldn't save the file…"), `copySecret` (`copy_secret`: `arboard` with history exclusion, cleared after 30 s if unchanged), `changeServer`, `checkForUpdates(tellUpToDate)` / `installUpdate`, `openUrl` (opener), `version`, `lockShortcut` (shown as `<kbd>` next to Lock). `prefs.onThemeChange` → `window.setTheme`, so the title bar and native dialogs follow the vault's theme. On desktop the Emergency Kit's "Print" is "Save kit" (WebKit can't print from the WebView) and `deviceInfo()` names the session "PassAuth desktop on <OS>".
- Flow: first run shows **Connect to your server** (origin only, a pasted page address is fine; `https://` required except `localhost`/`127.0.0.1`; `set_server` + `GET /api/config` must answer, reqwest errors mapped to "certificate isn't trusted" / "can't reach" / "took too long") → the web vault's Login/Register → vault. The address is kept in WebView localStorage (`desktop.server`) and re-validated with `parseServer` at launch. "Change server" (Login footer, Settings → About, Vault menu; confirmed in-app when signed in) logs out, clears the secret mirror and every `pa.*` localStorage key, and shows Connect prefilled with the old address. Splash after 10 s: "Still connecting to {host}…" + Change server.
- Shell (`src-tauri/src/lib.rs`): one window (1100×760, min 900×620, state remembered).
