Menus on every platform (Title Case): macOS PassAuth (About, Settings… ⌘,, Check for Updates…, Hide, Quit) / Vault (Lock Vault ⌘L, Change Server…) / Edit / Window / Help (PassAuth Help → website, Report a Problem → issues); Windows and Linux File (Lock Vault Ctrl+L, Change Server…, Settings…, Exit) / Help (Check for Updates…, Report a Problem, About PassAuth). Tray everywhere: Open PassAuth, Lock Vault (enabled only while unlocked: the webview emits `vault-state`), Check for Updates…, Quit PassAuth. Menu accelerators replace any keydown handler; the items emit `lock`, `change-server`, `settings`, `check-updates` to the webview. Closing the window hides it on every platform (tray / File → Exit / Cmd+Q quit); on macOS the app then becomes a menu-bar-only (accessory) app unless "Keep the Dock icon while the window is closed" is on (`ShellSettings.keep_dock`, kept by the shell in `shell.json` in the app config directory through `shell_settings_get/set`, because it applies before the webview loads); showing the window makes it a regular app again (`apply_dock`); single instance; `on_navigation` allows the app's own origin plus `about:`/`blob:` frames; `on_new_window` opens http(s) in the browser and denies the window; drag-drop handler off so the Import page's HTML drop works. Commands: `set_server`, `api_fetch` (`api_url`: `{base}api{path}`, the path starting with `/` without `://` and, once normalised, still on the server's origin under `/api/` (`..`, `%2e%2e`, backslashes refused); methods GET/POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE; request headers only `API_REQUEST_HEADERS` = `accept`, `authorization`, `content-type`, `x-passauth-access-code`, `x-passauth-client`, `x-reauth-token`, anything else (`Host` included) or a value with control characters refused, so a header the web vault starts sending must be added there; `redirect(Policy::none())`; 30 s total timeout; an answer over `API_BODY_MAX` = 128 MiB (room for a large unpaginated `/sync`) refused, by `Content-Length` before reading or while reading), `secret_get/set/delete`, `copy_secret`, `save_file` (suggests `files::safe_file_name`). Lock: the web vault's 15-minute idle lock plus 15 minutes hidden/minimised.
- File transfers (`src-tauri/src/transfer.rs`, `desktop/src/transfers.ts`; seam `server.transfers` = `NativeTransfers` in `web/src/lib/server.ts`, hook `saveBytes`): attachment files are bytes, which `api_fetch` (strings) can't carry, so they have their own commands on the same single network path: the connected server only (`{base}api{path}` re-checked to stay on its origin, no redirects), only `PUT /attachments/{id}`, `GET /attachments/{id}` and `GET /emergency-access/{grant}/attachments/{id}` (ids 1–64 of `[A-Za-z0-9_-]`, method fixed per command), only the `authorization` and `x-passauth-client` request headers from the WebView (Rust sets `content-type` and `content-length`), only `RESPONSE_HEADERS` back. Bytes cross the IPC raw, never JSON or base64. Upload: `upload_start {path, headers, size, onProgress: Channel}` → id; `upload_chunk` (≈1 MiB raw IPC body, the id in the `passauth-transfer` IPC header) resolves when the request body has room (a one-piece queue keeps the WebView at most two pieces ahead), `false` once the server answered early; `upload_finish {id}` → `{status, headers, body}`. Download: `download_start` → `{id, status, headers, body}`; `download_read {id}` returns what has arrived (≤ ≈1 MiB; empty = the end) from a 32-piece queue — a pull rather than a Channel push, because Tauri channels have no backpressure; the WebView decrypts with `FileDecryptor`. `transfer_cancel {id}` stops any transfer; `set_server` stops them all. Limits: 4 GiB per file, 16 MiB per piece; a second reqwest client without the 30 s total timeout (connect 30 s, download headers 30 s, an answer's text (≤ 1 MiB) within 30 s in all, 60 s without a byte ends a transfer, an upload's answer within 120 s of its last byte). Saving: `saveBytes` → `save_start {filename}` opens the native save dialog from Rust, suggesting `files::safe_file_name(filename)` (no dialog or filesystem capability in the WebView, which never names a path); `save_chunk` writes to a hidden `.part` file beside the target (keeping at most 100 bytes of the name); `save_finish` fsyncs it, marks it as downloaded from the internet and renames it; a cancel or failure deletes it. The mark goes on before the rename (it moves with the file), so the file never sits under its name unmarked: macOS `com.apple.quarantine` = `0081;<Unix seconds, 8 hex digits>;PassAuth;` (the flags LaunchServices writes for an app that isn't sandboxed, with the agent's name and no download-log row, `setxattr(…, XATTR_NOFOLLOW)`); Windows the `Zone.Identifier` stream `[ZoneTransfer]\r\nZoneId=3\r\n` (Chrome's format); Linux has no standard mark. Gatekeeper, SmartScreen and Office Protected View then treat an opened attachment like a browser download; a file system without the mark (FAT, some network shares) only logs to stderr and keeps the file. `safe_file_name` (also used by `save_file`) removes Unicode Cc/Cf (bidi overrides and isolates, directional marks, zero-width characters, BOM, tags) and U+2028/2029, turns `/ \ : * ? " < > |` into `_`, folds white space, removes dots and spaces at either end, gives Windows device names (CON, PRN, AUX, NUL, CONIN$, CONOUT$, COM0–9, LPT0–9, COM¹–³, LPT¹–³, any extension) a leading `_`, keeps at most 200 bytes with an extension of up to 16, and never returns an empty name (`attachment`); cleaning twice changes nothing, so names the web vault cleaned pass unchanged. Tests: `transfer.rs`, `files.rs`, `lib.rs` (unit; loopback HTTP, including a server that trickles its answer; IPC on Tauri's mock runtime via the dev-dependency feature `test`, `api_fetch` included; on macOS the quarantine attribute is read back with `xattr -p`).
- Hardening: production CSP (`script-src 'self' 'wasm-unsafe-eval'`, `connect-src 'self' ipc: http://ipc.localhost https://api.pwnedpasswords.com wss: ws://localhost:* ws://127.0.0.1:*` (api.pwnedpasswords.com: the password-health breach check, the one request the WebView makes itself instead of through the native client), `img-src` https + data/blob for icons and QR codes), capabilities limited to what the TS calls (`core:default`, `core:window:allow-set-theme`, updater, process relaunch, opener `allow-open-url` + `allow-default-urls`, `deep-link:allow-get-current`), no http, clipboard, dialog or filesystem capability in the WebView. The build-time CSP has to allow any server, so `desktop/src/csp.ts` narrows it at runtime: when `Root` boots a server it adds a second policy in a `<meta>` tag, `connect-src 'self' ipc: http://ipc.localhost https://api.pwnedpasswords.com wss://<server host>` (`ws://` for an http server) and `img-src 'self' data: blob: <server origin>`. Both policies are enforced, so from then on the WebView reaches only that server's WebSocket and icons (checked in WKWebView and Chromium: other hosts are blocked before any request). A policy can't be loosened, so booting a different server than the page is pinned to reloads the page (`location.replace("/")`); the first connect and a normal start don't reload, and `AddCode.tsx` remembers handled verification-code links (sessionStorage `desktop.handledLinks`, the last 8 as truncated SHA-256 hashes, never the link) so a reload doesn't offer the same one again. Not applied in dev builds or for an IPv6-literal server (CSP can't name one). A host the WebView must reach goes into both policies. Vite dev server `fs.allow` = `web/src`, `web/public`, `desktop`; `deny` `**/Keys/**` and key files. reqwest uses rustls with the OS trust store (`rustls-platform-verifier`) and the ring provider the updater also uses. `cargo audit` in CI covers `desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.lock` too.
- Verification-code links: `tauri-plugin-deep-link` registers `otpauth` and `apple-otpauth` (`tauri.conf.json` `plugins.deep-link.desktop.schemes`: macOS `CFBundleURLTypes`, so the app can be picked in System Settings → AutoFill & Passwords → "Set Up Codes In"; the Windows installer and deb/rpm register them too; an AppImage calls `register_all` at launch). macOS delivers links to the running app; on Windows/Linux a link starts a second process whose argument the single-instance plugin (feature `deep-link`) hands to the running one. Rust only shows the window (`on_open_url`); `desktop/src/AddCode.tsx` reads `getCurrent()` (link that launched the app) and `onOpenUrl`, turns `apple-otpauth:` into `otpauth:`, parses with `desktop/src/otpauth.ts` (same rules and messages as the iOS app's `OtpAuth`, the core's `totpCode` has the last word; HOTP and `otpauth-migration` refused), keeps the key in memory until the vault is unlocked ("Unlock PassAuth to save the verification code."), then shows "Add verification code" (search, New login, writable non-trashed logins with issuer matches first). Choosing one opens the web vault's editor through `web/src/lib/drafts.ts` (`openDraft(itemId, apply)`: the vault page shows that item's editor with the change applied; `Shell` drops the draft when the route leaves that item and the desktop drops it when the vault stops being unlocked; if the open editor has unsaved changes (`ItemEditor` `onDirtyChange`) the desktop asks "Discard your changes?" first; nothing is saved until the user saves; browser and extension never open a draft). Capability: `deep-link:allow-get-current` only.
- Updates: `tauri-plugin-updater` with the minisign public key in `tauri.conf.json` and endpoint `https://github.com/Fahed-Alhammadi/PassAuth/releases/latest/download/latest.json` (GitHub's "latest" skips pre-releases). Checks 10 s after launch, at most once a day (silent unless an update exists), and on demand (menu, tray, Settings → About). The flow is in-app on the web vault's `Modal` (`desktop/src/updater.tsx`): checking → "You're on the latest version" / "PassAuth X.Y.Z is available" with Install and restart / Not now → progress ("PassAuth closes and reopens when it's done.") → relaunch; failures show "Couldn't check for updates…" with the raw error behind a Details disclosure, and once the user confirmed an install every error is shown. Never installs without confirmation. The private key is GitHub secret `TAURI_SIGNING_PRIVATE_KEY` (copy in `Keys/`); losing it means installed apps can't verify any future update.
- Vault timeout (`desktop/src/timeout.ts`; Settings → Account → "Vault timeout", a card the web vault renders only when the `vaultTimeout` hook is present): the extension's options and meaning. Minutes (default 15) count from the last keyboard/mouse activity in the window, deadline-based so a hidden window (throttled timers) still locks on its next tick or when shown; "Immediately" locks when the window is closed to the tray or minimised; "On system lock" (macOS only: `watch_system_lock` in `lib.rs` observes `NSWorkspaceWillSleepNotification`, `NSWorkspaceSessionDidResignActiveNotification` and the distributed `com.apple.screenIsLocked`, emitting `system-lock`; Windows/Linux have no observer yet, so the option isn't offered there); "On app restart" keeps keys in memory only; "Never" stores the account session bytes in the keychain as `pa.keys` (host-scoped like the other secrets) and `applyTimeoutAtStart` opens the vault with them after `boot()`. Timeout action lock (default) or sign out; with sign out, a restart signs out (as the extension does). The web vault's own 15-minute idle lock (`useAutoLock`) is skipped when the hook exists. `pa.keys` is removed whenever the vault leaves "unlocked" (⌘L, menu, tray, timeout), on sign-out (server rejection or the user), on change server and when the setting leaves "never"; a server that can't be reached keeps it (status "unreachable", or `UnreachableError` from the restore's sync), and the restore is retried every 30 s and after each `boot()` ("Try again"). The sign-out action locks (keys dropped, `pa.keys` removed) before calling `/auth/logout`. `desktop/src/secrets.ts` serialises keychain calls per entry name, so a delete issued right after a set can't overtake it on the blocking thread pool; `secretStore.set` returns the keychain promise so lock paths await the wipe. Without a keychain (`keychainAvailable()` false: WebView-storage fallback) "Never" is disabled in the card ("Not available: this computer has no keychain service") and keys are never persisted. Setting lives in WebView localStorage `desktop.vaultTimeout`, per install. Keychain entries are filed under the bundle identifier (`app.config().identifier`, = `io.passauth.desktop`), so a test build with another identifier never touches the real app's secrets.
- "This app" (`desktop/src/app.ts`, hook `DesktopHooks.app`, card in Settings → Account): **Start at login** = an OS login item through `tauri-plugin-autostart` (macOS LaunchAgent, Windows registry Run key, Linux autostart .desktop) that launches the app with `--hidden`, so `build_window` creates the window invisible and the app sits in the tray (macOS: accessory policy until the window is opened); the card shows the OS's answer (`isEnabled`), and when it is on the app calls `enable()` again once per launch, because the login item stores the executable's path and `isEnabled` only checks that the entry exists (a moved app or a hand-downloaded AppImage would otherwise leave a dead login item that still shows as on). An update restart keeps this run's arguments (`relaunch`, and the Windows installer's `/ARGS`), so `installUpdate` first calls `prepare_relaunch` (one-shot `showNextLaunch` in `shell.json`): the next launch shows the window even with `--hidden`, and the flag is cleared when consumed or when the install failed. A second launch (single-instance plugin) shows the window unless it carries `--hidden` itself. **Keep the Dock icon** (macOS only, default off, see above). **Background sync** (default every 2 hours; Off/30 min/1 h/2 h/6 h/12 h, localStorage `desktop.app`): the live WebSocket pushes changes while unlocked, but can die silently after sleep or a network change, so while the vault is unlocked a one-minute ticker calls `resync()` (`web/src/lib/vault.ts`: `/sync/revision` first, which also refreshes an expired access token, then reopen the socket, then `sync()` if the server is newer) on the schedule, when a tick arrives more than 5 minutes late (the computer slept), on macOS `NSWorkspaceDidWakeNotification` (`system-wake` from `watch_system_lock`), and when the window becomes visible/focused if the last check is older than 5 minutes. Nothing runs while locked: desktop keeps no offline copy.
- Resource use: the hidden window keeps its webview (closing = `hide()`, so the account keys in wasm memory survive; destroying it would be a lock). While unlocked the live WebSocket is the only connection; when it drops, the reconnect delay starts at 3 s and doubles to 2 min (jitter, reset on open and on `resync()`), and while `navigator.onLine` is false nothing is retried until the `online` event. Timers while hidden: the one-minute background-sync ticker and the 30 s timeout ticker, both only while unlocked; nothing animates. `desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.toml` has its own `[profile.release]` (LTO, one codegen unit, stripped, `opt-level = "s"`, `panic = "abort"`) because the crate is outside the root workspace. Fonts: only the latin and latin-ext IBM Plex subsets are bundled. Measured 2026-11-09 on macOS, idle in the tray for 8 h: host process 35 MB footprint, WebKit content process 70 MB, GPU + networking helpers 28 MB, 0% CPU.
- Keychain hygiene: entries are `<host>|pa.*` under service `io.passauth.desktop`; **Change server** deletes the old host's `pa.email`, `pa.sk.<email>` and `pa.remember.<email>` besides the session and persisted keys, so servers the user left don't accumulate entries. Sign-out keeps `pa.sk`/`pa.remember` for the same server on purpose.
- Signing: macOS builds are signed with the Developer ID Application certificate (`APPLE_CERTIFICATE` + `APPLE_CERTIFICATE_PASSWORD`, p12 with Apple's intermediate; copy in `Keys/`) and notarized with the App Store Connect API key secrets; without the certificate the build is unsigned and Gatekeeper warns. Windows builds are unsigned (SmartScreen warns) until a code-signing certificate is added. Linux: AppImage (updatable), .deb and .rpm.
- Security round 7/8/9 (web vault + desktop, shared `web/src`): own-vault wraps v2 (`newVaultKeyBound` at 0 / `row.key_epoch + 1` in `createVault` and key replacement) only while the last sync's `user.account_wraps_v2 === true`; opens pass the row's epoch to `pinsLegacyWrapAllowed`, and `signed-sharing.ts` observes opened v2 own rows with `bound_wrap`. Sends: v2 for new/changed passwords when `send_v2` is listed (`send-client.ts`), the visitor's worker returns `{verifier, contentKey}` (`send-kdf.ts`) and the page opens either format with `sendOpen`. Item counters: `item-revs.ts` keeps item id → highest `rev` in local storage `pa.item-revs.<server>|<account>` (kept on Change server like trust memory), checked in `materialize` for every fresh decrypt (full, delta, live); a rollback keeps the newer copy this page holds (else shows the served one) marked `rollback`, with the item and sync warnings; ids no longer served are dropped.
- Build: `scripts/build-wasm.sh web/src/wasm && (cd web && pnpm install) && cd desktop && pnpm install && pnpm tauri dev` (web deps are needed because `web/src` imports resolve from `web/node_modules`) (against a `http://localhost:8091` throwaway server, typed with the scheme) or `pnpm tauri build`. Version in `desktop/package.json` + `desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.toml` (`tauri.conf.json` has none). Icons in `src-tauri/icons` were generated with `pnpm tauri icon` from a square render of the logo.

- Offline changes (desktop only; contract in "Offline changes"): capability = hook `DesktopHooks.offlineChanges` (`desktop/src/offline.ts`); without it (browser web vault) every write behaves as before. Rules in `web/src/lib/offline-changes.ts` (pure, vitest). `vault.ts` opens the sealed list before the first sync after unlock, keeps the server copy as `serverItems` and shows `state.items` = server copy with the list applied, plus `State.offlineChanges` for markers and status; sends through the existing `postItem`/`saveOver`/`changeItemState` paths (manifests, key epochs, rev memory unchanged) after every checked sync, on `online`, on `resync()` and every minute while changes wait (only `waiting` ones; held changes retry at the next ordinary sync; single run; a new change to an item waits for that item's send). The shell stores each list in `<app data>/offline-changes/<hex of host|account id>.sealed` (`offline_changes_get/set`, atomic, ≤ 12 MiB). The desktop keeps no offline copy across restarts, so changes are made while the vault stays unlocked; the list survives lock and restart and is sent after the next unlock. UI `components/OfflineChanges.tsx` (row marker, sync status, item bar with Decide…/Discard this change, the conflict sheet, sign-out/Change server/Delete account warnings). Beyond the contract: a trash goes ahead when the server's data equals `sent.data` also with a `base_revision` (an edit that may have been stored, then trashed); an edit of an item another device moved to another vault gets the deleted sheet (Save mine as a new item / Discard mine) and "Move to trash anyway" follows the item to its new vault; held changes without an item row are listed with Discard in the sync status; restore/delete for good/move are refused for an item with a waiting change; only an explicit Sign out, Change server or Delete account deletes the list (session loss or timeout sign-out keeps it); while locked the warning has no count; a new item saved offline can't take files.

### macOS AutoFill extension (`desktop/macos/`)
- Native credential provider `PassAuthAutoFill.appex` (`io.passauth.desktop.autofill`, display name PassAuth, macOS 14+ while the host app runs from macOS 12 (`minimumSystemVersion`): on 12/13 the appex is simply absent and the app works without AutoFill), embedded at `PassAuth.app/Contents/PlugIns/`; listed in System Settings → General → AutoFill & Passwords. Capabilities: ProvidesPasswords, ProvidesPasskeys, ProvidesOneTimeCodes (macOS 15), ShowsConfigurationUI. No save/generate requests (iOS 26.2 only, unavailable on macOS).
- Ported, not forked: XcodeGen project `desktop/macos/project.yml` builds `ios/Shared` as the static library `PassAuthKit` (minus `QrDecoder.swift`), the UniFFI bindings as `PassAuthCore` (static, from `scripts/build-macos-core.sh`: universal arm64 + x86_64 `desktop/macos/Core/PassAuthCoreFFI.xcframework` + `Core/Sources/PassAuthCore.swift`, gitignored, same bindgen as iOS), and the appex from `ios/AutoFill/AutofillModel.swift` (the whole request flow: matching, fill, TOTP copy, passkey create/assert, one-time codes, identity store sync), `ios/UI/Theme.swift` (colours, logo) and the macOS-only `desktop/macos/AutoFill/` (`NSViewController` subclass hosting SwiftUI in `NSHostingView`, 460×560 sheet; `MacViews.swift`, `MacSignIn.swift`, `TimedUnlock*.swift`). Platform differences in shared code are `#if os(iOS)` / `os(macOS)` / `#available(iOS 18.0, macOS 15.0, *)`; the iOS build is unchanged.
- Delta sync as on iOS, with `trash=summary` (`VaultRepository.trashSummary` is true on macOS: its own copy, no trash view); the server's feature list is kept with the account, so a per-request process doesn't ask `/api/config` every time.
- Self-contained (shares no session with the Tauri app; see the hand-off below): its own sign-in in the configuration UI and in the sheet when signed out (server with the iOS https/LAN-http rules and confirmation, email, master password, Secret Key, two-step login with remember-this-Mac), its own device on the server ("PassAuth for Mac AutoFill", kind `desktop`), unlock with master password or Touch ID (`.biometryCurrentSet` item), settings (gear button / configuration screen: vault timeout, Touch ID, TOTP copy, default URI match detection (same options and help as iOS Settings → AutoFill, per device; a change rewrites the identity store), lock now, sign out). `SecureStore` runs with `role: .app` (it owns its state); files live in the extension's sandbox container (Application Support; no App Group, no file-protection class on macOS), secrets in the data-protection Keychain (`kSecUseDataProtectionKeychain`) under its own group `JEXQBK3SQ5.io.passauth.desktop.autofill` (Info.plist `PAKeychainGroup`). Clipboard: `NSPasteboard` marked concealed + transient; never cleared by the extension (its process ends right after the request, so the clearing delay is ignored on macOS and the settings say so).
- Vault timeout: macOS starts the extension per request, so the unlocked keys (session bytes) are kept in its Keychain with a deadline that slides on each use (`TimedUnlock`, rules in `TimedUnlockPolicy`, unit-tested): "Immediately" keeps nothing, "Never" uses the repository's persisted keys, "On app restart" isn't offered. Lock now / sign out remove them.
- Desktop → extension hand-off (2026-11-09): the extension's separate sign-in confused users ("the app is running, why connect again?"). The Tauri app now leaves a Keychain item in the extension's access group (`keychain-access-groups` in `entitlements/macos-host.plist`; data-protection keychain via `security-framework`, service `io.passauth.desktop.handoff`, account `handoff`, its own service so the extension's fresh-install `Keychain.deleteAll` keeps it) with JSON `{server, email, secretKey}` whenever the vault is unlocked (`desktop/src/handoff.ts` → command `autofill_handoff`; `{}` on Change server). Never tokens or vault keys: the extension must get its own session (a shared refresh token would be flagged as reuse by the server and end both sessions). `MacSignIn` reads it (`Keychain.readDesktopHandoff`, `DesktopHandoff`): with no server yet it connects to the handed-over server by itself (`setServer(allowLanHttp: true)`: plain http was already accepted in the app) and shows the login step with email and Secret Key filled and a notice, so only the master password is typed; a signed-out extension also gets email + Secret Key prefilled when the hand-off is for its server. Everything after that (Touch ID, timed unlock, its own device on the server) is unchanged.
- Identity store on macOS (2026-11-09): nothing but the extension writes `ASCredentialIdentityStore` there (on iOS the app does too), and macOS ends the extension right after a request, so `AutofillModel` answers the system (`handOver`: complete, cancel, configuration done) only after `repo.identities.flush(timeout: 2 s)`; before that fix the write started by the unlock/sync was killed with the process and Safari only ever offered the full list instead of the site's logins. `CredentialIdentitySync` skips a write when the records' fingerprint equals the last one written; since every request is a fresh process, that fingerprint is persisted (`StoredFile.identityFingerprint`, set by `onWritten` only after the system store took the write, "" after it was emptied, cleared by `invalidate()`) and seeded on the first `refreshIdentities()`, so an unchanged store isn't rewritten (and waited for) on every request. Shared with iOS, where the app and the extension write the same store. Diagnostics (counts only) in the unified log, subsystem `io.passauth.autofill` (categories `requests`, `list`, `identities`): `log show --last 10m --predicate 'subsystem == "io.passauth.autofill"'`.
- Build: `desktop/macos/build.sh` → `desktop/macos/build/PassAuthAutoFill.appex` (Release, universal; runs `scripts/build-macos-core.sh` if the core is missing; version = `desktop/package.json`, build number as iOS). Signed when `MACOS_SIGN_IDENTITY` is set (+ `MACOS_AUTOFILL_PROFILE` = the Developer ID provisioning profile, copied to `Contents/embedded.provisionprofile`; optional `MACOS_SIGN_KEYCHAIN`), with hardened runtime, timestamp and `desktop/macos/AutoFill/AutoFill.entitlements` (sandbox, network client, AutoFill, Keychain group, application/team identifier); otherwise ad hoc with `AutoFill-adhoc.entitlements` (sandbox + network only: builds, but macOS won't use it). The profile accepts only the certificate it was made for (the `APPLE_CERTIFICATE` p12, SHA-1 F81B…); another Developer ID certificate gets the appex killed by AMFI ("No matching profile found"). Tests: `cd desktop/macos && xcodegen generate && xcodebuild -scheme PassAuthAutoFill -destination 'platform=macOS' test` (TimedUnlockPolicy + the shared UrlMatcher/ItemJson/OtpAuth/Autofill tests from `ios/Tests` on macOS).
- The containing app needs the AutoFill entitlement too: System Settings skips a credential provider whose host app lacks `com.apple.developer.authentication-services.autofill-credential-provider` (log: "Skipping extension … because its containing app is missing the … entitlement"; seen 2026-11-08 on macOS 27). So the Tauri app is signed with `desktop/src-tauri/entitlements/macos-host.plist` (application-identifier `JEXQBK3SQ5.io.passauth.desktop`, team-identifier, the AutoFill entitlement; no sandbox) and carries its own Developer ID profile for App ID `io.passauth.desktop` (capability AutoFill Credential Provider) at `Contents/embedded.provisionprofile` (`Keys/MACOS_DESKTOP_PROVISIONING_PROFILE.provisionprofile`, secret of the same name); without the profile, or signed with a certificate the profile doesn't name, AMFI kills the app at launch ("No matching profile found"). Both are CI-only config like the appex.
- Packaging (desktop CI): build and sign the appex before `tauri build`, put it at `PassAuth.app/Contents/PlugIns/PassAuthAutoFill.appex` (e.g. `bundle.macOS.files` `"PlugIns/PassAuthAutoFill.appex"`), sign the app with the host entitlements + embedded profile (`bundle.macOS.entitlements`, `files` `"embedded.provisionprofile"`), and sign inside-out: the appex first (build.sh), then the app without `--deep` (`codesign --force --timestamp --options runtime --sign <id> PassAuth.app`; `--deep` would re-sign the appex without its entitlements and profile), then notarize and staple. Check with `codesign --verify --deep --strict PassAuth.app` and `codesign -d --entitlements - PassAuth.app/Contents/PlugIns/PassAuthAutoFill.appex`.
- AutoFill identities (shared with iOS, `IdentityMapper`): only `.domain` identities are registered. The system offers an identity on every subdomain of its base domain and shows one row per user name there, whatever the identity type (seen on macOS 2026-11-21: a host-registered Cloudy login offered first on radarr.… and portainer.…; picking a row tells the extension only the record, not the page). So websites with Host/Exact/Starts with, and logins sharing a user name under one base domain (`crowdedDomains`), are left out; on those sites the system shows "PassAuth…", whose list picks by the page (`AutofillMatcher`). Base domain logins keep the one-tap row. Setting `suggestAllLogins` (default off; iOS Settings → AutoFill "Suggest every login above the keyboard", macOS AutoFill sheet → Filling): on, Host/Exact/Starts with websites are registered as `.URL` identities of their address and same-name logins by their full host (the system may then put another subdomain's login first). With it on, password rows are labelled "user · item name" (display only; a pick fills by record id), so same-name logins aren't merged into one row. Setting `fillSingleMatch` (default on; "Fill at once when one login matches"): "PassAuth…" on a page where exactly one login with a password matches the most specific page (`AutofillMatcher.onlyMatch`; not passkey/code requests, not warned copies) fills it without showing the list. Research 2026-11-22: no other manager fixes subdomain ranking in the system row (Apple documents `rank` only for identical service identifiers); 1Password/Bitwarden send users to their own list or browser extension.
- Default URI match detection is shared with the desktop app (Settings → This app → "AutoFill on this Mac"): Keychain item `io.passauth.desktop.autofill-settings` (account `settings`) in the AutoFill group, JSON `{defaultMatch, changedAt}` (unix ms). The desktop writes it on change (`autofill_settings_get`/`_set`, desktop/src-tauri/src/autofill_settings.rs); the extension reconciles on every request (`desktop/macos/AutoFill/DesktopSettings.swift` `reconcile`): a newer item is adopted through `updateSettings` (identities re-registered), a missing, unreadable or older item gets the extension's own value written back, so the desktop shows what AutoFill uses. Known: adopted while locked, Safari keeps the old identities until the next unlock refreshes them.

## Deployment
- One container image `ghcr.io/fahed-alhammadi/passauth` (server + web vault), tagged `X.Y.Z` and `latest`, with multi-arch builds (amd64, arm64). Distroless runtime, no shell. Users edit only `docker-compose.yml` (values written directly) and `.env` (PUID/PGID, admin user name and password, optional infrastructure: DOMAIN, DATABASE_URL, IP_HEADER, TRUSTED_PROXIES); everything else is set on the admin page.
- User switch (`crates/server/src/privileges.rs`, before the tokio runtime starts any thread): started as root, `serve` creates the SQLite folder and icon cache (absolute paths below `/` only) and, if a folder isn't already owned by `PUID:PGID` (default 1000:1000; `PUID=0` is refused), `lchown`s its contents children-first without following links; then `setgroups([PGID])`, `setgid`, `setuid`, checks it can't become root again and that `CapPrm`/`CapEff`/`CapAmb` are 0. `healthcheck` and `hash-admin-token` switch too. Compose grants only `CHOWN`, `SETUID`, `SETGID` (+ `no-new-privileges`, read-only root, `/tmp` tmpfs); missing ones stop the server with a message naming `cap_add`. Started as non-root (old compose files with `user:`), it changes nothing. CI: `scripts/docker-smoke.sh` (runs as PUID:PGID with a fresh volume, admin sign-in over HTTP, old compose style, clear error without capabilities) and `docker compose config` with `.env.example`.
- `docker compose up` gives the server, with SQLite in `/data` by default and PostgreSQL optional via `DATABASE_URL`.
- `passauth-server backup <file>`: SQLite `VACUUM INTO` a new 0600 file, consistent while the server runs (never overwrites a file, refuses a missing database); PostgreSQL: use `pg_dump` (the command says so and exits 1). The copy leaves out the sync generation; after restoring any other kind of backup, run `passauth-server reset-sync` before starting, so apps fetch everything once instead of trusting their cursors. `backup X` also writes `X.attachments/` (hard links where possible; only `ready` rows whose file was copied are kept). Attachments live in `ATTACHMENTS_DIR`, else `attachments/` beside the SQLite file, else `/data/attachments` with PostgreSQL when `/data` exists (otherwise attachments are off with a startup warning); the startup ownership fix covers that folder. A database whose schema is newer than the binary refuses to start.
- Runs as one instance per database: sign-ins in progress, rate limits (address keys capped at 100k; per-target counters and per-email slot tables are never dropped for room), live-sync sockets, admin sessions and the applied admin settings live in the server process.
- Free-disk reserve (`crates/server/src/disk.rs`, admin setting `min_free_disk_mb`, default 1024, 0 = off): `statvfs` (space available to the server's user) on the SQLite folder and the attachments folder, cached 1 s; folders it can't measure are skipped. Item create/update/import, Send create (and Send updates that grow), attachment create (declared size) and upload (counting uploads in progress, under the upload-slot lock) get 507 `insufficient_storage` when they would leave less free. Reading, sync, sign-in, trash, delete and purge are never refused, so space can be freed safely. The admin overview shows `disk_free_bytes` (null when nothing can be measured).
- The sweeper runs each step under `catch_unwind`, so a failure or panic in one doesn't stop the others.
- Vault ids are never reused (`vault_ids`, migration 12, filled from every id still named anywhere): creating a used id, also of a deleted vault or one deleted with its account, gives 409 `vault_exists`, so a re-created id never sees the old vault's events, versions or files. Ids are unique server-wide, so a 409 tells someone who already knows a random UUID that it exists. Server-keyed hashes are SHA-256(secret ‖ label ‖ message) without a separator: safe as used (no label is a prefix of another); HMAC with length-prefixed labels would be the cleaner form.
- Config via env or `*_FILE` secrets. See `.env.example`.

## CI/CD (GitHub Actions)
- CI = one workflow per product holding both its checks and its release (`.github/workflows/`): `core.yml` (fmt, clippy, tests on SQLite + PostgreSQL, `cargo audit` of the root lockfile, also weekly; no release), `server.yml` (amd64 + arm64 image, not pushed; release = GHCR publish), `extension.yml` (web vault build + unit tests, golden-vector check, extension Biome lint + unit tests + per-context type check and build, Firefox build + `web-ext lint`; release = reproducible zips + Chrome Web Store + addons.mozilla.org), `desktop.yml` (desktop app check, `cargo audit` of `desktop/src-tauri`, macOS AutoFill extension unit tests; release = installers + GitHub release), `android.yml` (debug build + unit tests; release = Play bundle), `ios.yml` (simulator build; release = TestFlight archive). Check jobs run on pushes to main and PRs only when the product's own files or the code it is built from change (`paths:`); a change to `crates/` or `Cargo.*` runs all of them, a change to one app runs only that app's. Release jobs run only on that product's tag (GitHub ignores `paths` for tag pushes) or "Run workflow", never on branch pushes or PRs. Concurrency group = workflow + ref: check runs on a branch replace each other, tag runs are never cancelled.
- Versions: five parts, each with its own version and tag; bump one with `scripts/release.sh <part> X.Y.Z` (commits only that part's version files and tags it), push main, wait for CI, push the tag. A shared core change ships in whichever parts you release.
  - server + web vault: root `Cargo.toml` `[workspace.package] version` (+ `web/package.json`) → tag `server-X.Y.Z` → `server.yml`: multi-arch image to GHCR as `X.Y.Z` + `latest` (pre-releases `X.Y.Z-rc.N` don't move `latest`), no GitHub release: `scripts/release.sh server` adds the notes from `scripts/release-notes.sh` (commits since the previous `server-` tag touching server paths) to `CHANGELOG.md` in the release commit. GitHub releases are used only by the desktop app.
  - extension: `extension/package.json` + `public/manifest.json` → `extension-X.Y.Z` → `extension.yml`: builds `passauth-extension-X.Y.Z.zip` + `passauth-extension-firefox-X.Y.Z.zip` + its `-source.zip` (kept on the run as artifacts), submits to the Chrome Web Store (needs a higher manifest version per upload) and, when repo variable `AMO_UPLOAD_ENABLED=true`, to addons.mozilla.org (`web-ext sign --channel listed --approval-timeout 0`, secrets `AMO_JWT_ISSUER`/`AMO_JWT_SECRET`; pre-releases skipped). Manual run = store connection checks only (`cws-publish.sh --check`, `scripts/amo-check.mjs`). The extension and apps get just a tag.
  - iOS: `ios/VERSION` → `ios-X.Y.Z` → `ios.yml` (TestFlight). Android: `android/VERSION` → `android-X.Y.Z` → `android.yml` (Play track from repo variable `PLAY_TRACK`: `internal` when unset, `alpha` = closed testing; manual runs can pick one). Build number / versionCode = major·1 000 000 + minor·1 000 + patch, so every store upload needs a higher version. Old `vX.Y.Z` tags (≤ 0.4.3) released all parts together; `<part>-vX.Y.Z` tags were used until 2026-11-05.
  - desktop: `desktop/package.json` + `desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.toml` (+ `Cargo.lock`) → tag `vX.Y.Z` → `desktop.yml`: macOS universal (signed + notarized when the Apple secrets exist), Windows x64 NSIS, Linux AppImage/deb/rpm, built with `tauri-apps/tauri-action` into a GitHub release marked Latest with `latest.json` for the updater (pre-releases `X.Y.Z-beta.N`: prerelease, no `latest.json`), plus fixed-name copies (`PassAuth-macOS.dmg`, `PassAuth-Windows-x64-setup.exe`, `PassAuth-Linux-x86_64.AppImage`, `-amd64.deb`, `-x86_64.rpm`) for stable download links. Manual runs build only. CI job `desktop` typechecks, builds the frontend and runs clippy on Ubuntu.
- Stage 2: `ios.yml` (xcodebuild archive + upload to App Store Connect / TestFlight) and `android.yml` (signed bundle to the Play Console internal track).
